# @windmorph/react — Architecture

Status: **Tier 3 complete** · Last updated: 2026-10-15 (web density; reduced motion removes movement in every component, §6; accessibility fixes: focus, keyboard, touch targets; 2026-10-14: component tokens, overlays' `className` on the outermost element, parts loaded on demand; rev. 44, 2026-10-13: type in rem, layout in px)

A production-ready React component library implementing the **latest Material Design 3 Expressive** specification, consumed by many React and Next.js projects. This is the free, MIT-licensed core: M3 Expressive components only.

```tsx
import { Button, Dialog, DialogTrigger, ThemeProvider } from '@windmorph/react';
```

---

Components outside M3 (a code block, an avatar, a skeleton, rich text, …) live in **Windmorph Pro** (`@windmorph/react-pro`, a separate package and repo), never improvised in an app. Pro builds only on the core's public entries, and the core never imports Pro; see §3 | 23  | Consumer docs site   | A **Next.js App Router static-export app at `apps/site`** (MDX guides, generated props tables, live examples), built **only with Windmorph: the core and Windmorph Pro** (`@windmorph/react`, `/next`, `@windmorph/react-pro` and its `/rich-text` entry; enforced by `no-restricted-imports`). Pro's pages are documented publicly in full (API, props tables, guides and live examples) and marked with a "Pro" chip (the catalog's `pro` flag); the site installs Pro from its private repo with a read-only token. It is the library's first real consumer and doubles as proof of Next.js support; Storybook (`apps/docs`) stays the workbench, not the consumer docs. **Shell:** a top app bar (medium+) or an app bar with search (compact) holding the page search (a `SearchBar` listing guides and components that match by title, summary or catalog `keywords`, built from the registry on the server; a component's code names and its distinctive prop names, those on at most three pages, open its API tab, a prop name at that prop's anchor, 2026-10-14; a whole-title match ranks first, then a title's start, 2026-10-17) and a "Display settings" side sheet (theme, mode, contrast, motion and a right-to-left switch, whose choice a pre-paint script applies on every page). **Props lists** (2026-10-14): every props table on the site is one responsive list (`components/props-list.tsx`, built from core components, no longer Pro's `PropsTable`). Each component has a real `h3` (its name in monospace and its prop count), which the "On this page" outline lists under Props. Its props are grouped by name: Props (required first), Events (`on…`), Text (`labels`, the ARIA naming props) and Styling (`className`, `classNames`, `style`, folded), keeping source order, then **Inherited from React Aria** (folded, 2026-10-17): the props a component passes through from `react-aria`, `react-stately` or `@react-types/*` (`onPress*`, `name`, `validate`, selection keys…), minus deprecated, `UNSAFE_*`, `is*` and ARIA plumbing props (`INHERITED_DENYLIST` in `scripts/generate-props.ts`). Other DOM attributes aren't rows: one line under the table says the component also accepts every `<button>` (or its root element's) attribute, as other libraries' API pages say it. The generator reads union props types with the TypeScript checker: a prop is required only when every form requires it (a naming pair notes "One of `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required"), takes one form's description rather than all of them joined, prefers its `@default` tag and quotes a bare string default. Every prop has the same two parts: a first line with its name (monospace, the strongest text), its type as quiet monospace text reading like TypeScript (an enum's members joined by ` | `, wrapping between them; more than 16 fold behind "Show all N"), a "Required" label and `= default`, all left-aligned and wrapping like text; then its description at the list's full width with its JSDoc Markdown rendered (code spans, `{@link}`references linked to their pages, links, emphasis;`lib/inline-markdown.ts`). In a list narrower than 42rem the type and default take their own line under the name, the same for every row. Only the groups after the main one have subheads. An anchor (`#prop-<Component>-<name>`) links to each prop and copies its link. An object prop's keys nest under it behind a disclosure naming how many there are; a link to a key opens what folds it. Pages with more than 12 props get a "Filter props" field above them (a core `TextField`) that filters names and descriptions across all their components, opens the folds holding a match, highlights the matched words (the CSS Custom Highlight API, so it's visual only and leaves the text alone), announces the count and shows an empty state. **What's new** (`/whats-new`, the last guide, 2026-10-13) lists the releases in `content/releases.tsx`, newest first; each new version adds an entry at the top. **Component pages** (2026-10-15): an import line under the title (copyable; Pro pages note Pro and link its guide), an Accessibility tab with the same six sections on every page (Keyboard, Screen reader, Focus, Touch target, Motion, What you need to do; data in `content/components/accessibility/`, a section that doesn't apply says why), and Do and Don't cards on the ten most-used components' Guidelines (inert, `aria-hidden` live examples; the label and sentence carry the meaning). The **Windmorph Pro** guide (`/pro`) lists Pro's components from the catalog and links the components index filtered by URL (`?filter=pro`); it also shows Pro's docs components (`CodeBlock`, `PropsTable`, with a live sample) and answers common questions in Pro's `Accordion` (2026-10-16). The footer names Windmorph with links to the repository, npm, the licence and the Pro guide. The 404 page (`app/not-found.tsx`, 2026-10-17) follows GOV.UK's "Page not found" pattern in the site's shell: its own title, what to check, search and links to Home, Components and Getting started. Example code is shown as an app would write it (icons from `./icons`, sample photos from the reader's own data). The phone drawer is M3's 360px wide with 48px rows; a Pro row's tag is text inside its link (a link can't hold another), and "Pro components" links the filtered index under All components (2026-10-17). See §13 |

## 2. Stack

| Concern       | Choice                                                              | Notes                                                                                                                     |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Framework     | React 19 + TypeScript strict                                        |                                                                                                                           |
| Accessibility | **React Aria** (`react-aria` + `react-stately`)                     | Keyboard, focus, ARIA, RTL, i18n. Exposes pressed/hovered/focus-visible states needed for M3 state layers. Replaces Radix |
| Styling       | **Tailwind CSS v4**                                                 | `@theme`, `@theme inline`, `@custom-variant`, `@utility`. No `tailwind.config.js`                                         |
| Variants      | **tailwind-variants v3** (slots)                                    | Replaces CVA — slots style multi-part components in one call                                                              |
| Class merge   | `cn()` = tailwind-merge (extended) + clsx                           | `packages/react/src/utils/cn.ts`; its token lists are generated from the token source (§4)                                |
| Motion        | **Motion** (`motion/react`)                                         | Spring choreography, enter/exit, layout, morph. `LazyMotion` + `m`. Simple state transitions use CSS `linear()` springs   |
| Colour        | `@material/material-color-utilities` 0.4.0 with **`ColorSpec2025`** | Vendored into the build (decision #21)                                                                                    |
| Dates         | `@internationalized/date`                                           | A dependency (React Aria's own date library); picker values are its `CalendarDate` / `Time`                               |
| Shapes        | Port of Android `androidx.graphics.shapes`                          | Feature-matched morph across M3's 35 shapes. Replaces Flubber. Vendored with Apache-2.0 NOTICE                            |
| Fonts         | Roboto Flex (variable: wght, wdth, opsz, GRAD)                      | Consumer-loaded                                                                                                           |
| Repo tooling  | pnpm workspaces + Turborepo                                         | Cached builds/tests across packages and apps                                                                              |

## 3. Composition architecture (DRY)

Four layers. **Dependencies only point downward**, enforced by `eslint-plugin-boundaries` in `eslint.config.js`. In source, the layers are `src/tokens` → `src/utils` → `src/theme` / `src/motion` → `src/primitives` → `src/components` → `src/composites`. Composites may not import `src/primitives`. `src/shapes` (the androidx.graphics.shapes port) is pure geometry: it imports nothing, and primitives and components may import it.

**Windmorph Pro (decision #22):** components outside the M3 spec are not in this package, so `src/components` stays M3 only. They live in Windmorph Pro (`@windmorph/react-pro`), which lists `@windmorph/react` as a peer dependency and imports only its public entries (`@windmorph/react`, `/primitives`, `/next`). The core never imports Pro: `no-restricted-imports` forbids `@windmorph/react-pro` in `packages/react`. What Pro needs to build its components (the token value helpers, `defineComponentTokens`, `componentTokensCss`, `OptionPopover`, `useTriggerProps`, `splitRootProps` with its `RootDOMProps` and `WrapperRootDOMProps` types and `pickWidgetAria` (2026-10-17), `splitDataAttributes`, `ThemeScopeContext`, `generateSchemeColors`, `materialShapeMask` and the precomputed shape outlines, `useKeepPressedInPlace`) is public API, documented on the docs site's Primitives page.

**Every new component is also added to the docs site** (2026-10-08): a catalog entry in `apps/site/content/components/catalog.ts` puts it in the side nav under the right category, in the components listing and in the component count (both computed from the catalog), and in the search index, with `keywords` for the words people search for that aren't in its title or summary; plus its page body, examples and playground. **Docs completeness is tested** (2026-10-13): the playground has a control for every public prop of every component it renders, its own and the siblings its `componentSwitch` shows (Progress: linear or circular), or the prop is in the descriptor's `notControlled` allowlist with a reason, and each other component the page documents is in its `notRendered` list with a reason (2026-10-14); every snippet a playground can copy writes a controlled prop with its handler (a `useState` pair, the controlled form docs sites show, React Aria's among them; a trigger-wrapped overlay leaves `open` to its trigger), checked by parsing every snippet (`components/playground/playground-snippets.test.ts`, 2026-10-17); the playground's controls sit in sections and every number control has a slider range, with each control linking to its prop's API entry (`components/playground/descriptors.test.ts`, 2026-10-15); every public prop of the core has a JSDoc description, every optional string-literal prop its `@default`, and every `labels` and `classNames` key a description (the labels with their English default), which the props tables list key by key (`apps/site/scripts/props-docs.test.ts`, 2026-10-14). The same test covers Pro's installed declarations (2026-10-16), with the gaps the pinned Pro build still has listed by name, so a new gap fails and a fixed one must leave the list; every Pro token set has its table on a Pro page (`token-coverage.test.ts`); no component hard-codes a word it shows or announces (`packages/react/src/built-in-strings.test.ts`: it comes from `labels` or a prop, 2026-10-14); every component and hook the core exports has a generated table (hooks are documented as functions: parameters and return value, an object's fields key by key without the members it inherits from React or another library, as the props tables leave out inherited props; 2026-10-15, `useTriggerProps` had listed `DOMAttributes`' 180 handlers twice, which made the Primitives API page four times heavier than any other and timed out its smoke test), and the component's spec data (`apps/site/content/components/spec-data/`) maps each M3 Expressive spec variant and configuration it covers to a live example. The same data lists its deliberate differences from the spec, its extensions beyond it and its Compose counterparts, and builds the API tab's spec status, the `/spec-status` summary and the `/from-compose` guide.

1. **Tokens** — CSS variables for colour, shape, type, motion, elevation, state opacity, z-index.
2. **Primitives** (internal; also exported from `@windmorph/react/primitives`)
   - `useM3Interaction` — wraps React Aria `usePress` / `useHover` / `useFocusRing` / drag → emits `data-pressed`, `data-hovered`, `data-focus-visible`, `data-dragged`, `data-disabled`, `data-selected`. Pass `isPressed` from another React Aria hook (e.g. `useButton`) to skip its own press handling. It also sets the ripple origin on pointer-down (primary button) or Enter/Space. The **ripple follows Compose's `RippleAnimation`**: it fades in over 75ms; its radius grows from 30% of the longer side to half the diagonal + 10px over 225ms (fast-out-slow-in) while its centre moves to the middle; it fades out over 150ms. `data-rippling` (from `useRippleHold`, also used by Checkbox, Radio and Switch) stays on from press start until release, but for at least 225ms, so a quick tap still grows to the edges before fading. Earlier the growth was tied to `data-pressed` and froze wherever it was on a short press. Every press starts a **fresh** ripple, as in Compose, even while the last one is still growing or fading: `restartRipple` pins the ripple's animated properties to the new press's start values with their transitions off, flushes, and releases them. Only the ripple's transitions are switched off (2026-10-13): the component's own (`--m3-transition-property`) keep running, since switching every transition off cancelled a running corner morph and made the corners jump on a quick second press. Otherwise the delayed reset of the idle ripple made a press grow from the previous press's position.
   - `ButtonBase` — unstyled button behaviour shared by every button-like component: `<button>`, link (`href`, via `useLink`) or toggle (`toggle`, via `useToggleButton`), with `useM3Interaction` applied. `children` may be a function of `{ isSelected }`. Button, IconButton, the FABs and the chips render it and only add styling. It also takes `dragged` (2026-10-13), passed to `useM3Interaction` as `isDragged`, so `data-dragged` comes from the same source as the other states; a consumer's own `data-dragged` attribute counts as dragged rather than being overwritten. And `pending` (2026-10-17, `ButtonBasePendingProps`, for `Button` and `IconButton`): presses blocked, focus kept, `aria-disabled` and `data-pending` (§9, Expressive Button family).
   - `TouchTarget` — 48×48px hit area for small controls, placed in the inner wrapper.
   - State layer + ripple as **background-layer utilities** on the root (no child elements — see §10): `state-layer`, plus `focus-ring` / `focus-ring-inset` (outline). `Surface` (`container` role + `elevation` 0–5 + `shape`) covers elevation; there is no separate `Elevation` component.
   - `Overlay`: portal to `body` that **re-applies the theme attributes and text direction (`dir`) of where it was rendered**, so overlays opened inside a `ThemeScope` or an RTL region keep them. Its `exiting` prop is passed to React Aria as `isExiting`, so focus containment is released while animating out. `topLayer` marks the overlay as React Aria's top layer (`data-react-aria-top-layer`, as its toasts use): a portalled overlay sits outside any open modal, whose `ariaHideOutside` would otherwise mark it `aria-hidden`; a top-layer one stays visible to assistive tech, focus may reach it and a press on it is not a press outside the modal. Tooltips and rich tooltips use it, so they work inside dialogs, sheets and viewers. **Direction before first style** (2026-10-13): on mount the wrapper (`data-overlay-scope`, now the overlay's `useId`) gets its `dir` from the marker's ref callback, which runs before the content's layout effects, read with `:dir(rtl)` (no style recalculation) where `CSS.supports` says so. The content's own layout effects can force styles (React Aria's scroll lock reads `clientWidth`), and the old layout effect ran after them, so `@starting-style` transitions began in the wrong direction; the layout effect still keeps `dir` current afterwards.
   - `TriggerContext`: an overlay trigger (DialogTrigger, MenuTrigger, SheetTrigger, the tooltip triggers) passes its press handler, ARIA attributes and ref to whichever button-like child opens it. `ButtonBase` merges them in, so no cloning or private React Aria APIs are needed. Triggers nest: each merges its props into an enclosing trigger's (`useTriggerProps`, React Aria `mergeProps`: handlers chained, refs merged), so `<TooltipTrigger><MenuTrigger><IconButton/>…` gives a menu button its tooltip. Overlays, and a FAB menu's items, reset it to `null`.
   - `DomDirectionLocale`: React Aria takes keyboard direction from its locale, not the DOM `dir`. This render-prop component reads an element's computed direction and, when it differs from the locale's, provides **the same locale with only its script changed** (`localeWithDirection`: `en-US` → `en-Arab-US`, `ar` → `ar-Latn`), which React Aria's `isRTL` reads, so dates and numbers keep the app's language. Toolbars, lists, sliders and the pickers use it, and Tabs uses `localeWithDirection`. (Before rev. 35 it switched to `ar` / `en-US`, which also changed formatting; found when the date picker printed Arabic month names in an RTL region.)
   - `usePresence(isOpen, property = 'opacity')`: keeps an overlay mounted until its exit transition of `property` ends (fallback 800ms); `property` may be a list, ended by the first of them to finish (2026-10-15: sheets end on `translate`, or on `opacity` under reduced motion). The full-screen search view ends on `clip-path`. Dismissal, focus containment and scroll lock come from the React Aria overlay hooks (`useModalOverlay`, `usePopover`, …) used by each component.
   - `useKeepPressedInPlace(selector, version, settling)` (2026-10-17, public for Pro's Accordion): keeps the element a press landed on still on screen while content above it changes size because of the press, by scrolling its scroller (the nearest scrolling ancestor, or the page) by however far it moved, once before the change paints and then on every frame while `settling`. It notes the press on pointer down, key down or a virtual click (`capture`, with `release` on the matching up events), acts when `version` changes, and lets go on a wheel, touch, pointer or key input; a press after which the scroller moved, or that changed nothing, is dropped. Pair it with `overflow-anchor: none` on the folding parts. The List's tree grid uses it (§9 Lists).
   - `useM3Morph` (built): `useM3Morph(shapes, progress, { size, startAngle })` returns a Motion value of SVG path data for an `m.path`'s `d`. `shapes` are `MaterialShapes` names or any normalized `RoundedPolygon`; progress `0…n-1` walks the sequence, and values outside it extrapolate the first or last morph, so springs can overshoot. Morph matching is cached per shape pair. Reduced motion is the caller's job (snap `progress`). `morphPathAt` is the same thing as a plain function.
   - **Shape library** (`src/shapes`, exported from `@windmorph/react/primitives`): a TypeScript port of `androidx.graphics.shapes` (`RoundedPolygon`, `CornerRounding`, `Morph`, `circle` / `rectangle` / `star` / `pill` / `pillStar`) and Compose's 35 `MaterialShapes`, from androidx `120345129e`. The androidx commonTest suites are ported too. Deviations: 64-bit numbers (so the tests androidx skips on JS for float rounding pass here); `calculateBounds` seeds with ±Infinity, fixing shapes in negative space; no SVG parser, serializer or validation. `polygonToPath` / `morphToPath` port Compose's internal `ShapeUtil.kt` but emit SVG path data (4 decimals) and rotate about the pivot, since SVG has no re-centering step. Coordinates are snapped to 9 decimals before that rounding (2026-10-13): Node and browsers can differ in the last bits of `Math.sin` / `Math.cos`, which rounded a Triangle coordinate (0.45625) differently on each side and broke a server-rendered `Avatar`'s hydration. **Note:** morphs use each polygon's unsplit feature cubics, so mid-morph control points can sit up to ~1% outside the unit square (as upstream); render with `overflow="visible"` or padding.
   - **Precomputed static outlines** (2026-10-17, plan `avatar-size.md`): a shape that never moves (Pro's Avatar, ShapedIcon and EmptyState) needs only its outline, but `materialShapeMask(name)` built the `RoundedPolygon` at runtime, which put the whole engine (about 13 kB gzipped counted per module, 6.6 kB minified and tree-shaken) into every app that drew one, a round avatar included. `pnpm generate` now writes `src/shapes/generated/material-shape-paths.ts`: the names tuple (`materialShapeNames`, `MaterialShapeName`, moved here so listing the names doesn't load the 35 builders) and one `MaterialShapePath` constant per shape (`circleShape` … `heartShape`: `{ name, path }`, Compose's names in camelCase plus `Shape`, since `circle`, `pill` and `star` are polygon builders), the normalised outline rounded to 3 decimals (at most 0.0005 of the size off: 0.12px at 240px; 2 decimals erred up to 1.2px) with leading zeros dropped and `-` as a separator. `material-shape-path-record.ts` holds `materialShapePaths` (all 35 by name, about 10.5 kB gzipped) for galleries and pickers. Flat constants rather than a namespace object, since a bundler keeps only those imported (`sideEffects: ["*.css"]`); a component's `shape` takes the value, not a name, because resolving a name needs the whole table. `MaterialShapePath` is structural, so an app's own outline in the unit square works too. `materialShapeMask(shape: MaterialShapePath)` wraps the path in the same 1 × 1 SVG `data:` URI (cached per value in a `WeakMap`) and imports no engine module. The files are checked in; `scripts/material-shape-paths.test.ts` fails when they're stale, when the names differ from the builders' keys, or when a coordinate is more than 0.0005 from the engine's. `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails the build if `shapes/mask.js` or the generated modules import `material-shapes.js` or `rounded-polygon.js`, and `pnpm size` runs shape probes (`scripts/lib/shape-probes.ts`: tiny entries bundled against `dist/primitives.js` with tsdown, tree-shaken and minified, dependencies left out): the mask helper alone 257 B (budget 300 B), Cookie12Sided +371 B over it (600 B), SoftBoom, the largest, +2281 B (2560 B), the record 10.7 kB (11 kB), and none may bundle the engine. The outlines aren't mirrored in RTL (Compose's `RoundedPolygon.toShape()` ignores its `layoutDirection`), and server and client emit the same constant, so the `Math.sin` / `Math.cos` hydration risk is gone for static shapes. The engine stays for what morphs (`LoadingIndicator`, the wavy progress indicator, `useM3Morph`). Delivery stays a CSS `mask-image` `data:` URI with `mask-size: 100% 100%`: `clip-path: path()` doesn't scale with the box, `shape()` is Baseline only since 2026, and an inline `<clipPath>` needs a unique id per element. Chrome and Edge 113–119 read only `-webkit-mask-*`: Tailwind's optimised (minified) output adds the prefixes to mask utilities, but an inline `style={{ maskImage }}` gets none, so a component sets the mask through a class reading a variable, or sets `WebkitMaskImage` too.
   - `useM3Spring` (resolves the active motion scheme → Motion spring config)
   - **Parts loaded on demand** (internal, `utils/load-on-demand.tsx`, 2026-10-14): `onDemand` wraps a dynamic `import()` in a component that renders nothing until its module arrives and never suspends (unlike `React.lazy`, whose `Suspense` reveal React can hold back by up to 300ms). `usePreloadWhenIdle` preloads in the browser's first idle period after mount (one shared `requestIdleCallback`, a 1s timeout without it, never when the user asks to save data), and `usePreloadOnIntent` adds `pointerover` / `focusin` on an element. Built-in tooltips, `Menu`'s and `Select`'s bottom sheet, `ButtonGroup`'s overflow menu, the loading indicator in option lists and `PullToRefresh`, and `DateField`'s compact-window picker load this way (§9).
   - **Announcer** (internal, `utils/announce.ts`): `announcePolite(message)` writes to one visually hidden `aria-live="polite"` region on `document.body`, created on first use and written to only `LIVE_REGION_DELAY` (100ms) after it mounts, since screen readers skip content that arrives with its region; `prepareAnnouncer()` creates it ahead of a first message (a button that takes `pending`). A `Snackbar` without a host and a focused button turning pending announce through it.
   - `Field` parts: `FieldLabel`, `SupportingText`, `ErrorText`, `CharacterCounter`
3. **Components** — Button, IconButton, FAB, Card, TextField, Checkbox, Radio, Switch… assembled from primitives.
4. **Composites** — built **only from public components** (dogfooding the public API):
   - Button group (standard and connected) = Buttons / IconButtons + group context, the only composite so far. Select and Autocomplete, planned here as composites, are components (`src/components/select`): they share Text field's field markup and Menu's `menuStyles` (§9).

### Rules

- **Flat named exports** for multi-part components: `Dialog`, `DialogTrigger`, `DialogTitle`, `DialogContent`, `DialogActions`. No dot-notation (`Dialog.Title`) — it breaks in Next.js Server Components.
- **Two API levels**: a convenient "batteries-included" form (`<TextField label="Email" />`) built from exported parts for full control. One implementation, not two. (TextField exports only the batteries-included form in v1, §9.)
- **Context cascade**: parents pass shared props to children (`ButtonGroup size="sm"` → Buttons). Child's explicit prop wins.
- **Shared style fragments**: state-layer, focus-ring, disabled styles defined once and reused; never copy class strings.
- Because composites use public components, a theme or token override on Button also applies to the Buttons inside a ButtonGroup.

## 4. Tokens — single source of truth

One token source file (TypeScript) generates **all** of:

- the CSS variables in `styles.css` (`@theme` / `@theme inline`)
- the token lists used by `cn()`'s tailwind-merge config
- the Motion spring configs used by `useM3Spring`

so CSS, class merging and JS motion can never drift apart.

In practice the source is `packages/react/src/tokens/*.ts`. `pnpm generate` writes `src/styles/generated/{tokens,themes,theme,components}.css`; those files are gitignored and Turborepo regenerates them before build, typecheck, lint and test. `cn()` and `useM3Spring` import the token objects directly.

Tailwind utility names:

| Token        | Utilities                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Colour roles | `bg-primary`, `text-on-surface`, `border-outline-variant`, …                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Type scale   | `text-<role>` sets size, line height, tracking and weight (`text-body-large`, `text-label-large-emphasized`), in rem so text follows the browser's font size (see Typography below); typeface via `font-brand` / `font-plain` |
| Corners      | `rounded-corner-<name>` (`rounded-corner-full`, `rounded-corner-medium`, …)                                                                                                                                                   |
| Elevation    | `shadow-elevation-0` … `shadow-elevation-5`                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Springs      | `ease-m3-<family>-<speed>` and `duration-m3-<family>-<speed>`; legacy curves are `ease-m3-emphasized`, …                                                                                                                      |
| Variants     | `medium:` / `expanded:` / `large:` / `xlarge:`; `dark:` follows `data-mode` (including `system`); `motion-standard:`                                                                                                          |

### Component tokens (`--md-comp-*`, 2026-10-14)

M3's component tokens as CSS variables an app sets to restyle every instance of a component, globally or within a scope, from CSS only. Plan: `docs/plans/component-tokens.md`. Card was the first component with them; 30 `*-tokens.ts` files now cover the core (each component's are listed in §9).

- **Names** are M3's `md.comp.*` token names with the dots as dashes: `md.comp.filled-card.container.color` is `--md-comp-filled-card-container-color`. Each is checked against m3.material.io's token tables, which `packages/react/scripts/m3-token-names.txt` records (active token sets only, read 2026-10-14). Where M3 Expressive replaced a component's token set (buttons are `md.comp.button.filled.*`, the deprecated set `md.comp.filled-button.*`), the current name is used. Non-M3 components (Windmorph Pro) keep `--wm-*`.
- **Source:** each component's `<component>-tokens.ts` defines a set with `defineComponentTokens`. A set's `scopes` give each value of a `data-*` attribute its own public prefix (`variant: { filled: { prefix: 'md-comp-filled-card', tokens } }`), and `fixed` adds private-only values. `pnpm generate` discovers every `src/**/*-tokens.ts` file (no hand-kept list), writes `generated/components.css` and the checked-in, sorted name list `component-tokens.txt`.
- **Two variables per token:** the public `--md-comp-<component>-<part>-<property>` is unset by default. On the root marked `data-tokens="md-comp-<set>"`, a zero-specificity `:where()` rule resolves the private `--_md-comp-<set>-<part>` to the public variable, falling back to the system token the component used before (for the root's `data-variant` / `data-size`). Classes read one private variable per part, written `bg-[var(--_x)]`, `text-[color:var(--_x)]`, `shadow-[shadow:var(--_x)]` (the type hints let `cn()` merge them with a consumer's class).
- **What's exposed:** resting container and content colours, shapes, elevation, outline colours and selected / unselected colours. State-layer colours per state, size-less shape aliases, sizes, spacing, outline widths, divider thickness, typography and motion aren't.
- **Defaults were unchanged by the token work:** the computed-style snapshot of every story (`apps/docs/e2e/computed-styles.spec.ts`, recorded before any token work) and the screenshot suites matched exactly; later deliberate changes re-record them. `component-tokens.spec.ts` sets every token on each set's `Tokens/<set name>` probe story and checks what it paints. `scripts/component-tokens.test.ts` checks reads, definitions, scope completeness, the name list and the M3 names.
- **Several sets on one root** (2026-10-16): a `data-tokens` an app or a library passes to a core component is appended, never replaced: the root carries the component's own set first, then the incoming ones, each once (`"md-comp-assist-chip wm-reaction-summary"`). `mergeDataTokens` (`utils/merge-data-tokens.ts`, public from `/primitives` beside `splitDataAttributes`) does it for every root that takes the app's props or `data-*` attributes; inner parts that take none (a checkbox's box, a radio's ring, a switch's thumb, a search view, an option list) keep their own set. Every generated rule, and every hand-written selector and e2e guard, matches with `~=` (or `^="md-comp-"`, which the set coming first keeps true), never an exact `=`.
- **Overlays:** a token on `:root` or `[data-theme]` reaches portalled overlays. `Overlay` also puts its enclosing ThemeScopes' classes on its `display: contents` wrapper, so a token set through a ThemeScope's `className` reaches them too, and the CSS variables (`--*` keys) of the scopes' `style` (2026-10-17; inner scope first; only variables travel, not layout styles), so `<ThemeScope style={{ '--md-comp-…': … }}>` reaches a `Select`'s portalled menu.

### Shape (corner) scale — spec tokens only

| Token                 | Value  |
| --------------------- | ------ |
| none                  | 0      |
| extra-small           | 4px    |
| small                 | 8px    |
| medium                | 12px   |
| large                 | 16px   |
| large-increased       | 20px   |
| extra-large           | 28px   |
| extra-large-increased | 32px   |
| extra-extra-large     | 48px   |
| full                  | 9999px |

No invented radii (e.g. the earlier `m3-expressive-1` / `m3-expressive-2` are removed). The 35 Expressive _shapes_ (cookie, clover, burst, …) are path shapes in the shape library, not radii.

### State layer opacities

hover 8% · focus 10% · pressed 10% · dragged 16% · disabled content 38% · disabled container 12%.

### Typography

Full baseline type scale + Expressive **emphasized** variants for every role (display/headline/title/body/label × large/medium/small).

**Type in rem, layout in px** (2026-10-13). Android sizes text in sp, which follows the system font scale, and everything else in dp, which doesn't. The web equivalent is rem for type and px for layout: the token source keeps Compose's values (px at a 16px root) and the generator writes each role's size, line height and tracking as rem (`px / 16`, so `body-large` is `1rem` / `1.5rem` / `0.03125rem`). At the browser's default 16px the result is pixel-identical to the px scale; when a reader raises the browser's font size, every role scales with it while containers, padding, corners, icons and touch targets keep their px sizes, as on Android. Browser zoom scales both, as before. Before this, the scale was in px, so a 32px default font size still gave a 24px dialog title: WCAG 1.4.4 (resize text) relied on zoom alone, and the M3 guidance that text should work at 200% was not met. Tracking is rem rather than em so it stays a fixed fraction of the root, matching Compose's sp tracking. **Touch targets are layout** (2026-10-16): `TouchTarget` and the M icon button's `::after` target are `48px` tall and `max(100%, 48px)` wide; they were Tailwind's `h-12` and `3rem`, so a 32px root made them 96px tall, and neighbours spaced for 48px targets in px (button groups, radio and chip rows) overlapped. Pro's Avatar had pinned 48px to work around it. `type-scale.spec.ts` checks that every target stays 48px at a 32px root.

Components write text through the type roles (`text-<role>` or the `typescale()` tokens), never as px sizes. Where a container had a fixed height that 200% text overflowed, it has a minimum height instead and grows, as Compose's `heightIn(min = …)` / `defaultMinSize` containers do: the small top app bar's row (64px), tabs (48 / 64px), the time picker's period selector (80 / 72 / 38px), the large badge (16px, Compose's `Badge` uses `defaultMinSize`; its 16px minimum and 34px maximum width are rem since 2026-10-17, so a digit stays a circle and text isn't cut sooner), and since 2026-10-13 the XS and S buttons (32 / 40px), the XS and S split button's halves (32 / 40px; they stretch to the taller one; S since 2026-10-15) and chips (32px), as Compose's `defaultMinSize` buttons and chips do. Those also take `h-fit`, so a stretching flex or grid parent doesn't make them taller; at the default 16px root nothing changes. Containers whose 200% text still fits keep their fixed height (M–XL buttons and split buttons, the search bar, dialog headers and action bars, the slider's value label). Sizes the spec fixes as geometry keep it: the time picker's 96 × 80px selectors and 256px dial, and the date picker's 48px day cells, so their larger digits fill the box. `apps/docs/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts` sets a 32px root and checks that the roles double, that layout stays in px and that the grown containers hold their text.

## 5. Theming

### Token layering

```css
/* Layer 1 — theme sets M3 system tokens */
[data-theme='ocean'][data-mode='dark'] {
  --md-sys-color-primary: …;
}

/* Layer 2 — Tailwind utilities reference them at runtime */
@theme inline {
  --color-primary: var(--md-sys-color-primary);
}
```

- Components use **only semantic M3 roles** (`bg-primary`, `text-on-surface`, `bg-surface-container-high`, …), never raw colours → every component is themed automatically.
- Switching theme = changing attributes on an ancestor. No re-render, SSR-safe.

### Attributes

- `data-theme` — colour theme
- `data-mode` — `light` | `dark` (or system)
- `data-contrast` — `standard` | `medium` | `high`
- `data-motion` — `expressive` | `standard`
- `data-density` — `0` | `-1` | `-2` | `-3` (web density, 2026-10-15; see Density below)

### Built-in themes (ColorSpec2025)

| Theme                | Seed      | Scheme     |
| -------------------- | --------- | ---------- |
| `baseline` (default) | `#6750A4` | Tonal spot |
| `ocean`              | `#0061A4` | Tonal spot |
| `forest`             | `#386A20` | Tonal spot |
| `sunset`             | `#A04100` | Tonal spot |
| `rose`               | `#9C4146` | Tonal spot |
| `slate`              | `#545F71` | Neutral    |

Each: light + dark × 3 contrast levels, generated at **build time** into static CSS. Mode follows system preference unless set.

### API

- `<ThemeProvider defaultTheme defaultMode defaultContrast defaultMotion defaultDensity themes>` + `useTheme()` → `{ theme, setTheme, mode, setMode, resolvedMode, contrast, setContrast, motion, setMotion, density, setDensity, themes }`.
  - Each dimension can also be controlled: `theme` + `onThemeChange`, and likewise for the others.
  - `storage`: `"cookie"` (default), `"local-storage"` or `"none"`. The key is `windmorph-theme` (`storageKey`) and the value is URL-encoded `theme=…&mode=…&contrast=…&motion=…&density=…`.
  - The stored value is read with `useSyncExternalStore`, so there's no hydration mismatch or extra render.
  - `applyToDocument={false}` stops it from writing to `<html>`.
  - `createTheme()` results passed in `themes` are injected as hoisted React 19 `<style precedence>` tags.
- `<ThemeScope theme mode contrast motion density>` — theme a subtree; nestable. It writes all five attributes, inheriting the ones you don't set, and adds `text-on-surface`, which `className` can override. `useThemeScope()` reads the effective state.
  - **Only scoped roles** (2026-10-13): Tailwind's `--color-*` theme variables resolve once at `:root`, so a component that reads `var(--color-*)` ignores a `ThemeScope` (the top app bar's scrolled fill and the slider's disabled handle had). Components read `var(--md-sys-color-*)` or a colour utility; `src/theme-scoped-colors.test.ts` fails on any `var(--color-` in the package's source outside tests. `apps/docs/e2e/theme-scope.spec.ts` (2026-10-14) renders every story (token stories aside) in a dark `forest` scope under a baseline light root and fails on any visible element, or its `::before` / `::after`, whose resolved colour is one only the root theme has.
- Attribute fallbacks: a missing `data-contrast` behaves as `standard`, a missing `data-mode` as `light`, and a root without `data-theme` gets `baseline`.
- **Theme selectors have zero specificity** (2026-10-14): every generated theme selector is wrapped in `:where()`, so a plain consumer rule (`:root { --md-sys-color-primary: … }`, `[data-theme="ocean"] { … }`) wins in every mode and contrast level. Before, the selectors combined theme, mode and contrast, and only a rule matching all three applied. Among the theme's own rules, source order decides: an element matches one theme and one contrast level, and each contrast level writes light, then dark, then system (in its media query), so the matching mode rule comes last. Defaults are unchanged.
- **Density** (2026-10-15; M3 text fields: "Avoid applying density by default … give people a way to choose a higher density, like selecting a denser layout or changing the theme"; menus: "On web only … Increasing density decreases the top and bottom padding"):
  - Levels `0 | -1 | -2 | -3` (`Density`, `DENSITIES` in `tokens/density.ts`), default 0. A fifth theme dimension: `ThemeProvider` `density` / `defaultDensity` / `onDensityChange`, `setDensity` on `useTheme()`, stored with the theme (`density=…` in the cookie; a value stored before density existed reads as 0), read by `ThemeScript` and `getThemeFromCookies`; `ThemeScope density`.
  - **CSS only:** `data-density` (on `<html>`, a `ThemeScope`, an overlay's wrapper, or a component root with a `density` prop) sets the system token `--md-sys-density` to the level for its subtree (`tokens.css`: `:root, [data-density="0"]` → 0, then one plain attribute rule per level, so the nearest attribute wins). Components read it in their sizes, `calc(56px + var(--md-sys-density, 0) * 4px)`, so nothing reads a context in JavaScript and density works in RSC and static pages. Not component tokens: density is one multiplier for every component, not a value per component, so a per-level token override in `components.css` would have repeated the same 4px rule in every set. A component joins by reading `--md-sys-density` in its sizes and spreading `densityAttribute(density)` (exported) for its own `density` prop; nothing in the foundation changes.
  - **Overlays:** `Overlay` writes the enclosing provider's or scope's density on its wrapper, or the component's own (`density` prop on `Overlay`), so a `Select` with `density={-2}` opens a dense list. As with the other dimensions, a bare `data-density` attribute that no `ThemeScope` wrote is not carried into portals, and a `ThemeScope` below it writes its own (inherited from the provider).
  - **Per level** each step takes 4px off a component's height by trimming the padding above and below its content: fields (TextField, text areas, Select, Autocomplete, DateField, the date pickers' input mode) 56 → 52 → 48 → 44px; the time picker's input mode 72 → 60px; menu items and option rows 44 → 40 → 36 → 32px (the existing 44px Expressive item, so density 0 is unchanged), with divider spacing 8 → 2px. Density 0 is pixel-identical to before (every element box of the affected stories compared, and the screenshot suites).
  - **Not dense:** the date picker's calendars (docked and modal) and the time picker's dial keep their 48px targets; M3 doesn't make pickers denser.
  - **Controls** (2026-10-15; M3 checkbox, radio button, switch, chips, icon buttons and tabs: "Avoid applying density by default … give people a way to choose a higher density … keep all the targets to change it at a minimum of 48x48"; chips: "a minimum 48dp target size, regardless of placement or density … the target can extend beyond the visible container"; lists: "can adjust margins, spacing, or density"). Per-level sizes after Material Components Web's density mixins (`@material/*` 14.0.0: `$density-config` per component, `density.$interval` 4px, each clamped at its `minimum`): checkbox and radio state layers 40 → 36 → 32 → 28px (MDC `$ripple-size` 40px, `$minimum-size` 28px; the 18px box and 20px ring stay); the switch's state layer 40 → 36 → 32 → 32px (MDC `state-layer-size` 48 → 28px, track untouched; ours stops at 32px so it still rings the pressed 28px thumb); chips 32 → 28 → 24 → 24px (MDC `$height` 32px, `$minimum-height` 24px, scales 0 to -2; avatar and remove button at most the height inside the border); icon buttons 4px off height and width at every size and width, never below the icon plus 4px (MDC `$size` 48px, `$minimum-height` 28px: XS 32 → 24px, S 40 → 28px, M 56 → 44px, L 96 → 84px, XL 136 → 124px); tabs 48 → 36px and 64 → 52px with icons above (MDC tab bar 48px, `$minimum-height` 30px, -4 supported; stacked 72 → 56px); list items 4px a step, 2px off the padding above and below (one line 56 → 44px, two 72 → 60px, three 88 → 76px; MDC one-line 48 → 24px, two-line 64 → 48px, three-line 88 → 56px) and section subheaders 48 → 36px. Density 0 is pixel-identical: every element box of all 287 Storybook stories compared before and after (only additions: a `::after` target on M icon buttons and a `0px` row gap on radio groups, neither changing a box), and no existing screenshot or computed-style baseline changed.
  - **Control targets:** checkboxes, radio buttons, switches, chips and icon buttons keep 48 × 48px targets past their containers (`TouchTarget`, or a `::after` of the same box on an M icon button, so density 0 adds no element). Where the library stacks them it adds room so a target never reaches a neighbour's visible part: a `RadioGroup` puts 2px more between rows a step (0 → 6px) and a `ChipSet` 2px more between rows (8 → 12px), its scrolling row as much above and below; rows still come 2px closer a step. A standard `ButtonGroup`'s XS and S gaps grow as density shrinks its icon buttons (2026-10-15): `max(18px, 48px − max(24px, 32px + 4px × density))` and `max(12px, 8px − 4px × density)`, so neighbouring centres stay 48px apart and the targets never overlap (before, the 18 / 12px gaps stayed and targets overlapped from -1 on); M and up keep 8px, which their sizes already clear. A group holding a narrow icon button spaces by the narrow width instead (2026-10-17; the spec's gaps assume the default width, and narrow XS / S buttons sat 44–46px apart): `max(18px, 48px − max(24px, 28px + 4px × density))` (XS), `max(12px, 48px − max(28px, 32px + 4px × density))` (S) and `max(8px, −4px × density)` (M), found with `:has()` on the icon buttons' `data-width`; the gap is the `--m3-button-group-gap` variable, so a consumer `gap-*` still replaces it. Tabs and list items are adjacent rows, so they follow the menu-row rule (full-row, non-overlapping targets above WCAG 2.5.8's 24px; the chevron's target is at most a one-line item tall). Checkboxes and switches that an app stacks itself are its layout: the Accessibility tabs say to leave 2px a step between them or use list items.
  - **What keeps its size:** icon buttons in a text field's icon slots (the 48px icon column that field density only shortens; `[&_:not([data-density])]` resets `--md-sys-density` to 0 there, so one with its own `density` still follows it), the pickers' surface buttons (calendar navigation, the mode toggles, the close button: `density={0}`) and `Autocomplete`'s input chips (its row maths assume 32px chips). **Icon buttons in parts that keep their own size** (2026-10-15, owner decision) stay at density 0: top app bars and search app bars (`AppBarItems`), docked and floating toolbars (button groups inside too: `ButtonGroup` passes its parent's `density` on), search bars and their views (`SearchField`), all through `ButtonContext.density` (`standardIconButtons` = `{ iconButtonVariant: 'standard', density: 0 }`); a navigation rail's `header`, a full-screen `DialogHeader`'s `action` and a `SideSheet`'s `actions` through `DensityZeroButtons` (the enclosing `ButtonContext` with `density: 0`); and the built-in close, back and collapse buttons of dialogs and sheets and a snackbar's dismiss button (`density={0}`; the snackbar keeps its 48px height, 2026-10-17). An icon button's own `density` wins over `ButtonContext`'s. Buttons have no density: a denser layout uses a smaller `size`. `Tabs` writes its density on the tab row, not on the panels; `List` on its root, so the controls in its items follow.
  - **Targets:** a dense field keeps a 48px hit area (its container's `::before` / `::after` add 2px above and below at -3), and its icon buttons keep their 48px touch targets beyond the container. Menu items at 40 / 36 / 32px are stacked rows, so a 48px target would overlap the next item; they keep non-overlapping full-width rows above WCAG 2.5.8's 24px minimum, and density is the reader's own choice, as M3 asks. The site's display settings offer density with small buttons that keep 48px targets (M3: "keep all the targets to change it at a minimum of 48x48").
- `createTheme({ name, seed, variant, contrast })` — runtime custom theme. `contrast` takes one level or a list (default: all three).
- `palettes` (on `createTheme`, `ThemeSeed` and the CLI's repeatable `--palette <name>=<source>`) takes any of the scheme's six palettes (`primary`, `secondary`, `tertiary`, `error`, `neutral`, `neutralVariant`) from another variant of the same seed or from a hex colour, as Material Theme Builder's core colours do (2026-10-08; docs/plans/create-theme-palettes.md). Roles still take their tones from the theme's own variant, so contrast holds. Without `palettes`, output is byte-for-byte unchanged. The common case is vivid accents on calm surfaces: `variant: 'vibrant'` with `palettes: { neutral: 'tonal-spot', neutralVariant: 'tonal-spot' }`.
- **Success and warning** (2026-10-10; docs/plans/custom-colors.md): every theme has `success`, `on-success`, `success-container`, `on-success-container` and the same for `warning`, made the way Material Theme Builder makes custom colours. Each colour's tonal palette (green `#1E8E3E`, amber `#F9AB00`) is harmonised toward the seed (`Blend.harmonize`, up to 15° of hue) and takes the error roles' tone rules, so it has error's contrast in every mode and at every contrast level (a test checks 4.5:1 for content and 3:1 against `surface` for every built-in theme). `customColors` and `harmonize: false` on `createTheme`, `ThemeSeed` and the CLI (`--custom`, `--no-harmonize`) set a brand's own.
- CLI: `npx @windmorph/react theme --seed <hex> --name <name> [--variant] [--contrast] [--out]` → static CSS (no flash). The bin is `windmorph`.

## 6. Motion system (M3 Expressive)

Springs replace duration + easing pairs.

- **Two motion schemes**: `expressive` (visible overshoot/bounce, **default**) and `standard` (restrained, no visible overshoot). Set via `data-motion` / `ThemeProvider` / `ThemeScope`, independent of colour theme.
- **Two spring families**:
  - **Spatial** — position, size, shape, rotation. May overshoot.
  - **Effects** — colour, opacity. Never overshoot (critically damped).
- **Three speeds** each: fast, default, slow.
- Expressive spatial overshoot: fast ≈ 9%, default/slow ≈ 1.5%. Standard spatial: no visible overshoot. Exact damping/stiffness taken from Compose `MotionScheme` tokens (decision #3).
- **Delivery**:
  - CSS: `--md-sys-motion-spring-{spatial|effects}-{fast|default|slow}-{easing|duration}`, easing as `linear()` approximations; values switch with `data-motion`. Utilities like `ease-m3-spatial-fast` read these variables, so they follow the scheme automatically.
  - JS: `useM3Spring('spatial', 'fast')` → Motion `{ type: 'spring', stiffness, damping, mass }` for the active scheme.
- **Reduced motion** (2026-10-15; WCAG 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions, M3's carousel guidance, and Android's "Remove animations", under which Compose's animations end at once): under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, non-essential movement is removed. Spatial transitions (slides, size and shape changes, morphs, rotations, travelling indicators) become instant, or a short fade on the effects springs for a surface that enters or leaves; state changes stay visible, with colours and opacity still fading. Essential motion keeps working: indeterminate progress and loading indicators (they convey activity), and anything that follows the pointer or the scroll position directly (drags, a pull, a two-row app bar collapsing with the page), but nothing springs or overshoots after it. Before, reduced motion mostly switched the springs to the `standard` scheme, so most movement still ran. The library models reduced motion only through the media query; `data-motion` has no reduced setting.
  - **Tokens:** the reduced-motion token set is the `standard` scheme with every `--md-sys-motion-spring-spatial-*-duration` at 0ms (generated, `scripts/lib/token-css.ts`), so anything on the spatial springs, and the `duration-m3-spatial-*` utilities, changes at once without per-component code; the effects springs keep their durations. A transition list that drops to `opacity` under reduced motion must give opacity an effects duration of its own, since the list's first duration (often a spatial one) would otherwise apply to it.
  - **Shared pieces:** `reducedMotionFade` (`src/motion/reduced-motion.ts`): classes that make a surface entering with `@starting-style` and leaving with `data-exiting` only fade under reduced motion (default effects in, fast effects out); its own entry and exit geometry is `motion-safe:`. `usePresence` takes several end properties, so an exit ends on the slide or on the fade. `useM3Spring` still returns the `standard` spring under reduced motion (public API unchanged); components that animate positions in script skip the animation themselves (`useReducedMotion`).
  - **Tested:** `apps/docs/e2e/reduced-motion.spec.ts` with `e2e/reduced-motion.ts`, which records every part on each animation frame (script-driven motion included) and fails if a box, transform, clip or corner passes through a value between its first and last, or if a transition other than fades and colours starts; component specs keep their own reduced-motion tests (dialog, menu, search, carousel, pickers, progress, loading indicator, buttons, button group, split button, icon faces, shapes).

  | Component                                                                   | Under reduced motion                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Kind                |
  | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
  | Button, icon button, split button, FAB, card, chips, menu items, list items | Press and selection morphs snap; the ripple appears at full size where it ends; colours and elevation fade. The split button's chevron turns over at once; a button group's press bump doesn't happen                                                                                                                                                                         | Instant             |
  | Checkbox, radio, switch                                                     | The check mark appears without drawing, the dot at full size, the thumb jumps (and resizes) to its end; colours fade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Instant             |
  | Text field                                                                  | The label floats and settles at once; the border, indicator and notch change at once; colours fade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Instant             |
  | Chips                                                                       | A filter chip's check and padding appear at once; the check fades                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Instant + fade      |
  | FAB                                                                         | Show / hide (`visible`, `appear`) only fade, without the scale; an `ExtendedFab` collapses and expands at once, its label fading. A hidden FAB (and a FAB menu's anchor, and a toolbar leaving on scroll) turns `visibility: hidden` after the longer of the spatial and effects springs (`max()`), so the fade still shows when the spatial springs are instant (2026-10-15) | Fade                |
  | FAB menu                                                                    | The button becomes the close button at once; the items fade in and out together at full width, without the stagger; `rotate` icons keep only the crossfade                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Fade                |
  | Icon faces (`iconTransition="rotate"`)                                      | Only the crossfade, without the turn                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Fade                |
  | Navigation bar and rail                                                     | The active pill fades in whole instead of expanding (`slide` fades in place); the rail widens and narrows at once; the modal rail fades in and out at full width; `hide-on-scroll` stays put                                                                                                                                                                                  | Fade / instant      |
  | Tabs                                                                        | The indicator jumps to the selected tab; a scrollable row scrolls to it at once                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Instant             |
  | Top app bar                                                                 | `enter-always` stays on screen (a two-row bar only collapses with the scroll, as `exit-until-collapsed`); collapsing still follows the scroll position                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Scroll-tracked kept |
  | Toolbars                                                                    | Leaving on scroll fades out instead of sliding; collapse and expand (and the FAB layout) happen at once                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Fade / instant      |
  | Bottom and side sheets                                                      | Modal sheets fade in and out where they rest, or where a drag to close let them go; detent changes are instant; a drag still follows the pointer; a standard side sheet's width opens at once; the scrim fades                                                                                                                                                                | Fade                |
  | Dialog, picker dialog                                                       | Only the wrapper's and scrim's fades: no slide, clip, scale or container transform                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Fade                |
  | Menu, select, autocomplete                                                  | The menu only fades (no reveal); the arrow turns over at once; filtered options jump                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Fade                |
  | Tooltip, snackbar                                                           | Only fade, without the reveal from the anchor or the bottom edge                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Fade                |
  | Search                                                                      | The docked field is at its focused width at once and the dropdown fades; the full-screen view fades over the window                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Fade                |
  | Lists                                                                       | Nested rows only fade (no height); in a switch (rows opening as others fold) the folding rows go at once and the opening ones fade in, so what's below shifts once (with the folding rows above the pressed one, the page scrolls once instead and the pressed row stays put); the chevron flips at once; corners snap                                                        | Fade                |
  | Date picker                                                                 | A new month, a mode switch and the year list only fade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Fade                |
  | Time picker                                                                 | The hand jumps on taps and the hour / minute switch; a drag moves it directly; the faces crossfade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Instant             |
  | Carousel                                                                    | Items keep the large size, unmasked (M3); keyboard scrolling jumps; the pressed corner snaps                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Instant             |
  | Slider                                                                      | No animation either way: the handle and tracks follow the value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | —                   |
  | Progress indicator                                                          | The wave stops travelling; value, amplitude and the indeterminate-to-determinate handoff snap; indeterminate motion stays                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Essential kept      |
  | Loading indicator                                                           | Indeterminate: only a steady rotation of its first shape, no morphs or springy turns; determinate follows `value`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Essential kept      |
  | Pull to refresh                                                             | The indicator follows the pull directly; on release, or when a refresh starts or ends, it jumps to the threshold or away; the refreshing indicator only rotates                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Essential kept      |

- Legacy M3 easing/duration tokens kept only for consumers migrating from baseline M3.

## 7. Layout & breakpoints

M3 window size classes as Tailwind custom breakpoints (alongside Tailwind defaults):

| Class       | Width       | Utility prefix         |
| ----------- | ----------- | ---------------------- |
| Compact     | < 600px     | default (mobile-first) |
| Medium      | 600–839px   | `medium:`              |
| Expanded    | 840–1199px  | `expanded:`            |
| Large       | 1200–1599px | `large:`               |
| Extra-large | ≥ 1600px    | `xlarge:`              |

Adaptive components (Navigation rail ↔ Flexible navigation bar, dialogs ↔ full-screen dialogs, sheets) use these.

## 8. Consumer overrides (broad → narrow)

1. **Tokens via CSS** — globally or per theme:
   ```css
   :root {
     --md-sys-shape-corner-full: 12px;
     --md-ref-typeface-brand: 'Inter';
   }
   [data-theme='ocean'] {
     --md-sys-color-primary: #0050c8;
   }
   ```
2. **Component tokens** — `--md-comp-*` (§4), globally, per theme or on any ancestor. Role values go on `:root, [data-theme]`, so they resolve again in each theme and scope:
   ```css
   :root,
   [data-theme] {
     --md-comp-filled-card-container-color: var(--md-sys-color-secondary-container);
   }
   ```
   **Elevation shadows follow the scope** (2026-10-14): `--md-sys-elevation-level-*` mix in `--md-sys-color-shadow`, and a custom property resolves where it is declared, so on `:root` alone a scope's shadow colour was ignored. They are declared on `:root, [data-theme]` like the role values, with the same defaults, so a `ThemeScope` (and an overlay portalled from it) that sets its own shadow colour gets its shadows. Override a level there too.
3. **Brand theme** — `createTheme({ seed })` or the CLI.
4. **Restrict the picker** — `<ThemeProvider themes={['baseline','ocean','acme']}>`.
5. **Subtree** — `<ThemeScope theme="forest" mode="dark">`.
6. **Component** — `className`, `classNames={{ root, label, icon }}` (consumer always wins via `cn()`); exported variant definitions (`buttonStyles`, …) can be extended with new variants.

## 9. Component API conventions

- `variant`, `size`, `className`, `classNames` (per slot), `ref` as prop. `classNames` has a slot for every visible part a component draws (its surfaces, text, icons, indicators, state layers, built-in tooltips); purely structural wrappers and library-owned motion layers have none, so they stay layout-safe. A slot never takes `className` off the outermost element; where the outermost element is itself a slot (a sheet's `container`), that slot merges with `className`. A dialog's `positioner` sits inside its outermost layer, so it doesn't (§10 rule 1). The site's `class-names-keys.test.ts` checks that each core component's props list documents every slot of its `classNames` type as a key, and no others (2026-10-14; the separate slots line and its hand-kept list were removed, 2026-10-15). Added 2026-10-14: `TimePicker` `dial` (documented), `DateRangePicker` `rangeBand` (each cell the range band spans; `DateRangePickerClassNames` extends `DatePickerClassNames`), `SideSheet` `container` (as `BottomSheet`'s), `Menu` and `Select` `sheet` (the bottom sheet's surface in sheet presentation) and `FabMenuItem` `label` (the label, now wrapped in a span).
- Controlled + uncontrolled (`value`/`defaultValue`, `open`/`defaultOpen`, `checked`/`defaultChecked` for checkboxes and switches, `selected`/`defaultSelected` for toggles, `onChange`/`onOpenChange`/`onCheckedChange`/`onSelectedChange`).
- State exposed as `data-*` attributes for consumer styling.
- **API naming round** (2026-10-17, decided: every convention that isn't best practice changes before 0.1.0, with no aliases since nothing is released; the review compared HTML, WAI-ARIA APG, M3 / Compose and the leading React libraries). One name per idea across the library:
  - **Words are in `labels`:** the date and time pickers' `strings` became `labels` (`DateFieldLabels`, `DateInputLabels`, `DatePickerLabels`, `PickerDialogLabels`, `TimePickerLabels`). Every labels type lists all its keys and the prop is `Partial<…Labels>`.
  - **`checked` for checkable inputs:** `Checkbox` and `Switch` take `checked` / `defaultChecked` / `onCheckedChange(checked)` (HTML, Compose, the leading libraries; the core's `ListItem` already did), and checkboxes, switches, radios and list controls mark `data-checked`. `selected` stays for `aria-pressed` / `aria-selected` controls.
  - **`min` / `max`** (HTML's range and date inputs, every slider library but React Aria): `Slider`, `RangeSlider`, `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker` and `DateField`, replacing `minValue` / `maxValue`.
  - **Optional parts are named after the part,** as `ticks`, `stopIndicator` and `closeButton` already were: `Slider` / `RangeSlider` `valueLabel` (was `showValueLabel`), `DatePicker` / `DateRangePicker` `header` and `modeToggle` (`showHeader`, `showModeToggle`), `TextField` `counter` (`showCharacterCount`).
  - **Icon slots are `leadingIcon` / `trailingIcon`** everywhere (M3's names): `Slider`'s `startIcon` / `endIcon` were the last pair under other names.
  - **Closing follows HTML's `<dialog closedby>`:** `Dialog`, `BottomSheet`, `SideSheet` and `PickerDialog` take `closedBy: 'any' | 'closerequest' | 'none'` (the `ClosedBy` type) instead of `dismissable` + `keyboardDismissDisabled`, a negative pair that also allowed the one combination HTML rules out (an outside press closes, Escape doesn't). Defaults keep the behaviour: `closerequest` for an alert dialog, `any` otherwise.
  - **Positive booleans:** `ButtonGroup`, `ChipSet`, `List` and `Menu` take `deselectable` (default `true`; `false` keeps at least one item selected) instead of React Aria's `disallowEmptySelection`.
  - **No leaked React Aria props:** `Select` and `Autocomplete` drop React Stately's deprecated `selectedKey` / `defaultSelectedKey` / `onSelectionChange` (they take `value` / `defaultValue` / `onChange`); `Autocomplete`'s `allowsCustomValue` is `allowCustomValue`; `Select`'s `shouldCloseOnSelect` is `closeOnSelect` and `allowsEmptyCollection` is gone; `Switch` takes `required` and `invalid` (not `isRequired` / `isInvalid`); `Menu` drops `shouldFocusWrap` and `escapeKeyBehavior`; `Checkbox`, `Switch`, `TextField` and `RadioGroup` drop the deprecated `validationState` and `excludeFromTabOrder`. **Enforced:** `packages/react/src/public-api-names.test.ts` reads every exported component's props with the TypeScript checker (inherited ones included) and fails on an `is*` / `allows*` / `should*` / `disallow*` name, `validationState`, `excludeFromTabOrder`, any name this round replaced, a spaced multi-word enum value, or a component without `className`, `style`, `ref` and `id` (listed exceptions, each with its reason).
  - **Arrow keys wrap everywhere** (2026-10-17): `Menu` and the `Select` / `Autocomplete` option lists now wrap from the last item to the first and back, as lists, tabs, chip sets, toolbars and M3's own menus already did (APG makes wrapping optional; React Aria's default for menus and list boxes is not to). There is no prop for it.
  - **One single-choice API:** `Tabs` takes `value` / `defaultValue` / `onChange` instead of React Aria's `selectedKey` / `defaultSelectedKey` / `onSelectionChange`, and a radio `List` reports its choice with `onChange` (2026-10-24; it was the library's one `onValueChange`). A `List`'s `onChange` is the radio list's value callback, not the DOM's change event, which a list root never fires.
  - **One placement vocabulary:** every "where does this part go" prop is `*Placement` (`NavigationBar` `iconPlacement`, as `Tabs`; `FloatingToolbar` `fabPlacement`; were `iconPosition` / `fabPosition`) and side values are CSS's logical `start` / `end` (`List` / `ListItem` `controlPlacement`, was `leading` / `trailing`; M3's leading / trailing stay the names of icon slots).
  - **Hyphenated multi-word values:** placements are `'bottom-start'`, `'top-end'` and so on (`TooltipTrigger`, `RichTooltipTrigger`, `Menu`'s new `MenuPlacement` type), as every other multi-word value already was and as Floating UI and most libraries write them; `utils/placement.ts` maps them to React Aria's spaced, physical form.
  - **One name per idea:** `Dialog`'s `quick` is `instant`, `Menu`'s name for the same "no motion" switch.
  - **Every built-in word is in `labels`:** `DialogHeader`'s `closeLabel` is `labels.close` ("Close") and `SplitButton`'s `menuLabel` is `labels.menu` ("More options"), both optional now that they have English defaults. Words with no sensible default (`NavItem` `badgeLabel`, `TextField` `prefixLabel` / `suffixLabel`) stay props.
  - **`as` picks the element**, as Pro's components already did: `ButtonBase` (and every button built on it) takes `as: 'button' | 'div'` instead of React Aria's `elementType`.
  - **One size scale (`xs`–`xl`):** `Fab` and `FabMenu` sizes are `default | md | lg` (were `default | medium | large`, now as `ExtendedFab`'s `sm | md | lg`) and `ListItemVideo`'s are `sm | lg` (were `small | large`). M3 component names that are variants (`TopAppBar` `small | medium | large`) stay.
  - **`default*` for initial state, hooks too:** `useToolbarScrollExpansion` and `useToolbarScrollExit` take `defaultExpanded` / `defaultVisible` (were `initialExpanded` / `initialVisible`).
  - **One `classNames` slot name per part:** `List`'s `supporting` is `supportingText` (as `TextField`, `Select` and `RadioGroup`), the sheets' `closeButton` slot is `close` (as `DialogHeader` and `PickerDialog`; `BottomSheet`'s boolean `closeButton` prop stays) and `SideSheet`'s `backButton` is `back`.
  - **Root props everywhere:** every component with an element of its own takes `className`, `style`, `ref` and `id`. A field's `id` goes to the element that takes its value (`Select`'s trigger, `Autocomplete`'s and `DateField`'s input, as `TextField`'s already did), so a `<label for>` or an error summary's link reaches it; `Slider` / `RangeSlider` put it on their `group`, and `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker`, `TimePicker`, `List`, `Carousel` and `SearchBar` on their root. `Tooltip`, `RichTooltip`, `TimePickerModeToggle`, `PickerDialog`, the list media (`ListItemAvatar`, `ListItemImage`, `ListItemVideo`) and the `TouchTarget` primitive gained `style` and `ref` (and `id` where React Aria doesn't own it); `Menu`'s `ref` reaches its `role="menu"` list. Triggers, providers, contexts and collection items have no element of their own and are exempt, each with its reason in `public-api-names.test.ts`. **DOM attributes too** (2026-10-17): the root also takes its element's DOM attributes, as Radix, React Aria Components and Ark spread them. The components whose other props go to a React Aria hook (`Checkbox`, `Switch`, `Radio`, `RadioGroup`, `TextField`, `Select`, `Autocomplete`, `DateField`, `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker`, `TimePicker`, `Slider`, `RangeSlider`, `Tabs`, `Carousel`, `SearchBar`, `List`, `Menu`'s list, `PickerDialog`'s panel) split them with `splitRootProps` (`utils/root-props.ts`, type `RootDOMProps`; both public from `/primitives` for Pro and apps): `data-*`, the `aria-*` attributes other than the naming ones, global attributes (`title`, `lang`, `dir`, `hidden`, `inert`, `translate`, `draggable`) and pointer, mouse, touch, drag, wheel, scroll and animation events go on the root, merged with its own handlers; the naming attributes (`aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-details`, `aria-errormessage`), focus, key and input events and React Aria's own callbacks stay with the hook, which puts them on the element it labels (a field's input, the tab list), so a key event never fires twice as it bubbles. **Widget attributes stay on the widget** (2026-10-17): where the root only wraps the element React Aria labels (`TextField`, `Checkbox`, `Switch`, `Radio`, `Select`, `Autocomplete`, `DateField`, `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker`, `TimePicker`, `Tabs`, `SearchBar`), `splitRootProps(props, { wrapper: true })` also keeps the attributes that describe that element (`WidgetAria`: `aria-controls`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-autocomplete`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-required`, `aria-invalid`, `aria-readonly`) off the root, where ARIA doesn't allow them on a generic `div` or a `label` (they used to land there, even those React Aria's types route to the input). `TextField`, `Checkbox`, `Switch` and `Radio` put them on their input (`pickWidgetAria` hands over the ones React Aria's hooks don't forward), so a field that opens its own suggestions, or a form library's `aria-invalid`, reaches it;
