the component's own state wins when set (2026-10-24: `Checkbox`, `Switch` and `Radio` merge the app's attributes before React Aria's, so `invalid` beats an app's `aria-invalid={false}`, and a radio drops an app's `aria-invalid` / `aria-required` while its group carries that state); `Select`, `Autocomplete`, `SearchBar`, `DateField`, the pickers and `Tabs` set them themselves, so their props (`WrapperRootDOMProps`) leave them out. A root that is the widget itself (`RadioGroup`, `Slider`'s group, `Carousel`, `List`, `Menu`'s list, `TimePickerModeToggle`) takes them as before. `Tooltip`, `RichTooltip`, `TimePickerModeToggle` and the list media spread them on their root; a tooltip's `data-tokens` joins its surface's token set. **Enforced:** `public-api-names.test.ts` checks every component's props for `aria-hidden`, `title` and `onPointerEnter` (exceptions where `title` means a headline, and the `SelectionControl` primitive, whose `rootProps` carry them), and `root-dom-props.test.tsx` renders every component that splits its props and checks they land on the element its `ref` reaches.
  - **Recipes aren't props:** the exported `*StyleProps` types say in their TSDoc that they are a recipe's variant keys (some named `has*` / `is*` after the classes), for building on the styles, not component props; the props tables don't list them.
  - **Documented and guarded:** the site's **API conventions** guide (`/api-conventions`, after Customisation) states these rules for both packages, and `apps/site/scripts/old-api-names.test.ts` fails if a replaced name (or a spaced placement, an old size or `quick`) appears in `content/`, `examples/` or the playground descriptors, ignoring the Compose names the "From Compose" mappings quote.
  - **Disabled items stay focusable** in `Tabs`, `List` and the option lists, as `Menu`'s did (2026-10-17, decided; APG "Focusability of disabled controls": screen reader users find them; React Aria skips them by default). They can't be selected or activated and say so (`aria-disabled`). An `Accordion` header in Pro is a standalone button, so a disabled one stays out of the Tab order.
- A component with component tokens marks its root with `data-variant` (and `data-size` where it has sizes); both are public styling hooks (2026-10-14). `data-tokens` is internal and not documented; a set passed on it is appended to the component's own (2026-10-16, §4's component tokens).
- Icons passed as React elements (`icon`, `leadingIcon`, `trailingIcon`).
- Accessibility target **WCAG 2.2 AA**: keyboard, visible focus, 48px touch targets (even for XS visuals), `prefers-reduced-motion`, RTL, screen-reader labels required by types where needed (e.g. icon-only buttons require `aria-label`).
- **Accessibility fixes** (2026-10-15; WAI-ARIA APG patterns, React Aria behaviour and the M3 accessibility pages; browser checks in `apps/docs/e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`):
  - **Hidden is `inert`:** a bar scrolled fully off screen (a `TopAppBar` with `enter-always` or a small one's `exit-until-collapsed`, a `NavigationBar` with `hide-on-scroll`) is `inert` until it comes back; while keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) is in it, it stays on screen, and focus landing in a partly hidden bar brings it back (M3: "Maintain access to app bar actions when the content is scrolled"; WCAG 2.4.11). Pointer focus doesn't pin it. A hidden `NavigationBar` isn't made `inert` while focus is in it (2026-10-15): an item pressed with a pointer kept focus as the bar scrolled away, and `inert` dropped it to `<body>`; it becomes inert once focus leaves. A collapsed standard `BottomSheet` makes the content's top-level children that don't fit entirely in `peekHeight` `inert` (keyed on `data-detent="collapsed"`, `sheet/use-peek-inert.ts`), moving focus to the handle if it was in them.
  - **Initial focus** goes to the first interactive element in every modal surface: `Dialog`, and now the modal `BottomSheet` (its drag handle, M3) and modal `SideSheet` (its first icon button, M3: "Focus lands on (non-disabled) icon button"), through `focusFirstInteractive` (`utils/focus.ts`).
  - **Focus return with a fallback:** a snackbar that leaves with focus in it returns focus where it came from or, if that element is gone, to the control after it in the Tab order (else the one before), recorded as focus enters (APG: move focus to a logical place).
  - **Radio groups select on the arrow keys:** a single-select `ChipSet` or `ButtonGroup` announces `radiogroup` / `radio`, so it follows the APG radio group as `RadioGroup` does: every arrow key (and Home / End) moves and selects, wrapping, and Tab enters at the selected item (`utils/composite-keys.ts`). M3's keyboard tables say the arrows move focus for chips and button groups; the role's expectation wins on the web. Other controls in the group (the overflow button) stay on the arrows' path without being checked.
  - **Toolbar keys:** `Toolbar`s and toolbar-style `ButtonGroup`s wrap their arrows and support Home / End (APG toolbar); a text field keeps those keys. Tab still moves through every toolbar control (M3).
  - **Search:** Enter in the collapsed bar opens the view, as a click and ↓ do (Compose expands the bar as its field takes focus); `onSubmit` runs from the open view.
  - **Stacked dialog actions** are rendered in their visual order, confirming action on top (M3: "Confirming actions appear above dismissive actions"), so Tab and screen readers go top to bottom; on one line, source order. `DialogActions` measures whether they fit (`data-stacked`; `--m3-dialog-actions-stack` turns it off in the full-screen bottom bar), keeps the buttons' keys so switching moves rather than remounts them, and refocuses the focused one after the move. **Initial focus on the least destructive action** (2026-10-15, owner decision; WAI-ARIA APG alert dialog: focus the least destructive action): when the dialog's first interactive element is one of its actions, initial focus goes to the dismissive action, the first in source order: the row's first button on one line, its last (bottom) one when stacked. `leastDestructiveAction` picks it from the row's `data-stacked`, written in the same render that reorders the row, so it holds whether the dialog takes focus before or after the actions stack (tested on one line, stacked from the start and reopened while stacked).
  - **Tooltips that repeat the name** don't describe: `Fab`, a collapsed `ExtendedFab` and a closed `FabMenu` drop `aria-describedby` as `IconButton` does (`WithoutDescription` in `tooltip-trigger.tsx`).
  - **48px hit areas without layout changes:** an xs / sm `Slider`'s track takes presses 2px past its 44px (a transparent `::before` under its parts); the `TimePicker`'s AM / PM options reach 48px away from each other (the options round their own outer corners, so the selector no longer clips them); a small `Carousel` item narrower than 48px gets a press area under its mask, grown into the spacing (half next to another growing item, all of it otherwise, the other way at the viewport's edge; `carousel-press-area.ts`), and a masked carousel's slots take no presses outside their visible item. **Not changed:** menu, `Select` and `Autocomplete` option rows stay 44px at density 0 (M3 Expressive's menu token); they're stacked full width with no gap, so a 48px hit area would overlap the neighbouring row, and they meet WCAG 2.5.8 (24px).
- Links (`href`) use client-side routing via React Aria `RouterProvider`.

### Expressive Button family (API fixed from day one)

- **Button** (built): `variant` = `elevated | filled | tonal | outlined | text`; `size` = `xs | sm | md | lg | xl`; `shape` = `round | square`; `toggle` + `selected`/`defaultSelected`/`onSelectedChange` (selected state changes colour and shape); **press morph** — corners animate squarer on press. Also `leadingIcon` (one icon, before the label: the spec says "Don't use two icons in the same button", so `trailingIcon` was removed on 2026-10-13), `href` (renders `<a>` via React Aria `useLink`), `disabled`, `classNames` slots `root | content | label | icon`, and `ButtonContext` so a parent (button group) can cascade `variant` / `size` / `shape` / `disabled`.
  - **Values follow the M3 Expressive specs and the token files** (2026-10-13; earlier they followed Compose's code where it overrides its tokens): text buttons use `primary` (Compose's code; the token file says `on-surface-variant`, flagged by a Compose TODO); XS padding is 16px with an 8px icon gap (`ButtonXSmallTokens`; Compose's code uses 12 / 4); small text buttons have 16px padding like the other small buttons (the spec: "24dp: Not recommended. Use 16dp"; Compose used 12); and toggle buttons press to the same corners as the others, 8 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 16px ("Both round and square buttons should have the same pressed shape"; Compose pressed small toggles to 6px). Label type: `label-large` (XS, S), `title-medium` (M), `headline-small` (L), `headline-large` (XL).
  - **Press morph** is a CSS `border-radius` transition on the root (border-radius is not a transform, so §10 rule 4 holds). Since 2026-10-13 it used the **fast spatial** spring for every button, toggle or not, which bounces in the expressive scheme (the spec drives "rounded corners" with spatial springs). **Since 2026-10-15 only toggles do**, as Compose's `ToggleButton` (FastSpatial): a plain button, the split button's halves and every icon button morph on the **default effects** spring, which never bounces, as Compose's `Button` (`Button.kt`, "DefaultEffects is intentional here"), `SplitButton` and `IconButton`. `container-motion` reads `--m3-shape-spring-duration` / `--m3-shape-spring-easing`, which these set (`nonBouncingMorph` in `button-styles.ts`); rapid presses and reduced motion still override them. The `container-motion` utility holds it for every interactive container (buttons, icon buttons, split buttons, FABs, menu items), so `container-motion-spatial` is gone. **Rapid presses:** the spec allows "a modified motion curve to avoid resonant effects from overlapping animations" on the web. `useM3Interaction` sets `data-rapid-press` when a press starts while the last release morph (the fast spatial spring's duration) is still running, and `container-motion` then morphs on the fast effects spring, which doesn't overshoot. Full corners are capped at half the container height (`min(var(--md-sys-shape-corner-full), h/2)`) so the transition interpolates smoothly.
  - **Toggle shape:** selected round buttons become square, as Compose does. Selected square buttons become round, which follows m3.material.io; Compose only ships round toggles. Text buttons have no toggle form, which the types enforce.
  - **Disabled:** container `on-surface` 10% and content `on-surface-variant` 38% for every variant (the Expressive token files). Compose's older `FilledTonalButtonTokens` (12%, `on-surface`) are not used.
  - **Elevation:** elevated rests at level 1 and hovers at 2; filled and tonal rest at 0 and hover at 1. A pressed button shows the pressed level (elevated 1, filled and tonal 0, the `PressedContainerElevation` tokens) even while the pointer is over it: the hover shadows are `data-hovered:not-data-pressed:` (2026-10-13; before, a mouse press kept the hover level). Split button halves share it through `buttonVariantClasses`.
  - **Outline inside the edge spacing** (2026-10-13): the leading and trailing space (16 / 16 / 24 / 48 / 64px) is measured from the container's edge for every style, so the outlined style's outline (1 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 3px, the `--m3-button-border` variable that also sets `border-width`) comes out of the padding (`px-[calc(16px-var(--m3-button-border,0px))]`). An outlined button is now the same size as a filled one with the same content (before, 2–6px wider). Split button halves do the same; chips took the same approach (`--m3-chip-border`). To change the outline width, set `--m3-button-border` rather than a `border-*` class, so the padding follows.
  - **Disabled outlines** (2026-10-13): a disabled outlined button's outline is `outline-variant` at 10%; a selected outlined toggle has no outline, disabled or not (`data-selected:border-transparent`; the dimmed outline is `data-disabled:not-data-selected:`), the same as IconButton. Before, a selected toggle kept a 10% outline once disabled. A selected, disabled outlined toggle's container is `on-surface` at 10% (2026-10-15, `OutlinedButtonTokens.SelectedDisabledContainerColor`, as the outlined icon button; it was `outline-variant` at 10%, from Compose's code); an unselected one keeps `outline-variant` at 10%.
  - **Selected icon** (2026-10-13, "use an outlined icon when unselected, and a filled version of the icon when selected"): toggles take `selectedLeadingIcon`, shown instead of `leadingIcon` while selected (as `IconButton`'s `selectedIcon`), so an uncontrolled toggle can swap its icon too.
  - **Forced colours** (2026-10-15, as Material Web's `button/internal/_shared.scss`): the browser paints every container as `Canvas`, so filled, elevated, tonal and text buttons draw a 1px `CanvasText` border, taking the outlined style's `--m3-button-border` so the padding subtracts it and the size doesn't change; filled and tonal icon buttons draw the same border (their sizes are fixed). A selected toggle (Button or IconButton) is filled with `Highlight` and its content `HighlightText` (`forced-color-adjust: none` keeps them), so it can be told from an unselected one; disabled buttons are `GrayText`. The classes are `forcedColorClasses` in `button-styles.ts`, shared with the split button's halves and icon buttons, and `!important`, applying only in forced colours. Playwright checks each under `emulateMedia({ forcedColors: 'active' })`.
  - **Pending** (2026-10-17, plan `docs/plans/button-pending.md`; M3's loading indicator guidelines: "Loading indicators can be placed within other components, such as buttons, to indicate that the action is ongoing"): `pending` (React's `useFormStatus().pending` / `useActionState` word, React Aria's `isPending` without the `is`) on `Button` and `IconButton` only; toggles (the types forbid it: a toggle's state is its selection), the FABs and `SplitButton` have none. The core `LoadingIndicator` (uncontained, indeterminate; M3 recommends it over the indeterminate circular indicator for 200ms–5s waits) replaces the content: the icon and label stay laid out at `opacity: 0` in the content wrapper's one grid cell (`inline-grid`, `grid-area: 1 / 1`) and the indicator sits in a zero-size centred slot in the same cell, so the button never changes size (24 / 24 / 24 / 32 / 40px for XS–XL, the icon's size but at least M3's 24px). It's in the content colour (the slot sets `--md-comp-loading-indicator-active-indicator-color: currentColor`, M3's 3:1 rule); a progress indicator passed as `pendingIndicator` also gets the label's colour and no track (M3's "Progress indicators in buttons"). The swap is an opacity crossfade on the fast effects spring, delayed 200ms on the way in (M3's lower bound) so a quick action never flashes, immediate on the way out, and kept under reduced motion. Presses are blocked from the first frame (`onPress`, `onClick`, Enter, Space, no hover or pressed layer), but the button isn't natively disabled, so focus stays and it stays in the Tab order (React Aria: "Pending buttons remain focusable, but are otherwise disabled"): `aria-disabled="true"`, `data-pending`, enabled colours. A submit button renders `type="button"` while pending and blocks a submission without a submitter, so Enter in a field doesn't send the form again; a link keeps `href` and role with its navigation prevented. The name gains `labels.pending` ("Loading"): after an `aria-label` ("Refresh, Loading", while the tooltip keeps the label), as an extra `aria-labelledby` id, or as a suffix at the end of the label's text (2026-10-24: inline text at `font-size: 0` with no line height or tracking, since browsers join a box after the label, such as an `sr-only` grid item, with a space, "Save , Loading"). No `aria-busy` (a live-region property). A button that turns pending while focus is in it announces its new name once, politely, through the internal body-level announcer (`utils/announce.ts`, prepared as soon as a button takes `pending`, so the region exists before its first message); nothing is announced when it ends, as the result is the app's to announce. `disabled` wins: natively disabled, the indicator in the disabled colour. The indicator's module loads on demand (`LazyLoadingIndicator`, preloaded when idle or on intent once a button takes a `pending` prop at all; `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails a static import), and the content hides only once it is mounted (`data-pending-shown`), so a server-rendered pending button shows its label. `ButtonBase` carries the behaviour (`ButtonBasePendingProps`, outside the props types the FABs and chips extend).
  - **Touch targets:** XS and S get a 48px `TouchTarget` inside the library-owned inner wrapper. Layout height stays 32 / 40px (a minimum since 2026-10-13: it grows with 200% text, see Typography).
  - **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `button-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-button-{filled,elevated,tonal,outlined,text}-*` (M3 Expressive's `md.comp.button.<variant>.*`, not the deprecated `filled-button` sets): the container and label colours, the elevated button's `container-elevation`, the outlined button's `outline-color`, and the toggle's `selected-*` / `unselected-*` colours with M3's system defaults (the elevated and outlined label and the outlined container have no unselected token, so an unselected toggle follows the resting one); and `--md-comp-button-{xsmall…xlarge}-container-shape-{round,square}`, with round capped at half the height (a derived value) and a selected round toggle taking the square token. The root carries `data-tokens="md-comp-button"`, `data-variant` and `data-size`. `buttonVariantClasses` read the same private variables, so the split button's halves follow the colour tokens. Hover, focus, pressed and disabled values, pressed shapes, connected-group corners, outline widths and sizes stay classes.
- **IconButton** (built): `variant` = `filled | tonal | outlined | standard` (no elevated or text), **filled by default** as the spec says ("Filled (default)"; 2026-10-13, it was standard like Compose's plain `IconButton`). Containers whose icon buttons M3 shows as standard (the toolbars, top app bar and search app bar) give them that default through `ButtonContext.iconButtonVariant` (`standardIconButtons`); an icon button's own `variant` wins. **Tooltip:** "On web, icon buttons should display a tooltip with an accessibility label", so an icon button wraps itself in a `TooltipTrigger` showing its `aria-label` (2026-10-13). `tooltip={false}` turns it off; it steps aside when named by `aria-labelledby`, and inside a `TooltipTrigger` or `RichTooltipTrigger` of the app's (`useInTooltipTrigger`), so there's never a second tooltip; same `size` / `shape` / `toggle`, plus `width` = `narrow | default | wide`, `icon`, an optional `selectedIcon` for toggles, and `href`. **Rotating icon** (2026-10-14, an **extension beyond the M3 spec**, as in Google's Expressive showcase): a toggle with `selectedIcon` takes `iconTransition` = `swap | rotate` (default `swap`, the icon changes at once). `rotate` uses the FAB menu's faces (`primitives/IconFaces.tsx`): the icon turns counter-clockwise (in LTR) and crossfades into `selectedIcon`, and back as it's deselected; reduced motion keeps only the crossfade. The home page bento's add / close tile uses it.
  - **Name required by the types:** either `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`.
  - **Pending** (2026-10-17): as Button's (above); the indicator takes the icon's place at the icon's size, 24px at least, in a zero-size slot, so even a dense XS or narrow button keeps its size.
  - **Tooltip, announced once** (2026-10-13): the built-in tooltip only repeats the `aria-label`, so the icon button drops the `aria-describedby` its `TooltipTrigger` adds (a `TriggerContext` wrapper inside `IconButton`; `Tooltip` itself is unchanged). Screen readers no longer read "Search, button, Search". A consumer's own `aria-describedby`, or a `TooltipTrigger` of the app's with other text, still describes the button.
  - **Width** = icon size + leading + trailing space. Compose's `Uniform` option is the default width, which resolves the `LargeIconButtonTokens` naming gap. In px (narrow / default / wide): XS 28/32/40, S 32/40/52, M 48/56/72, L 64/96/128, XL 104/136/184. Icons are 20/24/24/32/40px.
  - **Colour** (2026-10-13, from the spec's colour table; before, standard and outlined inherited the text colour like Compose's `LocalContentColor`): standard icons are `on-surface-variant`; outlined ones are `on-surface-variant` inside an `outline-variant` outline. Disabled is `on-surface` 38% for every variant, with filled / tonal / selected-outlined containers `on-surface` 10%. A disabled outlined icon button's outline dims to `outline-variant` at 10% (2026-10-13; before, it kept full strength): the token keeps `outline-variant` and gives no opacity, and 10% is the disabled outlined Button's (Compose's code draws the icon button's at 38% of the content colour; we keep both outlined styles alike). A container that needs another content colour for its standard and outlined icon buttons sets **`--md-comp-icon-button-icon-color`** on an ancestor, usually `currentColor` to inherit its own: the vibrant floating toolbar (so its standard icons are `on-primary-container`) and the app bars' navigation and action slots do. Inside a toolbar, `ToolbarContext` sets the icon button's `toolbar` variant, which only carries what the token can't: a vibrant toolbar's selected tonal toggles turn `surface-container` / `on-surface` (§9 Toolbars). There is no separate vibrant icon button variant.
  - **Selected toggles:** standard turns `primary`, filled `primary`/`on-primary` (unselected `surface-container`), tonal `secondary`/`on-secondary`, and outlined `inverse-surface`/`inverse-on-surface` with no border, disabled or not (2026-10-13, Compose `outlinedIconToggleButtonBorder` returns none when checked; before, a selected disabled outlined toggle showed its outline). Shape swaps round ↔ square (Compose tokens). Every icon button morph, toggles included, uses the non-bouncing default effects spring, as Compose does ("DefaultEffects is intentional here to prevent any bounce"); since 2026-10-15 the code does too: `container-motion` reads `--m3-shape-spring-duration` / `--m3-shape-spring-easing`, which the icon button sets (rapid presses and reduced motion still override them).
  - Reads `size` / `shape` / `disabled` and its default `iconButtonVariant` from `ButtonContext`, but not the Button `variant`, because the variant sets differ. A button group turns its `buttonVariant` into `iconButtonVariant` when icon buttons have that style (below).
  - **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `icon-button-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-icon-button-{standard,filled,tonal,outlined}-*` (M3 Expressive's `md.comp.icon-button.<variant>.*`): the container (filled, tonal) and icon colours, the outlined `outline-color`, the toggle's `selected-*` colours and the filled and tonal `unselected-*` colours; and `--md-comp-icon-button-{xsmall…xlarge}-container-shape-{round,square}`. The standard and outlined icon colours fall back to the existing `--md-comp-icon-button-icon-color` contract, then `on-surface-variant`, so its setters (app bar, search, vibrant toolbar, dialog close) work unchanged. The root carries `data-tokens="md-comp-icon-button"`, `data-variant` and `data-size`. The vibrant toolbar's selected tonal colours stay a toolbar-owned class.
  - **Density** (2026-10-15, §5): `density` (or the theme's) takes 4px a step off the height and width of every size and width, never below the icon plus 4px (XS 32 → 24px, S 40 → 28px, M 56 → 44px; `--m3-icon-button-height`); round corners are capped at half the dense height. XS and S keep their `TouchTarget`, and M gets the same 48 × 48px box as its content's `::after`. Icon buttons in a text field's icon slots and on the pickers keep their size.
- **Colour note:** the 2025 colour spec makes `inverse-on-surface` noticeably muted (baseline light `#a09ba1` on `#0f0d12`, about 7:1 contrast). Selected outlined buttons and icon buttons look greyer than the 2021 palette did. That's correct, not a bug.
- **Button group** (built, `ButtonGroup`, a composite): `variant` = `standard | connected`; shares `size` / `shape` / `disabled` and `buttonVariant` through `ButtonContext`, and the child's own props win. `buttonVariant` reaches `IconButton`s too when they have that style (`filled`, `tonal`, `outlined`, as `iconButtonVariant`; 2026-10-13, "Don't mix color styles in connected button groups"); `elevated` and `text` reach Buttons only.
  - **Development warnings** (2026-10-13, once per group): standard icon buttons or text buttons in a group (the spec: "Avoid using standard icon buttons or text buttons, as they have no container treatment"), counting only variants the group or the button sets (a toolbar's standard default doesn't count); and a connected group with no toggle buttons ("Avoid using a connected group when none of the buttons can be toggled").
  - **Overflow menu** (`overflow`, 2026-10-13; the spec: "Buttons at the trailing edge of the button group can be customized to collapse into an overflow menu at smaller breakpoints … Place the overflow menu at the trailing end of the group"; Compose `ButtonGroup(overflowIndicator = …)`): the group appends an icon button (`more_vert`, named and tooltipped "More options", `labels.more`) in its `ButtonGroupDefaults.OverflowIndicator` role, taking the group's icon button variant. `useOverflow` measures the items at their natural width (the group briefly becomes a `w-max` flex row with no button growing, a connected one too, all in one synchronous pass before paint), fits as many as the group's width allows with room for the indicator (Compose's measurement), and hides the rest with `data-overflow-hidden` (`display: none`, so they leave the tab order and the accessibility tree). A `ResizeObserver` on the group and its children re-measures. With `overflow` a standard group fills its container's width (`flex w-full min-w-0`) so it has a width to measure; its buttons stay at the start. The menu lists the folded buttons with their label (a Button's children, an icon button's `aria-label`) and icon; disabled ones are disabled items; toggles are checkable items (radio items in a single-selection group, checkbox items otherwise), checked from the group's selection or the button's `aria-pressed`. Only toggles (and buttons in the group's selection) are checkable: actions stay plain menu items (`menuitem`, no checked state or check) that close the menu, even when the menu is selectable because a toggle folded (2026-10-14; before, every item became checkable). Internally the overflow list uses `CheckableMenuItem` (`checkable={false}`), which `Menu.tsx` exports for the library's own menus only. Choosing an item calls `click()` on the folded button, so its own press handler, link, toggle or group selection runs (React Aria treats it as a virtual press). **Focus after a mouse choice** (2026-10-15): the menu closed with focus still on the chosen item, which then left with the menu and dropped focus on `<body>`; once the menu has closed, focus still in it (or on the page) goes back to the indicator, as after a keyboard choice. Folded buttons don't take part in the press bump. Connected corners follow the visible buttons, the indicator taking the trailing corners.
  - **Overflow menu loaded lazily** (2026-10-14): statically imported, `Menu` added about 10 kB gzipped to every `ButtonGroup`, `overflow` or not. The indicator stays a static `IconButton`, since `useOverflow` measures it in the same pass before paint; the menu is `button-group/overflow-menu-list.tsx`, rendered through `LazyOverflowMenuList` (`lazy-overflow-menu.tsx`: a cached dynamic `import()` through `onDemand`, which never suspends; it first used `React.lazy` and an empty `Suspense` fallback), the same pattern as `Menu`'s sheet. It is rendered only while at least one button is folded, and rendering it starts the load, so a group without `overflow`, or whose buttons all fit, never loads `Menu`, and a group that folds has it ready long before the indicator is pressed. If the indicator opens the menu first, nothing shows until the module arrives, then the menu opens with its first item focused, as before. A failed load is tried again when the pointer moves over the indicator, focus enters it, or the menu opens. To keep the trigger static, `MenuTrigger`, `ContextMenuTrigger` and the context they share with `Menu` live in `menu/menu-trigger.tsx` (re-exported from `Menu.tsx`, so the public API is unchanged). `SplitButton` imports only the trigger too, so its measured size no longer counts the `Menu` its users pass in and import themselves. `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails the build if `ButtonGroup` imports `Menu` statically again.
  - **Spacing** (2026-10-13, the spec's "Standard button group inner padding"; before, 12px at every size from Compose's small tokens): standard groups are 18 / 12 / 8 / 8 / 8px apart (XS–XL) so small buttons' 48px targets never overlap, the XS and S gaps growing with density (2026-10-15, see §5's control targets); connected groups are 2px apart at every size. The group's `size` (or the enclosing context's, else `sm`) picks the gap, and the root carries it as `data-size` beside `data-variant`, both public styling hooks (2026-10-24; the group has no token set, so no `data-tokens`).
  - **Keyboard:** without a selection mode the group is a React Aria `useToolbar` (role `toolbar`, or `group` inside another toolbar): Tab enters and leaves it, and the arrow keys move between its buttons ("the arrow keys to navigate between each item in the group", 2026-10-13), wrapping, with Home / End (2026-10-15, APG toolbar). A single selection is an APG radio group whose arrows select (2026-10-15, §9 Accessibility fixes). It sits in a `DomDirectionLocale`, so the arrows follow the DOM direction.
  - **Press expansion (standard groups only):** neighbours react to a pressed button (Compose `ButtonGroup`). Connected groups never do this: "Connected button groups don't add any interaction between buttons … They only affect the shape of the button being selected or activated" (2026-10-13). The pressed item grows by `expandedRatio` (0.15) of its width, and its neighbours shrink by the same amount. Each neighbour gives up at most its compression limit, which is half the space around its content (Compose: the item's end padding). Middle items split the growth across both sides, so the group's total width stays constant.
  - **How it works on the web:** the group watches its children's public `data-pressed` attribute (a MutationObserver), pins every item's `flex-basis` to its measured layout width (all measured before any is pinned, ignoring transforms such as a scaled ancestor; every item, not only the moving ones, since a content-sized `w-fit` group's width depends on how each item is sized, 2026-10-14), and animates the pressed item's and its neighbours' on the fast spatial spring (added to `container-motion`). While pinned, `min-width` is 0 (Compose forces exact widths too). Inline styles are removed once the release settles, so consumer sizing such as `flex-1` applies again: after the spring's duration, and then only once the release's `flex-basis` transitions have ended (2026-10-17; a busy main thread that started them late got them cut short, each item snapping its last 2px). Pinning and unpinning never animate (2026-10-14): the layout is the same either way, but in a connected group the switch is between `flex-basis: 0%` and a px width, which `container-motion` would transition (the items collapsed to their padding on every press, then sprang back past their width on cleanup), so the hook flushes the change and finishes its `flex-basis` transition, and unpins every item in one task; only rest → bumped → rest animates. The settle wait reads the fast spatial spring's duration token in either CSS time unit (a minified stylesheet writes `.36s`; read as 0.36ms, the cleanup cut the release short). A compressed item's content stays centred (2026-10-15): the Button's content wrapper never shrinks, so in a button narrower than its content plus padding the content overflows its padding equally on both sides instead of spilling past the trailing edge; the compression limit keeps it inside the item. A connected group is a grid at rest; while its items are pinned the hook sets `data-pinned` on the group, which makes it a flex row (`data-pinned:flex`) of the same pinned widths, so the bump animates `flex-basis` as in a standard group and the layout doesn't move at the switch (2026-10-15). `expandedRatio={0}` turns it off.
  - **Connected:** 2px gap. **They span their container** ("The connected button group should span the width of the page or surface it's placed on, increasing the button widths inside"): `flex w-full`, and the buttons share the width **equally** (2026-10-13, as Compose's connected sample gives each `Modifier.weight(1f)`; before, each grew by an equal extra share, so widths followed the labels). **Equal columns** (2026-10-15): the group is a grid of `1fr` columns (`grid grid-flow-col auto-cols-[1fr]`, `minmax(auto, 1fr)`: Tailwind's `auto-cols-fr` is `minmax(0, 1fr)`, which would squeeze a button below its minimum), not a flex row of `*:grow *:basis-0` items. With too little room a button still shrinks only to its own minimum, as before: 58px for XS and S buttons (their `min-w-[58px]`), its content for larger ones. A definite width (the default `w-full`, a fixed width) is shared equally, now truly equally (a flex basis of 0 still counted each item's padding, so a padded button got 32px more than an icon button); and a group whose width comes from its content (`w-fit`) gives every button the widest one's natural width, its content plus its full padding. A flex row couldn't size itself that way: it took the sum of the natural widths and split it equally, so a longer label rested squeezed into its padding ("Not going" beside "Going" and "Maybe" kept 8.5px of its 16px a side). Connected icon buttons take their width as a minimum (`w-auto min-w-[…]`) so the columns stretch them. `auto-cols-auto` on the group sizes the buttons by their labels again (it replaces `*:basis-auto`); a consumer `w-fit` or `max-w-*` narrows it. Corners follow the spec by size (2026-10-13; before, one set for every size from Compose): round groups keep full outer corners and inner corners of 4 / 8 / 8 / 16 / 20px; **square groups** (`shape="square"`, now honoured) give every corner that size. Pressed inner corners tighten to 4px (Compose's `PressedInnerCornerCornerSize`; the spec gives none), and to **2px at XS** (2026-10-13), whose inner corners already rest at 4px, so a press always shows a change; 2px is the one radius outside the shape scale (§4), an owner decision. Selected buttons become fully round. **Press bump:** by default a connected group doesn't change widths (the spec: they "don't affect adjacent buttons"; Compose's connected sample has no `animateWidth`). Google's Expressive showcase video does show the bump on a connected group, so `expandedRatio` opts a connected group into it (2026-10-13), as Compose allows by combining `animateWidth` with the connected shapes; the home page bento uses it. XS and S connected icon buttons are at least 48px wide ("a minimum width of 48dp"). The classes live in `components/button/connected-shapes.ts`, shared by Button and IconButton. These are logical corners, so they mirror in RTL. Children get `connected` = `leading | middle | trailing` through a per-child `ButtonContext` provider; Button and IconButton apply it in their recipes, and it replaces their normal shape classes.
  - **Selection:** `selectionMode` = `none | single | multiple` with `selectedKeys` / `defaultSelectedKeys` / `onSelectionChange` / `deselectable` (default `true`; `false` keeps one selected). Toggle buttons with a `value` join the group's React Aria toggle-group state (`ToggleGroupStateContext`, read by `ButtonBase`). Single selection renders as a `radiogroup` of `radio`s. React Aria keeps every option tabbable and adds arrow-key movement. No-op changes that React Stately reports (same keys) are not passed to `onSelectionChange`.
  - **Density** (2026-10-15, §5; the spec: "Button groups adapt to density of the buttons inside"): the group has no `density` of its own; its icon buttons follow the theme's or a `ThemeScope`'s, so the group's height follows them, and its gaps stay. Buttons (with text) have no density (a denser layout uses a smaller `size`), so a group that mixes them with icon buttons is uneven when dense. In a toolbar the group's icon buttons stay at density 0 with the toolbar's own (§5).
  - **Spec gaps (superseded 2026-10-13):** Compose only has _Small_ tokens for groups (standard gap 12px; connected gap 2px, inner corners 8/4px), which were used at every size until the spec's per-size values replaced them (Spacing and Connected above).
  - Children must be direct elements (each is wrapped in its own context provider).
- **FAB** (built, `Fab` + `ExtendedFab`): `Fab` `size` = `default | medium | large` (56 / 80 / 96px; small FAB deprecated); `ExtendedFab` `size` = `sm | md | lg` (56 / 80 / 96px tall).
  - **Shared:** `color`, `elevation`, `visible`, `appear` and `href`.
  - **Shapes and icons follow the spec tokens** (decided 2026-10-13, from the M3 Expressive spec audit; earlier revisions followed Compose's overrides): corners are large (16px) / large-increased (20px) / extra-large (28px), and icons are 24 / 28 / 32px (`FabLargeTokens.IconSize`; Compose's 36px override is not used).
  - **Extended FAB:** padding 16 / 26 / 28px; icon gap 8 / 16 / 20px (the `ExtendedFab*Tokens.IconLabelSpace` tokens, 2026-10-13); label `title-medium` / `title-large` / `headline-small`.
  - **Size guidance** (2026-10-13): the spec calls the medium FAB "most recommended", but the default `size` stays `default` (56px), matching Compose's `FloatingActionButton`; the docs recommend `size="md"` for most layouts.
  - **Elevation** (`elevation`, 2026-10-13, replacing the `lowered` boolean): `default` is level 3, hover 4, pressed 3; `lowered` is level 1, hover 2, pressed 1 (the hover level never applies while pressed, so pressing with the pointer still over the FAB drops it back to its resting level, the `PressedContainerElevation` token; 2026-10-13); `flat` is level 0 in every state, because the spec says "When nested within another component, such as the navigation rail, the FAB's resting elevation should be level 0". Rail-header examples use `flat`. There's no press morph, and no `disabled` (FABs have none in M3; the types enforce it).
  - **Colour styles:** `primary-container` (default), `secondary-container`, `tertiary-container`, `primary`, `secondary`, `tertiary`, each a role + on-role pair. Compose only ships tokens for the first two; the other four follow m3.material.io's colour styles.
  - **Tooltip:** `Fab` (and `ToolbarFab`) show their `aria-label` in a plain tooltip on hover and keyboard focus, as the spec asks of FABs on the web (2026-10-13). `tooltip={false}` turns it off, e.g. inside a consumer's own `TooltipTrigger`. `ExtendedFab` has none while expanded ("The extended FAB doesn't need a tooltip": its label is visible), but collapsed to the icon it is an icon-only FAB, so it shows its label (or `aria-label`) in the same tooltip (2026-10-13), with the same `tooltip={false}` opt-out; like an icon button's, it steps aside inside an app's own `TooltipTrigger` or `RichTooltipTrigger`. The tooltip stays mounted and is disabled while expanded (`fab/FabTooltip.tsx`, controlled so an open one closes as the label reappears), so collapsing never remounts the FAB. **Not beside a floating toolbar** (2026-10-13; "Floating toolbars can be paired with FABs, but not extended FABs"): an `ExtendedFab` inside a `FloatingToolbar` (the internal `ToolbarContext`) warns once in development. **First word of the label** (2026-10-15; "The accessibility label must include the same first word as the visible label"): an `ExtendedFab` whose `aria-label` starts with another word than its visible label (read through elements, case-insensitive) warns once in development.
  - **`expanded` (Extended FAB):** collapses to an icon-only square. The label's box animates its width in px between 0 and the label's measured width (2026-10-17, `useNaturalSize`; Compose lerps the FAB's width, `FloatingActionButton.kt`), so expanding shows the fast spatial spring's overshoot (about 10px on a large FAB), which the `0fr ↔ 1fr` grid column it used to be couldn't pass; a label that itself changes size (a web font arriving) is followed at once (`data-resized`), not sprung to. No transforms. Both directions run on the fast spatial + fast effects springs ("All component motion is driven by two tokens: expressive fast spatial and expressive fast effects", 2026-10-13). A large one's collapsed content (28 + 32 + 28px) is 8px short of its 96px minimum, so its centred icon sits 4px further in; the content takes those 4px a side as padding on the same spring, so the icon slides there with the width instead of jumping in the frame the minimum takes over (2026-10-24). The label stays in the accessible name while collapsed.
  - **Appear / disappear** (2026-10-13; the spec: "When a FAB animates on screen, it expands outward from a central point", and the FAB disappears and reappears on page and tab changes): `visible={false}` scales the FAB to 0.2 around its centre (Compose's `animateFloatingActionButton` target) on the fast spatial spring and fades it on the fast effects spring, then hides it (`visibility`) and makes it `inert`. `appear` plays the entrance on mount (`@starting-style`), so a new `key` replays it on a tab change. Reduced motion keeps only the fade. The scale is a `transform` on the root, set only while hidden or appearing: an exception to §10 rule 4 (see there).
  - **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `fab-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-fab-<colour>-container-color` and `-icon-color`, and `--md-comp-extended-fab-<colour>-container-color` and `-label-text-color`, for the six colour styles (`primary-container` … `tertiary`; the icon follows the extended FAB's label); `--md-comp-fab-container-shape`, `--md-comp-fab-medium-container-shape` and `--md-comp-fab-large-container-shape`; `--md-comp-extended-fab-{small,medium,large}-container-shape`. The extended FAB has its own M3 set (`md.comp.extended-fab.*`), so it reads its own names, not the FAB's. The roots carry `data-color` and `data-size` (public styling hooks) and `data-tokens`. Elevation stays with the `elevation` prop, and hover, focus and pressed values and sizes stay classes.

### Card (built)

- `variant` = `filled | elevated | outlined` (default `filled`, as Compose's `Card`). All have 12px corners and `on-surface` content: filled is `surface-container-highest`, elevated is `surface-container-low` at level 1, outlined is `surface` with a 1px `outline-variant` border.
- **Clipping:** cards clip content to their corners (`overflow-hidden`, as Compose clips), so media gets rounded edges; `overflow-visible` turns it off. They're `flex flex-col` with no padding, and content sets its own.
- **Three forms:**
  - **Static** (`<div>`).
  - **Pressable** (`onPress`): renders `<div role="button">` through `ButtonBase`'s `as: 'div'` (named `elementType` until 2026-10-17), because a `<button>` may only contain phrasing content and cards hold headings, media and blocks.
  - **Link** (`href`, `<a>`).
- **Interactive cards** fill their container's width (`w-full`), get state layers and the focus ring, and are named by their content: all their visible text, in reading order, headline first (2026-10-17). The documented pattern had named them by the headline alone (`aria-labelledby`), which left the rest of their text out of the name: axe's `label-content-name-mismatch` (WCAG 2.5.3, whose Understanding document asks for the visible text in the name, starting with the label) flagged every example. A native link wrapping a card, and other libraries' pressable card areas, are named the same way; a card whose text is too long to read as a name is better as a static card with its own link or button. In development they warn if they contain buttons, links or fields, since interactive content can't nest; use a static card with its own actions instead.
- **States follow the spec's state list with the card token values** (2026-10-13; this replaces following Compose's code, which kept outlined cards flat on hover):
  - Elevation, rest / hover / focus and press / dragged: filled 0 / 1 / 0 / 3, elevated 1 / 2 / 1 / 4, outlined 0 / 1 / 0 / 3. Hover applies only while the card isn't pressed, keyboard-focused or dragged, so exactly one rule wins.
  - A keyboard-focused outlined card's border is `on-surface` (`FocusOutlineColor`).
  - **Dragged** ("When moving a card, increase its elevation"): `dragged` sets `data-dragged`, on static and interactive cards alike; interactive cards also show the 16% dragged state layer. The app's drag and drop code sets it. An interactive card passes it to `ButtonBase`'s `dragged` input (2026-10-13; before, the card set the attribute in a layout effect because `ButtonBase` took no dragged state), and a static card renders the attribute itself.
  - Disabled: filled `surface-variant` 38% over its container (`color-mix`), elevated `surface` 38% over `surface-container-low` (`DisabledContainerOpacity`, 2026-10-13; it was solid `surface` before), the outlined border `outline` 12% (`DisabledOutlineOpacity`, over the card's own `surface`). Content is `on-surface` 38%.
- No anatomy sub-components (headline, media, actions) in v1. Compose doesn't ship them, and layout utilities cover them.
- **Forced colours** (2026-10-15): forced colours drop the container colour and the shadow, so every card takes a 1px `CanvasText` border there (`GrayText` disabled); filled and elevated cards had no edge.
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `card-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-{filled,elevated,outlined}-card-container-color` and `-container-shape`, `--md-comp-elevated-card-container-elevation` and `--md-comp-outlined-card-outline-color`. Hover, focus, dragged and disabled values stay classes.

### TextField (built)

- `variant` = `filled | outlined`, a floating `label`, `supportingText` (with `supportingTextOnFocus`), `errorMessage` / `invalid` / `validate` (React Aria `useTextField`), `required` (`*` in the accessible name, `aria-required`), `disabled`, `readOnly` (`data-readonly`), `leadingIcon`, `trailingIcon` (may be an `IconButton`), `errorIcon`, `prefix` / `prefixLabel`, `suffix` / `suffixLabel`, `maxLength` with a `count/max` counter, `multiline` (a fixed-height text area of `rows` lines, 4 by default, that scrolls) with `autoGrow` (grows from `rows` up to `maxRows`, then scrolls), and `labels` (`error`, `characterCount`). It's controlled or uncontrolled through `value` / `defaultValue` / `onChange(value)`.
- **Accessibility from the M3 spec** (2026-10-13; the text field accessibility page wins over our earlier choices):
  - The required `*` is part of the label's text, so the accessible name is "Email \*" ("The accessibility label must include the asterisk"). Only the copy in the outlined notch is hidden, with its fieldset.
  - `ErrorText` has the `alert` role ("When there is an error, "alert" is applied to the role"), and stays in `aria-describedby` after the supporting text.
  - The counter is read as "Character count: 12 of 80" (`labels.characterCount`): its digits are hidden and its id joins the input's description. It used to be hidden from assistive tech.
  - The prefix and suffix have ids and join the description, read as `prefixLabel` / `suffixLabel` ("US dollars" for "$") when given.
  - An invalid field shows an error icon (Material Symbols `error`, role `img`, named "Error" by `labels.error`) in the trailing slot, unless a `trailingIcon` holds it; `errorIcon` replaces it, `false` turns it off. M3 "strongly recommend[s]" it.
  - Supporting text and the counter take the `error` colour in every error state, with or without a message (Compose's `ErrorSupportingColor`).
- **Text area** (2026-10-13): M3 says text areas, "a fixed height and scroll vertically", "should be used instead of multi-line fields on the web". So `multiline` is now a fixed-height text area (4 rows by default), and the growing field is opt-in with `autoGrow`. Before, `multiline` grew without limit.
- **Read-only:** M3 asks for it to be "clearly labeled as read-only"; the field looks like a regular one, gets `data-readonly`, and the docs put "Read-only" in its supporting text.
- **Naming:** a `label` **or** `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` is required by the types.
- **Where props go:** `ref`, `className`, `style` and the root's DOM attributes (`data-*`, `aria-*` other than the naming and widget ones, `title`, pointer events: `splitRootProps` with `wrapper`, 2026-10-17) go on the root; `inputRef`, the naming attributes, the widget attributes (`aria-controls`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-invalid` …), focus, key and input events and every other attribute go to the input.
- **Values from Compose** (`TextFieldImpl.kt` + tokens):
  - 56px minimum height, 280px default width (`max-w-full`).
  - 16px padding; next to an icon it drops to 4px, because icons sit in 48px boxes.
  - Filled with a label: 8px padding, label floated 8px from the top. 4px above supporting text, 2px around affixes.
  - Filled has `surface-container-highest` with an `on-surface-variant` indicator (2px `primary` on focus). Outlined has a 1px `outline` border, 2px `primary` on focus, and the label centred on the border at 16px inside a notch with 4px padding.
  - With a leading icon, an outlined label moves from after the icon to 16px when it floats, while a filled label stays aligned with the text.
  - Hovered labels stay `on-surface-variant` on filled fields and darken to `on-surface` on outlined ones. Error-hover switches to `on-error-container`.
  - The label floats on the fast spatial spring and recolours on fast effects. The motion page says the web text field uses Standard easing; it compares easing sets rather than springs, so the label keeps the active scheme's spring (2026-10-13) rather than adding scheme-independent system tokens.
- **One state:** colours follow a single computed `data-field-state` (`disabled > error-focus > error-hover > error > focus > hover > rest`), so the styles of each part never compete on CSS order. `data-focused` / `data-hovered` / `data-invalid` / `data-disabled` / `data-floated` are exposed too.
- **Floating label:** an absolutely positioned `<label>` that transitions `top`, `inset-inline-start` and the type-scale properties, so it needs no transforms and mirrors in RTL. It also floats for browser autofill (`:has(:autofill)`), since autofilled values are hidden from JavaScript until the user interacts.
- **Outlined notch:** a hidden `fieldset`/`legend` carrying the label text, so the gap matches the label exactly. The outline is shifted up 8px only when there is a label. The legend is a one-column grid that opens from `0fr` to `1fr` of the label's own width on the label's fast spatial spring, so the gap keeps pace with the label (Compose sizes the cut-out from the moving label; 2026-10-13). It used to open `max-width` to 100% on effects-fast, fully open while the label had barely moved.
- **Timing** (Compose, 2026-10-13): the indicator changes thickness on the fast spatial spring and colour on the fast effects spring; the placeholder fades in on the fast effects spring as the label floats. The outline changes thickness on the fast effects spring (2026-10-15): a browser draws a border in whole device pixels, so a spring that overshoots 2px drew 2 → 1 → 2px for about 100ms while settling.
- **Errors:** the error message replaces the supporting text visually, but the supporting text stays in the accessible description (`sr-only`).
- With a label, the placeholder, prefix and suffix appear only once the label floats (Compose). The prefix and suffix fade in on the fast effects spring (M3 motion: opacity uses the effects springs; Compose's `affixOpacity`, 2026-10-13); they used Tailwind's default 150ms ease.
- Pressing the container focuses the input (not when pressing an icon button).
- **Not in v1:** composable TextField parts (the doc's "two API levels"); only the batteries-included form is exported, built from the Field primitives.
- **Density** (2026-10-15, §5): `density` (or the theme's) takes 4px a step off the container (56 → 44px) and 2px off the value's padding above and below (filled with a label: 24 / 8px → 18 / 2px; otherwise 16px → 10px); the resting label moves up 2px a step to stay centred and a filled field's floated label too (8 → 2px from the top), so it never meets the value; the outlined label stays on the border. Icon boxes are at most as tall as the container. Text areas keep their rows and lose the same padding. **Below 52px a filled field hides its floated label** (2026-10-15, as MDC's text field: `$minimum-height-for-filled-label: 52px`): at -2 and -3 the floated label touched the value, so once it floats it fades out (on the fast effects spring) and the value is centred as in a field without a label (16px less 2px a step above and below). The resting label still shows in an empty field and still names the input. CSS alone switches it: `--m3-field-label-room` is a clamped step of the density (1 down to -1, 0 from -2). Select and Autocomplete share it through `field-shell.tsx`.
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `text-field-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-filled-text-field-*` (container colour and shape, active indicator, focused active indicator) and `--md-comp-outlined-text-field-*` (container shape, outline, hovered and focused outline), plus for both the label, focused label, input text, caret, supporting text and leading and trailing icon colours (22). The root carries `data-tokens="md-comp-text-field"` and `data-variant`, and Select and Autocomplete read the same set through `field-shell.tsx` (M3's separate `filled-select` / `*-autocomplete` sets aren't exposed). Error, disabled and the other hover colours stay classes.

### Selection controls (built: Checkbox, RadioGroup + Radio, Switch)

- **Shared anatomy:** the `SelectionControl` primitive + `selectionControlStyles`.
  - A root `<label>` (`ref`, `className`, `style` and its DOM attributes: `data-*`, `aria-*`, `title`, pointer events) with a visually hidden native input (`inputRef`) from React Aria (`useCheckbox` / `useRadio` / `useSwitch`), so forms, labels and assistive tech work natively.
  - A control holding the focus ring and a 48px touch target, and the label text (`text-body-large`).
  - `kind: 'circle'` (Checkbox, Radio) makes the control the 40px state-layer circle: `on-surface` unselected, `primary` selected, inverted while pressed, `error` when invalid (M3 spec; this token set has no state-layer colours).
  - `kind: 'track'` (Switch) makes the control the track, with a state layer that follows the thumb.
  - The types require a label or an accessible name. The state is `checked` / `defaultChecked` / `onCheckedChange` (Checkbox, Switch; HTML's and Compose's names, 2026-10-17, replacing `selected`) or the group's `value` / `defaultValue` / `onChange` (RadioGroup). Roots carry `data-checked` (a checkbox's only while it shows the check; `data-indeterminate` for the dash), as do a radio's and a list item's control, matching HTML's `:checked`; toggle buttons, chips and navigation items keep `selected` / `data-selected` (`aria-pressed` / `aria-selected`). The drawings style the checked state through the `control-checked:` custom variant (`utilities.css`: the `group/control` ancestor is `data-checked` or `data-indeterminate`), which sorts after Tailwind's `group-data-*` variants, so checked colours still win over hovered, focused, pressed and invalid ones at equal specificity, as they did when the attribute was `data-selected` (alphabetically after them).
- **Checkbox:** Compose's **M3 styling**, i.e. `ComposeMaterial3Flags.isCheckboxStylingFixEnabled = true`. Compose defaults that flag to false, which keeps M2 styling.
  - Geometry: an 18px box with 2px corners and a 2px outline; check path (0.25, 0.5) → (0.4, 0.65) → (0.75, 0.3), 2px square cap.
  - Outline (CheckboxTokens, 2026-10-13): unselected `on-surface-variant`, darkening to `on-surface` on hover, focus and press (`Unselected{Hover,Focus,Pressed}OutlineColor`), and `error` when invalid, pressed included (stacked variant). A checked or indeterminate box has **no outline** (`Selected*OutlineWidth = 0`): its border turns transparent and the fill shows through, so an invalid one is a plain `error` box. Until then the outline stayed `on-surface-variant` in every state and a selected box kept a `primary` border, which ringed an invalid box's `error` fill.
  - Motion: the check draws on default spatial and snaps away 100ms after unchecking; the box fills on default effects and empties on fast effects.
  - `indeterminate` draws a dash path. **Deviation:** Compose morphs the check into the dash, but CSS can't animate a path shape in Safari, so the dash draws on its own.
  - Error uses `error` / `on-error`. Disabled checked is `on-surface` 38% with a `surface` check.
- **Radio:** a 20px ring (2px). The dot is 10px and grows on fast spatial; colour changes on default effects.
  - The unselected ring darkens to `on-surface` on hover, focus and press. Disabled is `on-surface` 38%, which also overrides the selected colour (stacked variant).
  - M3 radios have **no error colour**; an invalid `RadioGroup` shows its error text.
  - `RadioGroup` provides the state, arrow keys, label (`text-title-small`), supporting/error text and orientation. A `Radio` outside a group throws.
- **Switch:** a 52×32px track (2px outline).
  - Thumb geometry is computed like Compose's `ThumbNode`: 16px off, 24px on or with an icon, 28px pressed, centred 16 / 36px from the start. It's passed as `--m3-thumb-size` / `--m3-thumb-center`, so no CSS state selectors compete.
  - Pressing snaps the thumb; release animates `width` / `height` / `top` / `inset-inline-start` on fast spatial. Mirrors in RTL.
  - Hover, focus and press change the thumb to `on-surface-variant` (off) / `primary-container` (on). Disabled colours are Compose's composites over `surface` (`color-mix`).
  - `icons` shows the default check / close icons; `checkedIcon` / `uncheckedIcon` override them (named after `checked`, 2026-10-17; were `selectedIcon` / `unselectedIcon`). The focus ring surrounds the track.
  - **Enter toggles it, as Space does** (M3 spec, keyboard table; 2026-10-13): browsers toggle a checkbox input on Space only, so the switch handles Enter itself (the thumb grows on key down, the switch toggles on key up).
  - **The thumb can be dragged** (M3 spec, "Touch: Tap, Drag"; 2026-10-13): it follows the pointer between its off and on centres (mirrored in RTL, `data-dragged` while it moves) and releasing sets the switch from the half it's in; a tap still toggles. The track is `touch-action: pan-y`, and the drag uses window pointer listeners because React Aria `useMove` stops the pointer-down the label's press handling needs. `SelectionControl` takes `controlProps` for these handlers.
- **Density** (2026-10-15, §5): Checkbox, RadioGroup, Radio and Switch take `density` (or the theme's). The circle control is 40px less 4px a step (28px at -3) with the ripple scaled to match; the switch's state layer 40px less 4px a step, at least 32px (`--m3-switch-layer`), the track unchanged. `RadioGroup` adds 2px a step between rows. Targets stay 48px.
- **The hidden input never scrolls the page** (2026-10-15): React Aria focuses the input with a plain `focus()` when its label is pressed, and Chrome scrolled the 1px input into view, so a tap on the visible part of a control whose centre was above the window jumped the page about 430px. `preventHiddenInputScroll` (`primitives/hidden-input-focus.ts`) gives the input its own `focus` (defined, not assigned: test tooling can make the prototype's `focus` an accessor whose setter would replace it for every element) that defaults to `preventScroll: true` and calls the prototype's current `focus`; `SelectionControl` and List's control items use it. Tab focus is the browser's own and still scrolls. Vitest checks the options; `e2e/selection-control-scroll.spec.ts` taps each control and list row partly above a phone window.
- **Invalid wins** (2026-10-15): an invalid checkbox's state layer is `error` selected or not, pressed or not; the invalid rules are stacked (`data-invalid:data-checked:` and `data-invalid:data-indeterminate:`, `data-invalid:data-pressed:`, both) so they beat the selected and pressed colours on specificity (it turned `primary` / `on-surface`).
- **`RadioGroup` follows the laid-out direction** (2026-10-15): it is wrapped in `DomDirectionLocale`, as Slider, List and ButtonGroup are, so ← / → in a horizontal group inside an RTL region move the right way under an LTR locale.
- **Forced colours** (2026-10-15): the box, ring and thumb take system colours with `forced-color-adjust: none` (forced colours drop backgrounds, so the radio dot and the switch thumb vanished): checkbox `CanvasText` (a selected box filled, its mark `Canvas`), radio ring and dot `CanvasText`, switch an outlined `Canvas` track with a `CanvasText` thumb off and a `Highlight` track with a `HighlightText` thumb on; disabled parts are `GrayText`. The rules are `!important` inside the forced-colours media query, so they beat the stacked state rules. List items draw the same parts and inherit them. `e2e/forced-colors.spec.ts` covers these and the other controls and containers.
- **No CheckboxGroup** in v1. Compose has none, and a fieldset of Checkboxes with `name` covers forms.
- `splitDataAttributes` (utils) routes `data-*` to the root for components whose remaining props go to a React Aria hook (TextField, the selection controls). Those hooks forward only the attributes they know, so consumer `data-*` attributes were being dropped.
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `checkbox-tokens.ts`, `radio-tokens.ts`, `switch-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-checkbox-{selected-container-color, selected-icon-color, unselected-outline-color}`, `--md-comp-radio-button-{selected,unselected}-icon-color` and `--md-comp-switch-{selected,unselected}-{track,handle,icon}-color` with `--md-comp-switch-unselected-track-outline-color`. The drawn parts that List also renders (`checkbox-box.tsx`, `radio-ring.tsx`, `switch-thumb.tsx`) carry `data-tokens` themselves, and each control's root carries it with its state. List draws its own switch track, which carries `data-tokens` too, so the track's classes read the private variables with no fallback, like every other token. Hover, focus, pressed, error and disabled values stay classes.

### Dialog (built; a component, not a composite)

- `DialogTrigger` (trigger + dialog, `open` / `defaultOpen` / `onOpenChange`) or a standalone controlled `Dialog`. Flat parts: `DialogTitle`, `DialogContent`, `DialogActions`, and `DialogHeader` for dialogs that can be full screen. Children may be `({ close }) => …`. `icon` centres the title. `closedBy` and `placement` are configurable; `instant` (named `quick` until 2026-10-17, now as `Menu`'s), `onOpened` and `onClosed` (2026-10-14) turn the motion off and report its end, and `transition` (`reveal` | `scale`) picks the M3 motion or the original pop.
- **API against the references** (2026-10-14): Compose `AlertDialog` / `BasicAlertDialog` / `Dialog` + `DialogProperties`, Material Web `md-dialog` and the M3 spec's anatomy. Kept as they were: `onDismissRequest` → `onOpenChange`; `icon`; `title` / `text` / `confirmButton` + `dismissButton` → `DialogTitle` / `DialogContent` / `DialogActions`; `shape`, `containerColor`, `iconContentColor`, `titleContentColor`, `textContentColor`, `tonalElevation` → the `--md-comp-dialog-*` tokens and `classNames.panel`; `dismissOnClickOutside` and `dismissOnBackPress` → `closedBy` (HTML's `closedby`, 2026-10-17: `any`, `closerequest`, `none`); `md-dialog` `open` → `open` / `defaultOpen`; `type="alert"` → `role="alertdialog"` (the default); `show()` / `close()` → `open` / the render prop's `close`; its `open` / `close` events → `onOpenChange`; the spec's divider, scrolling content and full-screen type. Added: `instant` (Material Web's `quick`), `onOpened`, `onClosed`. Left out: `DialogProperties.securePolicy` and `decorFitsSystemWindows` (Android window flags), `usePlatformDefaultWidth` (the panel's 280–560px is the default width, and `classNames.panel` or `fullScreen` change it), `windowTitle` (`aria-label`), `md-dialog`'s `returnValue` (a `<form method="dialog">` pattern; each action's own handler says what was chosen), `noFocusTrap` (a modal dialog hides and makes the page inert, so focus has nowhere else to go), the cancelable `cancel` event (a controlled `open` keeps the dialog open by not closing it in `onOpenChange`, as the discard-confirmation example does) and `getOpenAnimation` / `getCloseAnimation` (the motion comes from the spring tokens, which a `ThemeScope` or `data-motion` changes).
- **Role** (2026-10-13, the spec: "On web, basic dialogs should have the alert dialog role"): `role` defaults to `alertdialog` for a basic dialog and to `dialog` when `fullScreen` isn't `never`. An alert dialog is described by its `DialogContent` (React Aria's `contentProps`; the reference is left out when there's no content) and, as before, isn't dismissed by pressing outside, which fits "Basic dialogs require a person to take action before it will close". `role="dialog"` or `dismissable` restores the press-outside dismissal.
- **Initial focus** (2026-10-13, the spec: "focus should automatically land on the first interactive element within the dialog"): `useDialog` focuses the panel unless something inside took focus (`autoFocus`); an effect then moves focus from the panel to its first tabbable element (`utils/focus.ts` `focusFirstInteractive`, shared with the modal sheets since 2026-10-15), or, when that element is an action, to the least destructive action (§6 and 2026-10-15 above). A dialog with no controls keeps focus on the panel.
- **Placement** (2026-10-13, the spec: basic dialogs are centred by default, "Their position can be overridden", in an area "that respects a 56dp margin from the edges of the screen"): `placement` = `center | top | bottom | start | end` aligns the panel in the positioner, which pads 24px on compact windows and 56px from the medium breakpoint (it was 24px everywhere). **Custom position** (2026-10-13, the spec: dialogs can be "custom-positioned anywhere on larger screens"): `classNames.positioner` is merged (`cn()`) over the positioner's classes, so `items-*` / `justify-*` place the dialog anywhere while the padding keeps the margin; the defaults are unchanged. The §10 override matrix runs on it (`PositionerOverride` story): with `fixed`, `absolute`, `sticky`, `static`, overflow, `w-full` or a transform on the positioner, the panel keeps its size, corners, motion and focus ring, the scrim covers the window and pressing outside still dismisses.
- **Built on** `Overlay` + React Aria `useModalOverlay` (dismissal, scroll lock, hiding the rest of the page) + `useDialog` (labelling, initial focus). Focus is contained and returns to the trigger. React Aria sets no `aria-haspopup` for dialog triggers; it does set `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`.
- **Values** (Compose `AlertDialog.kt`, `DialogTokens`):
  - Size: 280–560px wide, 24px padding, 28px corners, `surface-container-high`, level 3. The minimum is `min(280px, 100vw − 48px)` on the panel (2026-10-13): it was `min(280px, 100%)`, whose percentage had no definite base in the content-sized motion wrapper, so short dialogs measured 231–255px.
  - Level 3 stays (2026-10-13): `DialogTokens.ContainerElevation` is level 3 and the spec's dialog pages state no elevation, though Compose's `AlertDialogDefaults.TonalElevation` is 0dp.
  - Text: a 24px `secondary` icon, a `headline-small` title and `body-medium` `on-surface-variant` text.
  - Spacing: 16px below the icon and the title, 24px below the text, 8px between actions.
- **Forced colours** (2026-10-15): Windows high contrast replaces the panel's fill with the page's `Canvas` and drops its shadow, so a basic dialog's text floated on the page. The panel draws a 1px `CanvasText` outline there (`forced-colors:` classes, invisible otherwise).
  - Stacked actions put the confirm action (last) **on top**, matching Compose's flipped `FlowRow` (`flex-wrap-reverse`).
  - **At most two actions** (2026-10-13, the spec: a single action only to acknowledge, at most two, no third "Learn more"): `DialogActions` warns once per instance, in development only, when it holds more than two elements (a fragment's children are counted, not the fragment; `null` and `false` aren't counted).
  - Long content scrolls inside the panel. The scrim is `scrim` at 32%.
  - **Scrolling content and the keyboard** (2026-10-13, the spec: assistive tech must be able to "Scroll the dialog to access all of its contents"): while `DialogContent` overflows (a `ResizeObserver` on it and its children) it is `tabIndex=0` with `data-scrollable` and the `focus-ring`; React Aria's focus containment skipped it before. Initial focus skips it (`firstTabbable(panel, skip)`) and still lands on the first control.
  - **Dividers** (2026-10-13, the spec's anatomy: "Divider (optional)", 1dp): `DialogContent dividers` draws 1px `outline-variant` lines across the panel's full width above and below the content, 8px from it, keeping 24px to the actions. Opt-in rather than automatic, as the spec only marks them optional; basic dialogs only (from 600px for `compact`).
  - **Initial focus skips unrendered elements** (2026-10-13): `firstTabbable` leaves out elements whose `checkVisibility()` is false, so a `compact` dialog shown as a basic dialog no longer tries to focus the header's `medium:hidden` close button (focus stayed on the panel).
- **Motion (not from Compose,** which uses platform window animations). Rebuilt 2026-10-14 on the M3 dialog motion as Material Web encodes it (`dialog/internal/animations.ts`), mapped onto the M3 Expressive springs (spatial for movement and size, effects for opacity); 2026-10-13 it was a clip reveal and a fade only (no slide, no stagger), and before that a scale from 90%. Everything runs on CSS transitions (`@starting-style` entering, `data-exiting` leaving) on a library-owned wrapper (`motion`) and the panel's children, so any children composition takes part. Reference phase → ours:
  - **Slide:** the dialog moves 50px (`translateY(-50px)` → 0 over 500ms emphasized; back over 150ms emphasized-accelerate) → `translate: var(--m3-dialog-slide)` on the spatial default spring entering and the fast **effects** spring leaving. **Exit on the reference's own timing** (2026-10-14): leaving uses Material Web's close timing on M3's easing and duration tokens rather than springs: the slide and the shrink run 150ms (`--md-sys-motion-duration-short-3`) on `--md-sys-motion-easing-emphasized-accelerate`, the parts fade out over 100ms (`short-2`, linear), the container fades from 100ms to 150ms (`short-1` after `short-2`, linear) and the scrim fades out over 150ms (linear). Emphasized-accelerate starts slowly, so the dialog is still moving and shrinking on every frame until it has faded, and it never overshoots. Springs didn't fit: the expressive fast spatial spring overshot (-54.6px, ~29% shown) while visible, and the critically damped fast effects spring (tried first) finished the shrink by ~130ms, leaving a still 35% box fading out. It applies to every placement and both picker dialogs; under reduced motion the fade alone takes 150ms with no delay. The full-screen slide keeps its fast effects spring for opacity and the 48px slide: measured, its fade (0.10 at 64ms, 0.02 at 96ms) ends while the slide is still moving (43 → 47px), so it shows no still box. **Direction-aware:** toward the edge the dialog sits against: up for `center` and `top` (the reference), down for `bottom`, toward the start or end edge along the x axis for `start` / `end` (flipped with `rtl:`). The wrapper's `translate` is `none` at rest, so it is a containing block for fixed children only while moving.
  - **Container grow:** the container grows from the same edge, on the same springs, as a `clip-path: inset(… round r)` on the wrapper: `--m3-dialog-enter-clip` (the full inset on the growing side) → `--m3-dialog-open-clip` (`inset(-32px round r + 32px)`, 32px outside the panel so the shadow shows) → `--m3-dialog-exit-clip` (35% of the panel) leaving. The clip's sides open from the panel's edges to the 32px margin together with its radius (`--m3-dialog-corner`, the container-shape token, so a picker dialog's own token), so the corners stay concentric with the panel's and the growing edge keeps the panel's 28px corners on every frame. **From 0, as the spec says** (decided by frame-by-frame measurement, 2026-10-14): Material Web grows from 35% because its container is a `::before` background that doesn't clip the text, which could show over the scrim before the container reached it; ours clips its parts, so nothing is ever painted outside the container. Measured on the basic story (180px panel): the headline is 96% under the container at its first visible frame (56ms) and fully from 64ms; the supporting text shows at 11–45% opacity while the edge passes over it (64–88ms); the actions are fully covered (from 120ms) before they start to fade in (152ms).
  - **Fades:** the container 0 → 1 (50ms linear) → the wrapper's opacity on the fast effects spring; the headline and content (0 until 20% of 250ms, then 1) → every panel child but the actions fades on the default effects spring after 50ms (`--md-sys-motion-duration-short-1`); the actions (0 until 50% of 300ms) → `data-slot="actions"` children (`DialogActions`, a picker dialog's actions row, or a custom child marked so) on the fast effects spring after 150ms (`short-3`). Leaving, all parts fade out at once over 100ms (linear, as the reference), and the container fades from 100ms to 150ms (the reference's 50ms after 100ms); its `transitionend` ends presence at 150ms. The unmount waits for that event, not a timer; `usePresence`'s 800ms fallback (for a `transitionend` that never comes) is longer than any token-built exit (the reveal exit's 150ms; the slowest spring token, the expressive slow spatial at 600ms, plus a 100ms delay), which a unit test checks against the motion tokens, so it can't cut a fade short. With `instant` there is no exit to wait for (`usePresence(…, animated = false)`), and under reduced motion the wrapper's 150ms fade, with no delay, ends it.
  - **Scrim:** 0 → 32% over 500ms linear → the slow effects spring; out over 150ms linear, as the reference (`transition="scale"` keeps its fast effects spring).
  - **Reduced motion** keeps only the fades of the wrapper and the scrim: no slide, the clip stays open entering and leaving, the parts keep full opacity, and the exit fade doesn't wait.
  - **`instant`** (Material Web's `quick`): `transition-property: none !important` on the scrim, wrapper, panel and parts; `usePresence(…, animated = false)` (a new optional argument of the public primitive) unmounts the dialog as soon as it closes instead of waiting for a `transitionend` that never comes.
  - **`onOpened` / `onClosed`** (Material Web's `opened` / `closed` events): `onOpened` waits for every CSS transition on the scrim, the wrapper, the panel and its parts to finish (read on the first frame; a cut-short transition never finishes, so a dialog closed while entering never reports opening); `onClosed` fires when presence ends.
  - **`transition="scale"`** (2026-10-14, an extension beyond the M3 motion; the default is `reveal`, the motion above): the library's original 0.1.0 pop, kept as an opt-in. The scrim fades on the default effects spring; the dialog fades in (default effects) while it scales up from 90% (default spatial), and fades out (fast effects) while it scales to 95% (fast spatial); no clip, no slide, no staggered parts (the `parts` classes aren't applied). It scales from the edge the dialog sits against (`--m3-dialog-scale-origin`, set by `placement`: the centre for `center`, the top or bottom edge, the start or end edge flipped with `rtl:`). Reduced motion keeps only the fades, `instant` turns it off, `onOpened` / `onClosed` work the same, and full-screen dialogs (`always`, `compact` below 600px) ignore it. `PickerDialog` passes its own `transition` through (default `reveal`). Named `transition` like the icon buttons' `iconTransition` (a choice of how something changes), not `motion`, which already names the theme's motion scheme (`data-motion`, `ThemeProvider motion`).
  - Tested frame by frame (`e2e/dialog-motion.spec.ts`, `e2e/motion-frames.ts`): every transition is paused as the motion starts and stepped through `currentTime`, sampling the translate, clip and opacity of the wrapper, scrim and parts on 16ms frames, for each placement (and RTL), custom children, the icon, both picker dialogs, full screen and reduced motion.
- **Full screen** (`fullScreen` = `never | always | compact`; `compact`, the spec's recommendation, applies below 600px only): the panel fills the window on `surface-container-high` (2026-10-13, the spec's full-screen colour roles; it was `surface`) with no corners, elevation or padding. `DialogHeader` is a **56px** header (the spec; it was 64px) with the close icon button, a `title-large` headline and the confirming `action`; `divider` adds a 1px `outline-variant` line under it, and `closeDisabled` disables the close button (2026-10-24, for a confirming action under way; it doesn't follow `closedBy`, which is about light dismissal, so an app pairs it with `closedBy="none"`). With a header action the `DialogActions` are hidden; without one they form the spec's **56px bottom action bar** (the header reports its action through context). The panel slides up 48px and fades on the **slow** springs (the spec's speed for full-screen animations), and slides back down as it fades out on the fast springs with no wait; its parts don't stagger. Checked 2026-10-14: the spec's only full-screen motion is the container transform from a FAB (`origin`); Material Web has no full-screen dialog and Compose leaves it to the platform window, so the slide stays, along the y axis as the transitions page asks ("expand and collapse along the x or y axis", no scale). 2026-10-13, from the spec's full-screen measurements and colour roles: the content is padded **24px at the top** as well as the sides (it started flush under the header), and the close icon is **`on-surface`** like the headline (it was the standard icon button's `on-surface-variant`; set through `--md-comp-icon-button-icon-color` on the `close` slot). `always` fills wide windows too; the docs point to `compact`, since the spec keeps full-screen dialogs to compact windows, instead of capping the width at 560px.
- **Moved to the components layer:** the doc listed Dialog as a composite, but its core is the `Overlay` primitive and React Aria hooks, which composites may not import. Consumers' buttons go in `DialogActions`.
