React Aria's `useOption` names it by the headline and describes it by the supporting text, which leaves visible text out of the name: axe's `label-content-name-mismatch` (WCAG 2.5.3) flagged every option with supporting text. `test/label-in-name.ts` runs that experimental rule in Vitest. Menu items follow the same rule (see Menu).
- **Selection cue:** selected items show a 20px check at their end besides the `secondary-container` colour ("don't rely on color as the only visual cue"); `selectionIndicator={false}` removes it.
- **Values** (Compose `ListItem.kt` interactive overloads, `ListItemDefaults`, `ListTokens`):
  - Padding 16px start / end, 10px top / bottom; 12px between leading content, the text and trailing content. **The 12px belongs to the leading and trailing cells** (their `me-[12px]` / `ms-[12px]`; the grid has no column gap), which render only when there's content, so a text-only item's text spans 16px to 16px (2026-10-13, the spec's "label left padding 16dp" for text-only and one-sided items; the grid's gap had kept 12px beside empty columns). A tree row's trailing cell exists only for a selection check or a chevron.
  - Heights: at least 56px (one line), 72px (overline or supporting text) and 88px (both). Three-line items align to the top, the others to the centre (Compose's breakpoint). Compose also treats a multi-line supporting text as three lines; that needs measuring and isn't done.
  - **Density** (2026-10-15, §5): `List` takes `density` (or the theme's) on its root: 2px a step off the padding above and below (10 → 4px) and 4px off the minimum heights (56 → 44px, 72 → 60px, 88 → 76px), so items that media make taller lose 4px too; subheaders 48 → 36px; the expand chevron's target is at most a one-line item tall.
  - Type and colour: headline `body-large` `on-surface`; supporting `body-medium`, overline `label-small`, leading content (`title-medium` for avatar letters, 20px icons: the Expressive `ItemLeadingIconExpressiveSize`) and trailing content (`label-small` text, 20px icons) `on-surface-variant`. Selected items are `secondary-container` with `on-secondary-container` content; disabled content `on-surface` 38%. Segmented items are `surface` (`ItemSegmentedContainerColor`), so segmented lists usually sit on a `surface-container` background. **Deviation:** standard items are transparent rather than `ItemContainerColor` (`surface`), so a list takes the colour of its sheet, card or pane; in Compose consumers override the colour for that.
  - **Shapes:** Compose's precedence is pressed, then selected or focused (all 16px), then hovered (12px), else 4px. They're computed into one `data-shape` (`rest | hovered | active`), so no two rules compete, and morph on the fast spatial spring while colours change on the default effects spring. Segmented lists are 2px apart and round the outer corners of the first and last items to 16px at rest (`data-position`), as `segmentedShapes` does. Static items in a standard list have no corners.
- **Media** (Expressive tokens): `ListItemImage` 56px with 8px corners (`ItemLeadingImageExpressiveShape`), `ListItemVideo` 100 × 56px or 114 × 64px (large) with 8px corners, and `ListItemAvatar` (the spec's leading avatar): 40px, round, `primary-container` / `on-primary-container`, `title-medium` initials (hidden from assistive tech) or a photo cropped to fill.
- **Shared drawn parts** (2026-10-14): checkbox, radio and switch items draw the controls' own box, ring and thumb, which live in small modules the controls import too: `checkbox/checkbox-box.tsx` (`CheckboxBox`), `radio/radio-ring.tsx` (`RadioRing`) and `switch/switch-thumb.tsx` (`SwitchThumb`, its icons and `thumbGeometry`). `List` imported them from `Checkbox.tsx`, `RadioGroup.tsx` and `Switch.tsx`, which brought each control's whole module (its hooks, drag handling and field parts): about 4 kB gzipped more for every list. They are internal, so the public API is unchanged, and nothing is loaded lazily. `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails the build if `List` imports one of the controls' modules again.
- **Not in v1:** drag-to-reorder and its dragged state (`ReorderListTokens`, `ItemDraggedContainerExpressiveShape`; deferred until reordering lands) and swipe-to-reveal (Android Views only in the spec).
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `list-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-list-list-item-{container-color, segmented-container-color, label-text-color, supporting-text-color, overline-color, leading-icon-color, trailing-icon-color, selected-container-color}`. A standard item stays see-through by default. The root and each item carry `data-tokens` and `data-variant`; selected content, state and shape values stay classes.

### Sliders (built, Tier 3)

- `Slider` (`value` / `defaultValue` / `onChange` / `onChangeEnd`, `centered`, `leadingIcon`, `trailingIcon`) and `RangeSlider` (`[start, end]`, `labels` = `start | end`, the thumbs' names), sharing `size` = `xs | sm | md | lg | xl`, `orientation`, `min` / `max` / `step` (0–100 by 1; HTML's names, 2026-10-17, mapped to React Aria's `minValue` / `maxValue` inside), `ticks`, `disabled`, `valueLabel`, `formatOptions` and `name`. Built on React Aria `useSlider` / `useSliderThumb` (hidden range inputs, arrows, Page Up / Down, Home / End), following the DOM direction via `DomDirectionLocale`. A name is required by the types.
- **Sizes:** Compose's code ships one size (16px track, 44px handle). The Expressive sizes come from the M3 token values in Material Components Android (`m3_comp_slider_{xsmall…xlarge}_*`), generated from the same token database: track 16 / 24 / 40 / 56 / 96px, corner 8 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 28px, handle 44 / 44 / 52 / 68 / 108px, inset icons 24 / 24 / 32px (md, lg, xl). **The medium handle is 52px**, as the M3 spec's measurements table states (2026-10-13; the MDC token value of 44px had been used before).
- **Theme scoping:** the disabled handle's composite (`on-surface` 38% over `surface`) mixes `var(--md-sys-color-*)`, never Tailwind's `var(--color-*)` aliases, which resolve once at `:root` and ignored a `ThemeScope` or dark mode (2026-10-13; a unit test bans the aliases in slider styles, and an e2e test checks the colour in a dark scope).
- **Values** (Compose `Slider.kt`, `SliderTokens`): active track and handle `primary`, inactive track `secondary-container`; a 4px handle that narrows to 2px while pressed, dragged or keyboard-focused (not after a pointer release that leaves it focused; M3 spec, 2026-10-13); a 6px gap each side (10px while keyboard-focused, Compose's inset focus ring padding); 2px inside corners; 4px stop indicators a corner's length from the ends, and ticks. **Stop indicator and tick colours follow the live m3.material.io token table** (sliders specs, "Tokens & specs", read through Playwright on 2026-10-13): on the active track `on-primary` ("Slider active stop indicator container color", opacity 1), on the inactive track and at the end `on-secondary-container` ("Slider inactive stop indicator container color", opacity 1); disabled, `inverse-on-surface` on the active track and `on-surface` at 38% on the inactive one ("Slider disabled active / inactive stop indicator container color"; the table's only disabled opacity, 0.38, is in its deprecated group, so it is applied to the `on-surface` dot, which was 38% before, and not to the `inverse-on-surface` one). Until then they followed Compose's code (`Slider.kt:1729-1731`: `secondary-container` on the active track, `primary` on the inactive one), which ignores Compose's own tokens. Enabled dots keep at least 3:1 against their track in light and dark (an e2e contrast check). Disabled: active `on-surface` 38%, inactive 12%, handle `on-surface` 38% over `surface`.
- **Track drawing** ports `SliderDefaults.drawTrack` and `SliderImpl`'s placement as CSS length expressions (`slider-geometry.ts`, unit-tested): discrete values sit between the corners (except at the ends); segments draw only past Compose's thresholds, done with `min(len, max(0, (len − threshold) × 10⁴))`; the centred track leaves a plain 6px gap at the centre; range and centred tracks have stops at both ends; ticks under a thumb, at the centre, or replaced by a stop are left out. Because it is all CSS on the custom properties, the first server render is exact and resizing needs no measuring.
- **Layout:** the track is one grid cell; segments, ticks, thumbs and the value indicator are placed with logical margins (`margin-inline-start`, `inline-size`), so nothing is positioned (the layout-safety check now ignores visually hidden inputs). A vertical slider writes its track with `writing-mode: vertical-lr` + `direction: rtl`, so the inline axis runs bottom to top and the same geometry and logical radii serve both orientations and RTL. Vertical sliders run bottom to top (Compose's `reverseVerticalDirection = true`) and default to 240px tall; horizontal ones fill the width.
- **Inset icons** follow MDC (`BaseSlider`): the start icon 10px inside the active track's start, the end icon 10px inside the inactive track's end (replacing the stop indicator), each shown only when its segment fits it plus 20px; `on-primary` / `on-secondary-container`. **When the active track is too short for the start icon, it moves to the inactive track** (M3 spec: "the icon moves to the inactive track"; 2026-10-13): a copy placed on the track 10px after the inactive segment's start, sized by the complement of the active copy's CSS condition, so exactly one shows and nothing is measured. Centred sliders ignore inset icons, and so do XS and S sliders, with a development warning (M3: "Avoid adding inset icons to XS or S"; 2026-10-13, they had rendered unsized), and a vertical `RangeSlider` warns in development (M3: "Don't use inset icons on centered or range sliders", "Avoid using range sliders vertically").
- **Descriptions** (2026-10-24): `aria-describedby` and `aria-details` go to React Aria's `useSlider`, which hands them to every thumb's input with `aria-errormessage`, the elements that take focus; the group keeps only the name. React Aria writes them as empty strings without ids, so the thumb drops those. They were left out before, so helper or error text couldn't be linked.
- **Keyboard:** besides React Aria's arrows, Page Up / Down and Home / End, an arrow with **Space held** steps by the page size (M3 spec: "Space & Arrows — … by one interval"; 2026-10-13), as Shift + arrow already does.
- **Value indicator** (`valueLabel`): while dragging or keyboard-focused, a 44px `inverse-surface` pill with `label-large` `inverse-on-surface` text, 12px above the handle (left of a vertical one), from `ValueIndicator*` tokens. Compose's default slider has none.
- **Touch** (2026-10-15, Compose `Slider.kt`: `draggable(orientation)` and a tap committed on release): the slider is `touch-action: pan-y` (`pan-x` vertical), and `useTouchTrack` wraps React Aria's track props for touch and pen: nothing changes until the touch lifts (a tap: the nearest thumb jumps there) or moves 8px along the track (a drag: the nearest thumb follows the finger, pointer captured); a move across the track is left to the browser, which scrolls and cancels the pointer, and is never a tap. A vertical swipe that started on a horizontal slider changed the value (80 → 51) and never scrolled the page. Mouse presses keep React Aria's handling (value on press).
- **Forced colours** (2026-10-15): tracks, stops and the handle are backgrounds, so they take system colours with `forced-color-adjust: none`: the active track `Highlight` (its stops `HighlightText`), the inactive track an outlined `Canvas` segment (stops `CanvasText`), the handle `CanvasText`, `GrayText` when disabled; the value indicator gets an outline. The slider vanished before.
- **Deviations:** no haptics. **Discrete presses** (2026-10-17): discrete thumbs and stops are drawn between the corners, so every press on a discrete track (the mouse's too, handled by `useTouchTrack` instead of React Aria's linear mapping) maps back through the same inset, `(offset − corner) / (length − 2 × corner)` clamped, with the corner read in px from `--m3-slider-corner`: a tap on a drawn stop selects that stop at every size (XL picked 10 for 0, 80 for 90 and 90 for 100). Continuous sliders keep React Aria's mouse handling.
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `slider-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-slider-{active-track-color, inactive-track-color, handle-color, stop-indicator-color, value-indicator-container-color, value-indicator-label-label-text-color}`, one set for every size. The root carries `data-tokens="md-comp-slider"` and `data-size`. The stop indicators on the active track, inset icons and disabled values stay classes.

### Sheets (built, Tier 3)

- `SheetTrigger` (trigger + sheet, `open` / `defaultOpen` / `onOpenChange`, like `DialogTrigger`), `BottomSheet` (`variant` = `modal | standard`) and `SideSheet` (`variant` = `modal | standard`, `edge` = `start | end`, `detached`, `title`, `onBack`, `actions`, `footer`, `footerDivider`, `divider`, `swipeable`); both sheets take `swipeable` (default `true`). Modal sheets are `useModalOverlay` + `useDialog` dialogs portalled through `Overlay`; children, `actions` and `footer` may be functions of `{ close }`. Names: `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby`, or a side sheet's `title`. Announced words are in `labels` (bottom: `expand`, `collapse`, `dismiss`, `close`; side: `close`, `back`), with English defaults; they replaced the bottom sheet's `expandLabel` / `dismissLabel` (2026-10-13).
- **Bottom sheet values** (m3.material.io, Compose `ModalBottomSheet.kt`, `BottomSheet.kt`, `SheetDefaults.kt`, `SheetBottomTokens`): at most 640px wide, centred; a **72px top margin, and above 640px a 56px top margin and 56px side margins** (m3.material.io specs, 2026-10-13; until then a full sheet reached the top of the window); 28px top corners; `surface-container-low` at **level 1** (`DockedModalContainerElevation`, a shadow since 2026-10-13); a 32 × 4px `on-surface-variant` handle with 22px above and below; a 32% scrim on the default effects spring. It shows on the default spatial spring and hides on the **fast spatial** spring, since the exit moves the sheet and position moves on spatial springs (m3.material.io motion, 2026-10-13; it used Compose's fast effects `hideMotionSpec` before). The exit travels **112% of the sheet's size** (side sheets too), so the spring's overshoot (about 9.5% of the distance on the expressive fast spatial spring) settles out of view: m3.material.io's exits leave the screen for good, and with a 100% exit the sheet sprang a few pixels back into the window before unmounting (2026-10-13). A unit test checks the distance against every scheme's fast spatial spring, and an e2e test samples the exit frames.
- **Standard bottom sheet** (`variant="standard"`, 2026-10-13, m3.material.io: "Two variants: standard and modal", and Compose's `BottomSheetScaffold`): no scrim, no focus containment, nothing hidden from assistive tech and focus isn't moved, so the page stays usable; the panel is a non-modal `role="dialog"`. It renders in place (not portalled), `fixed` to the bottom of the window below every overlay (`--md-sys-z-sticky`); `className` (or `classNames.container`, the same outermost element) can place it differently, e.g. `absolute` in a bounded region. It is open by default and rests collapsed at `peekHeight` (56px, Compose's `SheetPeekHeight`); the handle (press, Enter / Space, or a drag) expands and collapses it, Escape inside it collapses it, and it never closes itself (`open` hides it).
- **Standard sheet detents** (2026-10-15; m3.material.io: a sheet "toggled between a collapsed and expanded state … can be set by the system or toggled by the user", and Compose's `rememberStandardBottomSheetState(initialValue = SheetValue.Expanded | PartiallyExpanded)` with `SheetState.expand()` / `partialExpand()`): `detent` (controlled) / `defaultDetent` (uncontrolled, default `collapsed`) / `onDetentChange`, with `BottomSheetDetent` = `collapsed | expanded`, the names `data-detent` already used. `defaultDetent="expanded"` opens the sheet fully raised: it enters straight to its full height on the default spatial spring (no stop at the peek), and the handle, a drag, Escape or the collapse button lower it to the peek. `defaultDetent` applies each time the sheet opens (the panel mounts afresh). A controlled sheet moves only when `detent` changes, and every user move (handle press, Enter / Space, drag settle, Escape, collapse button) calls `onDetentChange` instead; a change the app makes isn't reported back. `onDetentChange` fires only when the resting detent really changes: a sheet no taller than its peek is always `expanded` (`data-detent` now says so too; before, a handle press on such a sheet set `data-detent="collapsed"` while it stayed fully raised). Moves between detents run on the same `translate` transition as before (default spatial spring; under reduced motion the spatial springs are instant, so the sheet moves to its new detent at once, §6). Compose's `Hidden` is `open={false}` / `defaultOpen={false}`; the sheet never hides itself, as with `skipHiddenState = true`. A modal sheet ignores the three props: its detents stay half / full by `skipPartiallyExpanded`. Whether a collapsed sheet's off-screen content is inert is a separate concern, left as it is here.
- **Detents:** like Compose's legacy anchors, a modal sheet taller than half the window opens to half (`PartiallyExpanded` at window / 2) and can expand to its full height (up to the top margin); shorter sheets open fully. `skipPartiallyExpanded` opens fully.
- **Handle and dragging:** the handle is a button. Pressed (or Enter / Space), it expands a half-open or collapsed sheet, collapses a full standard one and closes a full modal one, as Compose's handle click does; its label says which. Dragging it moves the sheet (pointer capture after 4px, no transition while dragging) and releasing settles with Compose's `AnchoredDraggableState` rules (`sheet-settle.ts`, unit-tested): a fling over 125px/s goes to the next anchor in its direction; otherwise the sheet moves on after 56px and springs back if not; settling past the bottom closes a modal sheet. **The settle keeps the release velocity** (2026-10-17, as Compose's `animateTo(target, velocity)`): the spring (default spatial, or the fast spatial exit for a close) starts with the pointer's speed, written as a `linear()` easing and a duration for that velocity (`springWithVelocityToLinearEasing` in `tokens/spring-velocity.ts`, kept out of `tokens/motion.ts` since nearly every component imports that) set inline on the moving layer (`data-settling`) until its `translate` transition ends or the next press; it started from rest before, so a flung sheet stalled as it was let go. Under reduced motion settling stays instant. **Without a handle** (`dragHandle={false}`) a drag starting in the top 48px of the sheet still moves it (m3.material.io accessibility, 2026-10-13).
- **Dragging from the content** (2026-10-13; m3.material.io lists "swiping down" among the ways to dismiss a sheet): a touch or pen drag that starts on the content moves the sheet when it heads down (more vertical than sideways, past the 4px slop) while the content, and any scroller between it and the touch, is at its top; it then drags, settles and dismisses exactly as the handle does (`settleTarget`), with the same transitions, so reduced motion applies alike. An upward drag, or any drag while the content is scrolled, scrolls the content, and a mostly sideways drag is left to horizontal scrollers and text. The first decision holds until the pointer lifts (`bottom-sheet-gesture.ts`, unit-tested). Drags starting on form fields or on elements with `touch-action: none` (nested sheets) are ignored (a slider is `pan-y` since 2026-10-15, so a vertical drag on one moves the sheet, as Compose's nested scroll does, and the slider drops the touch), and mouse drags select text as on any page. Mechanics: the panel listens in the capture phase (pressable content stops its pointer events bubbling) and captures the pointer once dragging, which cancels the press underneath; while the content is at its top it is `touch-action: pan-x pan-down pinch-zoom`, so the browser only scrolls it for upward and sideways moves and a downward one stays a pointer gesture; a non-passive `touchmove` listener cancels a drag's moves for browsers without `pan-down` (iOS Safari), and `overscroll-behavior: contain` stops rubber-banding and scroll chaining. Playwright drives real touch gestures through CDP for drag-to-dismiss, scrolling back, an upward scroll and a horizontal scroller.
- **Swiping up raises the sheet first** (2026-10-15, Compose's `onPreScroll`): while a sheet rests below its top detent (`data-can-rise` on the content), an upward drag on content at its top drags the sheet too (`contentGesture`; the content is `touch-action: pan-x pinch-zoom` then), and once the sheet is fully raised the rest of the move scrolls the content (`scrollTop` follows the overflow). The content scrolled and the sheet stayed at half before.
- **Lower detents without a bounce** (2026-10-15, Compose's `partialExpand()` with `hideMotionSpec`): a move down to a lower detent that isn't a drag's settle (Escape, the handle or the collapse button on a full sheet, or the app lowering a standard sheet) runs on the fast effects spring (`data-lowering`, rendered state so the transition starts with it); it dropped 5.6px below the detent on the spatial spring. Settles, the entry and every move up keep the spatial springs; reduced motion stays instant.
- **No gap on overshoot** (2026-10-15): the bottom sheet's panel, and a docked modal side sheet's, carries a 32px bleed in its own colour past its anchored edge (`::after`, `background-color: inherit`), and the container clips below (or past the end of) itself with `clip-path`, so the spring's overshoot never shows the page under the sheet (3–6px before) and the bleed never shows at rest. Compose scales the sheet up for the same reason.
- **Focus stays on the sheet or returns** (2026-10-15): the standard bottom sheet's collapse button moves focus to the handle (or the panel) before it goes; a standard side sheet that closes with focus inside returns focus to where it came from (saved on focus-in), else to the control before the sheet; focus dropped to the page in both.
- **Forced colours** (2026-10-15): bottom sheets have a `CanvasText` border on their free edges, modal side sheets on their free edge (all round when detached), and the drag handle bar is `CanvasText`.
- **Close affordance:** while a sheet fills the window up to its top margin, a close button (modal) or a collapse button (standard, "a collapse icon … to return to their initial position") shows at its top end (m3.material.io guidelines, 2026-10-13). `closeButton={false}` hides it; `closeIcon` / `collapseIcon` replace the icons.
- **Dismissal:** a press on the scrim closes a modal sheet (Compose's `animateToDismiss`). **Escape closes a modal sheet in one press** from any height (2026-10-17, decided: on the web Escape is a close request, as WAI-ARIA APG's modal dialog and HTML's `<dialog>` treat it, and other libraries' drawers close on one press; it replaced Compose's back behaviour, `settleToDismiss`, which first returned a fully open sheet to half). Drags and the handle still stop at half. `closedBy` (`any` by default) limits it: `closerequest` ignores the scrim, `none` also ignores Escape and a drag down to close. A standard sheet's Escape still collapses it.
- **Side sheets** aren't in Compose; values follow m3.material.io and Material Components Android (`m3_comp_sheet_side_*`): 256px wide and **at most 400px** (m3.material.io's maximum, 2026-10-13: a consumer width stops there, and at the window's width); modal `surface-container-low` at level 1 with 16px corners on its free edge over a 32% scrim, sliding in from its `edge` (the end edge by default, mirrored in RTL); detached sheets (modal or standard) float 16px from the edges with 16px corners all round; standard sheets are `surface`, level 0, no corners, with an `outline-variant` divider on their content edge (`divider`, default on; not when detached), and sit in the layout, opening and closing their width (default spatial) and becoming `inert` while closed. **The width is a measured length** (2026-10-15): the root's `width` moves between `--m3-side-sheet-width` (the frame's layout width, from a `ResizeObserver`; `auto` until measured, so the server's HTML is right) and 0, with the panel in a clip that fills the root, against the root's inner edge (the content's side) since 2026-10-24, so the sheet slides in and out with its edge leading as M3's reference moves it (Material Components Android offsets the sheet and resizes the content beside it); it was end-aligned, which wiped the panel into view far side first. It was a grid track (`1fr ↔ 0fr`), and a fraction below 1 takes only that share of the shrinking free space, so the panel floated up to 64px off its edge mid-animation. The measured width is also the root's `max-width` (2026-10-17), so the spring's overshoot (256 → 259.8px) no longer opens a strip between the content and the divider. They use the bottom sheet's springs rather than MDC's 275ms tween.
  - **Role:** both variants are dialogs (m3.material.io: "The accessibility role for a side sheet is Dialog"); the standard one is non-modal (no `aria-modal`, focus isn't moved). Until 2026-10-13 the standard sheet was a role-less `div`.
  - **Header:** an optional back button (`onBack`, `labels.back`, `backIcon`, mirrored in RTL), a `title-large` `on-surface-variant` headline, then `actions` and **a built-in close button** (`labels.close`, `closeIcon`), in a 72px row with 24px start padding (16px with the back button), 12px end padding and 12px between parts. m3.material.io requires the close affordance in every side sheet, so it is always shown (2026-10-13); this supersedes decision #11's "no bundled icon" for this button, which ships the Material Symbols "close" path as the dialog's full-screen header does. Content has 24px sides.
  - **No horizontal scrolling** (m3.material.io: "Side sheets cannot scroll horizontally", 2026-10-13): the content scrolls vertically and clips anything wider (`overflow-x-hidden`); `overflow-y-auto` alone let it scroll sideways.
  - **Bottom actions** (`footer`): at least 72px, 16px above and 24px below, 24px sides, start-aligned with 8px between buttons; `footerDivider` adds an `outline-variant` divider above it (m3.material.io anatomy and specs, 2026-10-13).
- **Motion and layout safety:** sheets move by `translate` on a library-owned layer inside the container that places them; the consumer's `className` and `style` go on that container, the outermost element (2026-10-14; they went on the panel), and `classNames.panel` styles the surface. A bottom sheet sits at its container's bottom (`items-end`), so a consumer inset that stretches the container never stretches the sheet. The in-flow standard side sheet runs the layout-safety matrix; its `className` stays on its outer layout box, and `classNames.panel` goes on the painted `data-slot="panel"` surface (a fix, 2026-10-14: it went on the layout box with `className`, so a colour or corner class there painted the wrong box).
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `sheet-tokens.ts`). Bottom sheets (`data-tokens="md-comp-sheet-bottom-docked"` on the panel): `--md-comp-sheet-bottom-docked-container-color`, `-container-shape` (the top corners, which the handle's focus ring follows), `-drag-handle-color` (`data-slot="drag-handle"`), and `--md-comp-sheet-bottom-docked-{modal,standard}-container-elevation` by `data-variant`. Side sheets (`data-tokens="md-comp-sheet-side"`; on the panel of a modal sheet, on the root of a standard one, whose painted panel is `data-slot="panel"`): `--md-comp-sheet-side-docked-modal-container-color`, `-container-elevation` and `-container-shape` (the free edge's corners), `--md-comp-sheet-side-docked-standard-container-color`, and `--md-comp-sheet-side-detached-container-shape`, scoped by `data-detached`, which comes after `data-variant` so a detached sheet's corners win. A standard side sheet stays flat and square. No scrim token: M3 has none, so the scrims stay `bg-scrim/32`.
- **Side sheet edge and swipe to dismiss** (2026-10-17, plan `docs/plans/side-sheet-api.md`; m3.material.io side sheets, MDC-Android `SideSheet.md`, Compose `ModalWideNavigationRail` and `SheetState`):
  - **`edge`** = `start | end` (default `end`: m3.material.io's "usually on the right side", the left in RTL as it asks). Logical values only, as MDC's `start` / `end` sheet edge; `data-edge` on the root (a modal sheet's panel, a standard sheet's outer box) is a public hook. A modal sheet's container is `start-0` or `end-0`, its clip-path, enter / exit translation, free-edge corners, forced-colours border and overshoot bleed mirror per edge (and per direction, through `rtl:`), and a detached sheet keeps 16px from the chosen edge. A standard sheet stays where the app puts it in its row; `edge` says which side faces the window: its divider goes on the other side, and its panel rides the content-side edge as the measured width opens and closes, sliding in from the window's side.
  - **Swipe to dismiss** on the modal side sheet only (a standard one is opened and closed by the app), touch and pen only (a mouse drag selects text; mouse users have the close button, Escape and the scrim), starting anywhere on the panel, never the scrim. After the 4px slop (`sideSwipeGesture`) a move more vertical than sideways is the browser's (the content scrolls natively), a sideways move away from the edge is ignored, and one toward it drags; the first decision holds. The panel and its content (a scroller, which would otherwise give its descendants panning back) are `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom`, and a non-passive `touchmove` listener cancels a drag's moves. Content that owns horizontal drags keeps them: form fields, anything whose `touch-action` excludes horizontal panning (`none`, `pan-y`: a horizontal `Slider`), and a horizontal scroller that can still scroll the way the finger moves (one at its end lets the sheet have the swipe). `ownsGesture` takes an axis, so the bottom sheet's content drag shares the rules (a vertical slider, `pan-x`, now keeps its vertical drag there too). Dragging moves the library-owned motion layer by `--m3-side-sheet-offset` with no transition (`data-dragging`), clamped at the open position; the release settles with `settleTarget` and a **positional threshold of half the width** (Compose's horizontal rail, `distance * 0.5f`; the bottom sheet's 56px suits a taller travel) or a fling over 125px/s, continuing at the release speed (`springWithVelocityToLinearEasing`: the fast spatial exit to 112%, or the default spatial spring back). The scrim stays at 32% until the sheet closes, as the bottom sheet's. Taking the pointer once dragging cancels the press underneath. Release velocity, for both sheets, comes from the last 100ms of moves (`releaseVelocity`), so a slow drag ending in a flick is a fling and one that stops before lifting isn't. **One release module** (2026-10-17): both sheets settle through `sheet-settle.ts` (`settleTarget`, `releaseVelocity`, `releaseMotion` for the velocity spring or `null` under reduced motion, and the 112% `EXIT_DISTANCE`); the gesture rules are split so each sheet loads only its own (`sheet-gesture.ts`: the slop and `ownsGesture`, shared; `bottom-sheet-gesture.ts`; `side-swipe-gesture.ts`). The swipe costs the side sheet about 4.7 kB by the budget script (about 2.3 kB minified and gzipped), its budget set to the measured size.
  - **No `confirmValueChange`:** the controlled `open` is the veto. A swipe past the threshold calls `onOpenChange(false)`; if the app keeps `open` true, the sheet springs back from where it was let go. One mechanism covers every way of closing (button, Escape, scrim, swipe).
  - **`swipeable`** (default `true`; Compose's `sheetGesturesEnabled`, a positive adjective as `deselectable`; not `draggable`, an HTML global attribute): `false` stops the side sheet's swipe and the bottom sheet's handle, top-zone and content drags, while the bottom sheet's handle stays a button. `closedBy="none"` also stops the side sheet's swipe, as it stops the bottom sheet's drag down to close.
  - **No `sheetMaxWidth`:** m3.material.io fixes 400px; the panel's `max-w-[400px]` is a class, so `classNames.panel` lifts it for an app that must.
  - **Reduced motion:** the drag still follows the finger; a sheet let go past its threshold fades out where it was released (its offset stays while the exit fades), and one let go short of it returns at once. The modal bottom sheet does the same since 2026-10-17 (a drag down to close kept its offset only for the slide before, so under reduced motion it jumped back to rest and then faded); a refused close returns both to rest.
  - The swipe is never the only way to close (WCAG 2.5.7): the close button is always shown, and the scrim and Escape close it too.
- **Not in v1:** predictive back, swiping a closed side sheet open from the window edge (it competes with the browsers' back and forward gestures), and detents beyond half / full (modal) or peek / full (standard).

### Carousel (built, Tier 3)

- `Carousel` with `variant` = `multi-browse | uncontained | multi-aspect | hero | full-screen`, `orientation` (vertical by default for full-screen), `preferredItemSize` (multi-browse 186px by default, hero the whole carousel), `itemSize` (uncontained, 240px), `itemSpacing` (8px; 16px full-screen), `heroAlignment` = `start | center`, `minSmallItemSize` / `maxSmallItemSize` (40 / 56px), `contentPadding` (`{ start, end, cross }`), `snap` = `none | item` (uncontained and multi-aspect), `onAction(index)`, `disabledIndexes` and `labels` (`slide(position, count)`, numbers formatted in the locale). Children are the items (any elements, usually images), each filling its slot. The consumer gives the carousel its cross-axis size. It is a `region` with `aria-roledescription="carousel"` and a required name; items are `group`s labelled "n of N" (`slide`).
- **Layouts** (the M3 Expressive spec's six, 2026-10-13): multi-browse and hero (centre-aligned with `heroAlignment="center"`) on keylines; **uncontained**: items of one size (`itemSize`) that flow past the end edge, never masked or resized (2026-10-13, from the spec: "items are a single size and flow past the edge of the screen … without the text being masked or cropped"; until then it used Compose's uncontained keylines, which shrank and cropped items that were fully on screen); **multi-aspect** (uncontained multi-aspect ratio): each item keeps its own width at the carousel's height, clamped between 9:16 and 16:9 (`--m3-carousel-cross-size`, measured), no keylines; **full-screen**: one edge-to-edge item per viewport (the slot is the scroller's size), 16px apart, no padding or corners, snapping to every item.
- **Centred layouts** (decided 2026-10-17): the spec's guidelines say "All of these layouts can be centered, though center-aligned hero is the most common centered carousel", but its specs page gives measurements only for the center-aligned hero, and Compose, the source of exact values, builds only `HorizontalCenteredHeroCarousel` (its keyline code can centre an arrangement, but no public carousel uses it for multi-browse or uncontained). A centred multi-browse or uncontained carousel would need small and medium counts on both sides that neither defines, so only the hero centres (`heroAlignment="center"`); full-screen is centred by nature. Revisit when the spec or Compose defines them.
- **Decided, matching Compose** (2026-10-17): items scroll over the 16px end padding (the spec: items "bleed over the padding on each side when scrolling"), so it shows only at the end of the scroll.
- **Padding** (spec, 2026-10-13): multi-browse and hero 16px before and after the items and 8px across; uncontained and multi-aspect 16px before only, 8px across; full-screen none. The cross padding is the scroller's. Along the carousel, keyline layouts handle it as Compose does (`Strategy` with `beforeContentPadding` / `afterContentPadding`): the last start and end shift steps are shifted by the padding, and the shift distances are at least the padding, so items start inside it and scroll over it. The scroll range and snap positions don't change. Layouts without keylines (uncontained, multi-aspect, reduced motion) pad the scroller itself, with matching `scroll-padding`.
- **Small items stay 40–56px at the start and end** (2026-10-13, departing from Compose's maths to meet the spec: "Small carousel items have a minimum width of 40dp and a maximum width of 56dp"). Compose's `createShiftedKeylineListForContentPadding` takes an equal share of the padding from every item, so with 16px padding a 40px small item was 34.67px at the ends (360px multi-browse: 181 · 113 · 34.7). Here only the large and medium keylines give it up (those wider than `maxSmallItemSize`, passed to `createStrategy`), so the same carousel starts 178 · 110 · 40. The total taken is still the padding, so the anchors, snap positions and scroll range are unchanged; mid-scroll the keylines are the defaults, as before.
- **Keylines** are a TypeScript port of Compose's `carousel` package (`carousel-keylines.ts`, unit-tested): `Arrangement` (lowest-cost fit of large / medium / small items, the 10% medium flex), `multiBrowseKeylineList`, `heroKeylineList` (Compose's `uncontainedKeylineList` was dropped on 2026-10-13 with the uncontained keylines), keyline lists with pivots and cutoffs, `Strategy` (start / end shift steps and interpolation points, content padding), `getKeylineListForScrollOffset`, `getSnapPositionOffset` and `carouselItem`'s placement (keyline before / after the item's centre, interpolated size and offset, out-of-bounds offset). With 360px, 186px preferred and 8px spacing that gives 186 · 118 · 40, mirrored at the end, as Compose does.
- **Web rendering:** Compose drives a pager; here the carousel scrolls natively. Each item keeps a natural slot the size of the large item (so the scroll range is Compose's `calculateMaxScrollOffset`), and on every scroll frame a library-owned mask layer inside it is translated and clipped (`clip-path: inset(… round var(--m3-carousel-item-corner))`) to its keyline. The root and slots are never transformed; nothing re-renders while scrolling. `--m3-carousel-item-size` on each mask exposes the visible size (Compose's `carouselItemDrawInfo`) for parallax.
- **Reduced motion** (spec, 2026-10-13): items no longer change size as they scroll. Every item keeps the large size, unmasked and untranslated, so a hero carousel shows part of the next item; the docs ask for such carousels to reach the window's edges.
- **Snapping** (spec, 2026-10-13: "Hero carousels swipe through one item at a time. Multi-browse carousels scroll through many items at once."): hero and full-screen stop at every item (`scroll-snap-stop: always`, Compose's `singleAdvanceFlingBehavior`); multi-browse snaps with `snap-mandatory` but lets a fling cross several items; uncontained and multi-aspect scroll freely (`noSnapFlingBehavior`) unless `snap="item"`. Each keyline item's snap point is placed by `scroll-margin` from `getSnapPositionOffset`. Mouse drag scrolls (snapping pauses while dragging, and a drag never activates an item); touch and trackpads scroll natively. RTL reads the absolute scroll offset and mirrors the translation.
- **Overscroll on its own axis only** (2026-10-15): the scroller contains overscroll along the carousel (`overscroll-x-contain` horizontal, `overscroll-y-contain` vertical), so a vertical swipe that starts on a horizontal carousel scrolls the page; it used to contain both axes and swallowed it. A full-screen vertical carousel contains nothing, so past its last item a swipe goes on to the page instead of trapping it.
- **Keyboard and focus** (spec, 2026-10-13): the container is never focusable. Every item is a tab stop ("use Tab or the arrow keys to navigate the carousel items"); ←/→ (↑/↓ vertical, mirrored in RTL) move focus to the previous or next item and Home / End to the ends, and ↑/↓ (←/→ vertical) leave the carousel for the previous or next tabbable element, such as the "Show all" button the spec puts below it. A keyboard-focused item scrolls to its snap position (smoothly, instantly under reduced motion); a pressed one doesn't move. The focus ring is the item slot's outline with the item's corners (inside the edge for full-screen).
- **Item states** (with `onAction`): a press, Enter or Space activates an item (`useM3Interaction`). Hover, focus and the pressed ripple paint on a library-owned state layer (`on-surface`) sharing the mask's grid cell, over the item; a pressed item's corners tighten from `--m3-carousel-corner` (28px) to `--m3-carousel-pressed-corner` (20px, large-increased) on the fast spatial spring (`--m3-carousel-item-corner` is a registered `<length>`), the spec's "changes the shape slightly". Disabled items (`disabledIndexes`) are `aria-disabled`, at 38% opacity, and can't be activated.
- **Container colour** (2026-10-13, the spec's colour table: "Container — Surface"): each item's mask is `surface`, so transparent or still-loading content never shows what's behind the carousel.
- **Full-screen has no corners** (2026-10-13; it fills the window edge to edge): the variant sets `--m3-carousel-corner` and `--m3-carousel-pressed-corner` to 0 on the root, where they kept 28px before.
- **Corners and spacing:** items are masked with 28px (extra-large) corners, as Compose's samples use `maskClip(MaterialTheme.shapes.extraLarge)`; the component token `--md-comp-carousel-item-container-shape` sets it. Spacing defaults to 8px (m3.material.io; Compose's default is 0 and its samples use 8dp).
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `carousel-tokens.ts`): `--md-comp-carousel-item-container-shape` (M3's `md.comp.carousel-item.container.shape`, a length, 28px by default). The root carries `data-tokens="md-comp-carousel"` and `data-variant`; the internal `--m3-carousel-corner` takes the token, so the registered `--m3-carousel-item-corner` still animates a pressed item. The pressed corners and full-screen's square items stay fixed.
- **Deviations / not in v1:** no parallax helper, items aren't z-ordered by distance from the current item, and before hydration items render unmasked at the preferred size. The "Show all" button is the app's, as the spec places it outside the carousel.

### Date and time pickers (built, Tier 3)

- `DatePicker` and `DateRangePicker` (the modal picker's content, Compose's `DatePicker` / `DateRangePicker`), `DateField` (the docked date picker; plan `docs/plans/date-field.md`), `TimePicker` (dial or input, Compose's `TimePicker` / `TimeInput`) with `TimePickerModeToggle`, and `PickerDialog` (the modal form of both, Compose's `DatePickerDialog` / `TimePickerDialog`, with `confirmButton`, `dismissButton` and `modeToggleButton` slots). Values are `@internationalized/date` `CalendarDate` / `{ start, end }` / `Time`; all are controlled or uncontrolled. Words are in `labels` for localisation (renamed from `strings` 2026-10-17, so one name covers every component).
  - **Short windows** (2026-10-13): a `PickerDialog` too tall for the window (a short window, or text at 200%) stops at the window's height (`max-h-full`, `overflow-hidden`), and its content scrolls (`min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`) between the title and the actions, which keep their height (`shrink-0`) and stay in view. The picker keeps its own size inside the scroll area. Before, the panel itself scrolled and the content was `min-h-0` around a picker that couldn't shrink, so the calendar overflowed onto Cancel and OK. Playwright checks the date and range dialogs at a 720px-tall window with a 32px root, and at 480px with the default root.
- **The M3 date picker spec is followed in full** (2026-10-13; it overrides earlier choices that followed Compose, which has no docked picker): the three variants (docked, modal, modal input), the typed input without a mask, swiping months, the scrolling range list, the full-screen form, 48px targets, and the keyboard table.
- **Date pickers** (Compose `DatePicker.kt`, `DateRangePicker.kt`, `DatePickerModalTokens`), on React Aria `useCalendar` / `useRangeCalendar` / `useCalendarGrid` / `useCalendarCell` (arrow keys, Page Up / Down, Shift+Page Up / Down, Home / End, min / max, unavailable dates, the locale's calendar and first weekday):
  - 360px wide. Header at least 120px (128px for ranges): `label-large` title ("Select date" / "Select dates"), `headline-large` headline (`title-large` for a range: "Mar 10 – Mar 13"), both `on-surface-variant`, the mode toggle at the end, an `outline-variant` divider below. While a new range is being picked, the headline shows its first day and an open end, "Mar 20 – End date" (Compose; `labels.endDateLabel`; 2026-10-15), instead of the range it replaces.
  - Body with 12px sides: a 56px month row (a text button with the month and a dropdown arrow that turns over, `on-surface-variant`; previous / next icon buttons), a 48px weekday row (`body-large` `on-surface`) and six 48px week rows, always six as Compose keeps the height. Days are 40px circles: selected `primary` / `on-primary`; today a 1px `primary` outline with `primary` text; disabled 38%.
  - **Targets:** each day's `::before` widens its hit area to the 48px cell, and each 36px year pill's to 48px tall (spec: "Touch targets are 48x48dp"); the visuals stay 40px / 36px.
  - **Weekdays** are column headers (`<th abbr>` with the long name, which `title` shows on hover: "the tooltip is available on hover"). React Aria hides its header row by default; the spec's table makes them column headers.
  - **Paging:** previous / next, Page Up / Down, and a horizontal swipe (touch and pen, 48px and mostly sideways, mirrored in RTL; `touch-action: pan-y`). Each new month slides 48px in from its side on the default spatial spring with a fade (`@starting-style` on a keyed wrapper, not the root); reduced motion keeps only the fade.
  - **Ranges:** the ends are filled; the days between sit on a 40px `secondary-container` band, drawn as cell background gradients (half a cell at each end, mirrored in RTL; 1px wider than the cell so fractional widths leave no hairline), so nothing is positioned. The gradients use `var(--md-sys-color-secondary-container)`, not Tailwind's `--color-*`, which resolves once at `:root` and so kept the root's colour inside a `ThemeScope` or dark subtree (2026-10-13). **The range picker scrolls a vertical list of months** (spec: "To navigate across months, scroll vertically"; `monthLayout="scroll"`, the default; `"paged"` keeps the paged body). Each month is a 48px `title-small` subhead and its six weeks (336px), under one weekday row; the list is 344px tall, or fills a full-screen dialog. One React Aria range state covers every month of `yearRange` (within min / max) as its visible range, and each month is a `useCalendarGrid` with its own `startDate`; only the months in view (plus the focused one) render, between fixed-height spacers. This replaces the earlier deviation that paged ranges month by month.
  - **Input mode title** (spec, 2026-10-13): "Enter date" ("Enter dates" for a range; `labels.inputTitle`), as the spec's modal date input shows; the calendar keeps "Select date".
  - **Mode switch motion** (Compose's display mode animation, 2026-10-13): switching to the input slides it up from its own height as it fades in; switching back brings the calendar down 48px as it fades in (default spatial / default effects springs), in a clipping view. Only a switch animates; reduced motion keeps the fade. The outgoing view leaves at once. The picker's height moves to the new view's on the default spatial spring, clipped, as Compose's `SizeTransform` (2026-10-17; `use-size-transform.ts`: inline height only while it moves, so a consumer's height class still sets it at rest; a full-screen picker keeps the window's height; reduced motion jumps).
  - **Year list motion** (Compose, 2026-10-13): it expands down from the month row (a `clip-path` reveal) as it fades in from 60%, on the default effects spring; reduced motion keeps the fade. Closing (2026-10-15, Compose's `shrinkVertically` + `fadeOut`), it stays mounted over the grid (absolute below the month row, on the container colour, `inert`) and shrinks back up to the month row as it fades out on the fast effects spring; the grid is back underneath at once and takes focus. It used to vanish in one frame.
  - **Full-screen range header** (2026-10-13): at least 68px under the full-screen top row (Compose's range header is the 128px token less its 60px `HeaderHeightOffset`), so it is as tall as the title and headline; it was 128px under the row.
  - **Year list:** 72 × 36px pills, three columns 16px apart, in a 335px scroll area opened on the focused year; selected `primary`, this year outlined; arrow keys move between years (a radio group). It shows the whole `yearRange`, with years outside min / max **disabled** (`aria-disabled`, skipped by the arrows), as the spec lists a disabled state; it used to leave them out.
  - **Keyboard table extras:** Enter on a day closes and saves (in a `PickerDialog` with `onConfirm`, once a range has both ends; in the docked picker); Shift+M moves to the month menu (the docked menu, or the modal month button) and Shift+Y opens the year list. Handled in the capture phase, before React Aria's grid takes Enter. The previous / next and menu buttons have plain tooltips naming their shortcut ("Next month (Page Down)"), which describe them to screen readers.
  - **Typed input** (the modal date input and the docked field; `DateInput`): an outlined `TextField`, so it has the notch, state colours and `role="alert"` errors. **No input mask** (spec accessibility: "Don't automatically format the date … while the user is typing"); React Aria's segmented `useDateField` was replaced. `parseDate` reads the locale's part order and accepts dashes, spaces, slashes or dots, optional leading zeros, eight digits with no separator and a two-digit year (80 years back to 19 ahead); it reformats on Enter or blur. The locale's numeric pattern ("MM/DD/YYYY", "DD.MM.YYYY"), from `Intl.DateTimeFormat#formatToParts` in the Gregorian calendar with Latin digits, is the helper text and the placeholder (`labels.dateFormatHint` overrides it). Unusable text or dates outside min / max or unavailable say why in the supporting text. `inputMode="decimal"`, so phone keypads offer a separator.
  - Chrome icons (chevrons, dropdown, edit, calendar, check, close) are inline Material Symbols SVGs, as other components' own controls are; the chevrons mirror in RTL.
- **Docked date picker** (`DateField`): the typed field with a calendar icon button that opens the calendar just below it in the select menu's popover. A `surface-container-high` panel, 360px, 16px corners, level 3 (Compose has no docked tokens; these are the spec's docked roles). A 56px row holds the month and year menu buttons, each between previous / next icon buttons; a menu replaces the grid with 48px list rows (the chosen one `surface-variant` with a check), and the other menu button is disabled while it is open. The open menu's button keeps a pressed state layer (`data-menu-open`; M3 menus: the button "remain[s] the same visually, with the addition of a pressed state"; 2026-10-13). The grid shows the surrounding months' days in `on-surface-variant` (not choosable). Picking a day edits a draft; OK or Enter saves, Cancel or Escape closes. **On compact windows** (below 600px, `COMPACT_WINDOW`; 2026-10-15) the calendar button opens the modal `DatePicker` in a full-screen `PickerDialog` instead (the spec: "On compact breakpoints … a full-screen modal date picker is recommended"), editing the same kind of draft: Save or Enter on a day keeps it, the close button drops it, and focus returns to the button. At 330px the 360px docked panel was cut off ("Next year" off the edge) and its day targets overlapped. **Loaded only there** (2026-10-16): the modal picker added about 11 kB gzipped to every `DateField` (43.9 → 54.9 kB), so it lives in `date-picker/CompactDateDialog.tsx` and loads through `onDemand`. On a compact window it preloads in the browser's first idle period after mount, or sooner on `pointerover` or `focusin` on the field (`usePreloadOnIntent`; a tap fires `pointerover` before the press), so the first press opens it in the same pass; if the press wins, the dialog opens and takes focus once the module arrives. It mounts on the first open and stays mounted for its exit. A field on a wider window never loads it (47.0 kB), the server never does (`useMediaQuery` is `false` there), and `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails the build if `DateField` imports `CompactDateDialog` or `DatePicker` statically.
- **Time picker** (Compose `TimePicker.kt`, `TimePickerTokens`, `TimeInputTokens`):
  - 96 × 80px hour and minute selectors (114px wide on a 24-hour dial) in `display-large`, 8px corners, selected `primary-container`, unselected `surface-container-highest`, a 24px ":" between; a 52 × 80px AM / PM selector 12px away with a 1px `outline` border. Its selected colour is the token's `tertiary-container`: Compose's `isUpdatedTimepickerToggleEnabled` flag would switch it to `primary-container`, and the token was kept, as with the checkbox flag.
  - **The selectors take typed digits** (spec: "The dial time picker supports both manual and dial input"; "The hour and minute fields have the text input role"): they are numeric inputs styled as selectors; focusing one picks the dial's face. They were toggle buttons.
  - The dial, 36px below: 256px `surface-container-highest`, numbers on a 101px ring (69px inner ring on a 24-hour clock), a 48px `primary` handle with `on-primary` text, a 2px track and an 8px centre. It is an **SVG**, so nothing is positioned. **Each number is a 48px button** (spec: "{Value} Hours or minutes of {Total} — Button", "Space or Enter — Activates the time slot"), named "7 hours of 12" / "35 minutes of 60" (`labels.dialValue`), in a roving tab order (Tab lands on the chosen number, or the nearest five minutes); arrows move around the dial, Enter or Space chooses, and choosing an hour moves on to the minutes with focus. A `secondary` 3px ring marks keyboard focus. The dial used to be one `role="slider"`. Pressing or dragging picks the nearest hour (the inner ring within 74px of the centre) or five minutes; releasing on the hour face switches to minutes (Compose's `autoSwitchToMinute`). A tap turns the hand to the hour, which shows for 100ms once the hand arrives before the minute face replaces it (Compose's `onTap`: `rotateTo(animate = true)`, then `delay(100)`; 2026-10-15). It arrives within Compose's 0.01 rad visibility threshold (and 1px of the radius) on two frames in a row, not at Motion's full rest, which came about 0.3s later (2026-10-17); a drag switches on release. Focus on a tapped number moves on to the chosen minute's number, as after Enter, so it never drops to the page when the face changes (2026-10-15).
  - **From the full M3 spec check** (2026-10-13):
    - The hour and minute selectors show hover, focus and pressed state layers in their text colour (spec states: Enabled, Hover, Focus, Pressed). They are inputs, so pressing shows the pressed layer (`data-pressed`) rather than a ripple.
    - **The hand turns** on the default spatial spring along the shortest arc (spec motion: "Spatial springs apply to … rotation"; Compose animates the selector on taps and the hour ↔ minute switch). A drag moves it directly and reduced motion jumps it. The track, handle and clip are set as attributes from the animation (`useDialHand`), so turning renders nothing in React. The hour and minute faces crossfade on the default effects spring.
    - **Numbers under the handle are `on-primary`** (spec colour: the selected label is `on-primary`): the numbers are drawn twice, the second copy in `on-primary` clipped to the handle circle, as Compose blends them. At 14:07 the "05" under the handle had been `on-surface` on `primary` (1.99:1).
    - **AM / PM is one Tab stop** (spec role "Radio button (in list)", WAI-ARIA radio group): the checked option is in the Tab order and the arrow keys move the choice (selection follows focus), mirrored in RTL.
    - **The time reads left to right in RTL** (`dir="ltr"` on the display, as Compose forces `LayoutDirection.Ltr`); the AM / PM group follows the page's laid-out direction where the picker sits (its computed `direction`, the locale's on the server; 2026-10-17), not only the locale's.
    - Taps and drags on the minute face pick minutes in fives (Compose); the arrow keys, Enter and typing still reach any minute. A press within 74px of the centre picks the inner 24-hour ring (Compose's `MaxDistance`; it was the 85px midpoint).
    - In input mode, a complete hour (two digits, or one that can't take another, such as "7") moves focus to the minute field (Compose's `TimeInput`).
  - **24-hour ring layout:** 00–11 on the outer ring, 12–23 on the inner one. The spec's guidelines say "even numbers appear in an inner ring, and odd numbers appear in an outer ring", but that sentence contradicts the spec's own 24-hour image, its "Width (24h vertical)" figure, Compose and Android, so the ring layout is kept (decided 2026-10-13).
  - **Layouts** (spec: "In landscape mode, the stacked input and selection options are positioned side-by-side"; "Time pickers should avoid scrolling"; "can fallback to the input time picker"): `layout` is `vertical`, `horizontal` or `auto` (default). Horizontal puts the time (with a 216 × 38px AM / PM selector 16px below, divided by a vertical line) 36px beside the dial, as Compose's `HorizontalTimePicker`. `auto` picks from the window, sized for the picker in a `PickerDialog`: vertical needs 568px of height; otherwise horizontal needs 452px of height and 640px of width; otherwise it shows the input (`useTimePickerRoom`, media queries read with `useSyncExternalStore`, vertical on the server).
  - **Mode:** `mode` / `defaultMode` / `onModeChange`. `TimePickerModeToggle` is the keyboard / clock icon button (spec labels "Toggle input picker" / "Toggle dial picker", from `labels.inputMode` / `labels.dialMode`, the same `TimePickerLabels` object the picker takes; they replaced `toggleInputLabel` / `toggleDialLabel`, 2026-10-14) for `PickerDialog`'s `modeToggleButton`; it hides itself when there is no room for the dial.
  - **Input mode** (`TimeInputTokens`): 96 × 72px numeric fields for both hour cycles (the 114px width is only the 24-hour dial selector's) in `display-medium` with `body-small` "Hour" / "Minute" labels 7px below, `primary-container` with a 2px `primary` border while focused; ↑ / ↓ step and wrap.
  - The hour cycle defaults to the locale's (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` `hourCycle`). The hour shows two digits on both clocks ("02" at 2 o'clock on a 12-hour clock), in the selector and in input mode, as Compose's (`hourForDisplay.toLocalString(minDigits = 2)`; 2026-10-15); the dial's numbers stay unpadded. Every word, including the AM / PM group's name, is in `labels`.
- **Picker dialog:** `surface-container-high`, 28px corners, level 3, on the dialog's scrim and motion (2026-10-14: the dialog's slide and container growth, clipped with the picker's own container-shape token, and its staggered parts: the title and picker fade in after 50ms and the actions row, `data-slot="actions"`, after 150ms; the panel carries `data-exiting` so its parts fade out first). The date variant holds the picker edge to edge with actions 8px apart, 8px below and 6px from the end; the time variant pads 24px and shows a `label-medium` `on-surface-variant` title and the mode toggle at the start of the actions.
  - **`onConfirm`** saves the picked value. With it, Enter on a day or in a typed date closes and saves (a context the pickers read); it runs after the picker's new value has rendered (a timeout reading the latest handler), so a handler reading the draft sees the day just chosen.
  - **`fullScreen`** `never` (default) | `always` | `compact` (below 600px, read in JS so the picker can fill the height). Spec: "On compact breakpoints … a full-screen modal date picker is recommended", with "an additional close affordance (x) icon button and Save confirmation". It follows the full-screen `Dialog`: no corners or elevation, a 56px row with the `on-surface` close button (`classNames.close`) and Save (calling `onConfirm`; without `onConfirm`, the `confirmButton`) instead of the actions; the picker fills the width and the range list the height. The container stays the date picker's `surface-container-high` (spec colour roles; the full-screen `Dialog` uses it too). Until 2026-10-13 it was `surface` with a 64px row and an `on-surface-variant` close icon.
- **Component tokens** (2026-10-14, `date-picker-tokens.ts`, `time-picker-tokens.ts`): M3's modal date picker set, `--md-comp-date-picker-modal-{container-color, container-shape, container-elevation, header-headline-color, date-selected-container-color, date-selected-label-text-color, date-today-container-outline-color, date-today-label-text-color, range-selection-active-indicator-container-color}` (the range band's three gradients are derived from the last), and the time picker set, `--md-comp-time-picker-{container-color, container-shape, clock-dial-color, clock-dial-selector-handle-container-color, time-selector-selected-container-color, time-selector-unselected-container-color, period-selector-selected-container-color, period-selector-outline-color}` (the handle colour also paints the track and centre dot, as Compose's `selectorColor` does). `DatePicker` / `DateRangePicker` roots carry `data-tokens="md-comp-date-picker-modal"` and `TimePicker`'s `data-tokens="md-comp-time-picker"`; `PickerDialog`'s panel carries its variant's set and `data-variant` (`date` | `time`) and paints the container tokens. M3's docked date picker set and time input set aren't exposed: the docked calendar's days and the input fields keep their classes. Hover, focus, pressed and disabled values, the year list and the title stay classes.
- **Density** (2026-10-15, §5): `DateField`, `DatePicker` / `DateRangePicker` (their input mode's fields) and `TimePicker` (its input mode: hour and minute fields, ":" and the period selector, 72 → 60px) take `density`; the calendars and the dial keep their size.
- **Not in v1:** Compose's selectable-dates callbacks beyond `isDateUnavailable`, a docked range picker (the spec's docked picker is single-date), and time input validation messages.

## 10. Layout safety (consumer positioning never breaks a component)

**Principle:** a component's internal visuals never depend on the root element's `position`, `overflow`, `display` or `transform`. Consumers may put any layout class on any component — e.g. `<Button className="fixed bottom-4 right-4">` — and it must look and behave the same.

### Failure modes this prevents

- `static` on a root that relied on `relative` → absolutely-positioned state layer escapes and covers the parent/page.
- `overflow-visible` → ripple spills outside rounded corners.
- Motion animating the root's `transform` → fights consumer `translate`/`rotate`/`style.transform`, and turns the component into the containing block for any `fixed` descendant.
- `className` landing on an inner element of a multi-part component → `fixed` moves only part of it.

### Rules

1. **`className` and `style` always go on the outermost element.** Layout utilities apply to the whole component. `style` is merged, never replaced.
   - **Overlays** (2026-10-14; until then they went on the panel, so `absolute` or `overflow-hidden` could hide or clip it): a `Dialog`'s and `PickerDialog`'s `className` (and the dialog's `style`) go on a new full-window layer around the positioner, held over the window by an inline `position: fixed; inset: 0` that no class can move, so `static` or `absolute` never puts a modal dialog in the page's flow or out of view (`classNames.positioner` still reaches the positioner inside); a `Menu`'s and a tooltip's on the box React Aria places, whose inline position wins over a consumer class, and whose `data-overlay-placement` resets every inset React Aria leaves unset (an unlayered rule in `styles/utilities.css`, which beats Tailwind's layered utilities while React Aria's inline insets beat it; React Aria writes its insets to the element directly, so they can't be pinned through React's `style`), so a consumer inset (`bottom-4` while React Aria sets `top`) can't stretch it, with the surface inside `w-max`; a menu's box also keeps the menu's own width (`w-max! min-w-0!`, `menuOwnWidth`, 2026-10-17), since React Aria places it by its size and a full-width box sat the menu 12px off its trigger (only `Menu` and its submenus take it: on the shared `menuStyles` slot its `!important` minimum also beat the inline minimum that keeps a `Select`'s and `Autocomplete`'s option list as wide as its field, Compose's `exposedDropdownSize`, and their lists shrank to their content); a sheet's on its container, which places it while its motion runs on a library-owned layer inside (rule 4). Each surface is styled through `classNames.panel`; slots that name a built-in overlay (`tooltip`, `overflowMenu`, the FAB menu's `menu`) style its panel. Submenus open inside the root menu's box, so an `overflow-*` class or transform on a menu also reaches them.
2. **State layer and ripple are painted as background layers on the root**, not as absolutely-positioned children: state layer = `linear-gradient` overlay using state colour × state opacity token; ripple = `radial-gradient` animated via registered `@property` custom properties. They follow `border-radius` automatically and need neither `relative` nor `overflow-hidden`. Components add their own transitions (corners, colours, elevation) through the `--m3-transition-{property,duration,easing,delay}` lists, which the `container-motion` utility fills. Setting `transition-*` directly would cancel the state layer's transitions.
3. **Unavoidable inner overlays** (FAB morph shape, badges, touch targets) live inside a library-owned inner wrapper with its own `relative`/clipping. Consumer classes go on the outer element.
4. **Motion never animates the root's `transform`.** Springs, press-scale and morph run on inner layers. One exception (2026-10-13, for the spec's FAB appear / disappear and toolbar exit-on-scroll motion): a FAB or toolbar hidden with `visible={false}` (or a FAB appearing with `appear`) scales or slides through the root's `transform` property, set only while hidden or appearing, so at rest the root has no transform and traps no `fixed` children. It uses `transform` only, never the `translate` / `scale` / `rotate` properties that Tailwind's utilities set, so consumer positioning classes compose with it.
5. **Containers never trap `fixed` children.** Card, Dialog content, Sheet etc. never put `transform`, `filter`, `backdrop-filter`, `contain` or `will-change: transform` on their root.
6. **Focus ring = CSS `outline` (+ `outline-offset`)**, so `overflow-hidden`, `fixed` or clipping ancestors can't hide it.
7. **Layout-neutral roots**: no outer margins, no own `z-index`, no own positioning. Overlays portal to `body` with a z-index token scale (`--md-sys-z-*`) consumers can override.
8. **`cn()` conflict resolution** guarantees the consumer's layout class wins whenever the library does set a conflicting one.
9. **A component that resizes itself in place is never the scroll anchor** (2026-10-15): the browser's scroll anchoring picks a visible element near the top of the window and, when it moves, scrolls the page by the same amount. A `FabMenu` opening upward from its FAB (its root grows 56 → 300px toward the start of the page) was picked on phones and the page jumped 244px on every open and close. Roots that grow or shrink as part of their own motion carry `[overflow-anchor:none]`: `FabMenu` and `ExtendedFab` (its label). `FloatingToolbar` is the exception (2026-10-15): its root keeps anchoring and only its descendants carry `none` (`**:[overflow-anchor:none]`), because its start edge stays put while it collapses; excluding the root handed the anchor to the content after a vertical toolbar in normal flow, which moved by the whole height change and scrolled the page 96px on every collapse and expand. Components that only grow downward (a `List` item expanding) keep anchoring, which keeps the content below them still; a list's closing nested rows are the exception (`overflow-anchor: none`, 2026-10-17), so that when an item opens as one above it folds the page scrolls to keep the pressed row in place (the List section); fixed and portalled ones (search views, snackbars, sheets, menus) aren't candidates. The page itself is never changed. `scroll-anchoring.spec.ts` scrolls each one partly above a 330 × 620 touch window on a tall page and checks `scrollY` on every frame of opening and closing.

### Verification

An **override matrix** in CI: every component rendered with `fixed`, `absolute`, `sticky`, `static`, `overflow-hidden`, `overflow-visible`, `w-full`, a transform, inside a transformed ancestor, and in RTL — Playwright visual snapshots plus interaction checks. Each component gets a `LayoutOverride` story (`apps/docs`) and a Playwright test that checks layout size, the background state layer, corner radius, the absence of positioned children, pressing and the keyboard focus ring for every combination.

**Guards over every component story** (2026-10-15), run on the static Storybook with the other Playwright suites; one test per story, so they shard:

- **Scroll-jump guard** (`e2e/scroll-guard.spec.ts`): each story sits on a tall, scrolled page with its first pressable element a third above the window, at phone (390 × 844, touch) and desktop (1280 × 800) sizes. The element is pressed where it shows. **What it protects** (2026-10-17): nothing moves out from under the reader. On every painted frame (read in a `ResizeObserver` callback, after the page's own animation-frame corrections and layout, `e2e/painted-frames.ts`) the control the press lands on (the row inside a list, the button rather than its icon) stays where it was on screen; the page may scroll only to keep it there, by exactly as much as the content above it changed size, so a page that scrolls when nothing above changed still fails. A pressed control that is fixed or sticky doesn't follow the page, so there `window.scrollY` must not change at all, as before; once a pressed control leaves the page, the page must not scroll after. Stories whose press moves the pressed control by its own motion (a vertical slider's thumb jumping to the press, a sheet's handle rising as it expands, the toolbar FAB shrinking into its close button, FAB menus laid out in the page's flow, whose lists fold above their FABs) are listed in `MOVES_ITSELF` with a reason and keep the `scrollY` check only. Until then the guard checked only `window.scrollY`, which a press that let the content above the pressed element fold under it passed; on desktop, a press that lands in a composite widget is followed by an arrow key with the widget in view, and the page may move only as far as revealing a newly focused off-screen element needs. It found and fixed: a single-selection `ButtonGroup` or `ChipSet` moving a pointer press's focus to the checked radio (focus now redirects only for keyboard focus arriving from outside, `notePointerDown` in `utils/radio-group-keys.ts`); a removed chip's neighbour, a `TextField` or `Autocomplete` container press, the clear button and a touch on the autocomplete arrow focusing without `preventScroll`; React Aria centring a `List` taller than the window on an arrow key (the tabs' nearest-position reveal is now `utils/nearest-reveal.ts`, used by tabs and both list roots); and a one-radio `RadioGroup` letting arrow keys scroll the page.
- **Forced-colours guard** (`e2e/forced-colors-guard.spec.ts`): under `forcedColors: 'active'`, every component root (`[data-tokens]`) keeps a visible boundary (a non-`Canvas` border, outline or background on the root, a part or pseudo-element inside it, a progress indicator's strokes, or an enclosing component's root), every selected state differs from an unselected peer, and no text is the colour of what is painted behind it (2026-10-17: the `Canvas` backplate for text left to forced colours, else the nearest fill under it). Components M3 draws without a container (standard icon buttons, list rows, tabs, navigation items, carousels, top app bars at rest) are listed in `FORCED_COLORS_EXCEPTIONS` (`e2e/forced-colors.ts`) with a reason. It found and fixed: FABs, the FAB menu, the floating toolbar and the toolbar FAB (1px `CanvasText` border), the docked toolbar (top line), the filled field's line (`CanvasText`), and the date and time pickers' picked day, year, hour and period (`Highlight` fill; range days a `Highlight` ring; time fields a `CanvasText` boundary). Forced colours don't reach an SVG's fills, so the time picker's dial paints itself in system colours there (2026-10-17): a `Canvas` disc with a `CanvasText` edge and numbers, a `Highlight` handle and track with `HighlightText` numbers.
- **Coverage** (`e2e/catalog-coverage.spec.ts`): every core component in the docs site's catalog has a story the guards run, and every story title maps to a catalog component (`e2e/catalog-stories.ts`), so a new component can't skip them.

## 11. Next.js support

- Next.js 15/16, App Router primary, Pages Router supported; Turbopack + webpack.
- `"use client"` preserved at the top of every interactive component file in the build; utilities (`cn`, tokens, `createTheme`) are server-safe.
- **Every export can be imported into a Server Component** (checked 2026-10-13). The build keeps one output file per source module (`unbundle`), so the directive stays per module, and `scripts/check-dist.ts` fails the build if one is lost. In a Server Component:
  - **Server-callable** (from modules without the directive): every function, class and constant of the three entries: `cn`, `tv`, `twMergeConfig`, `createTheme`, the theme state helpers (`parseThemeState`, `serializeThemeState`, `themeAttributes`, `getThemeScriptSource`, `getThemeFromCookies`), the tokens and component-token helpers, `getM3Spring` and the spring helpers, every `*Styles` recipe, `matchesSearch` / `normalizeSearch`, `SnackbarHostState`, and from `/primitives` the shape library, `generateSchemeColors`, `getMorph`, `morphPathAt`, `localeWithDirection`, `splitDataAttributes` and `splitRootProps`. `src/server-safe-exports.test.ts` fails when an entry exports a function, class or constant (a camel-case name other than a `use*` hook, an upper-case constant, or one of the listed classes) from a `"use client"` module, where a Server Component would get an uncallable client reference. On 2026-10-13 that moved `getM3Spring`, `buttonGroupStyles`, `getMorph`, `morphPathAt` and `localeWithDirection` out of client modules.
  - **Components** render from a Server Component with serialisable props: text, numbers, plain objects and elements (icons, nested components). `Divider`, `LabeledDivider`, `ThemeScript` and the `/primitives` field parts, `Surface` and `TouchTarget` are server components themselves; the rest are client components.
  - **Contexts** (`ButtonContext`, `ToggleGroupStateContext`, `ThemeScopeContext`, `TriggerContext`) render as providers from a Server Component, with a serialisable `value`.
  - **Hooks** (`use*`) run only in client components.
  - **Client-only by their props:** functions (event handlers, render-function children, label functions) and class instances (a `SnackbarHostState`, `@internationalized/date` values) can't cross from a Server Component. So `SnackbarHost`, `TimePickerModeToggle`, `OptionPopover` and `/primitives`' `DomDirectionLocale` and `SelectionControl` are always rendered from a client component, as is any component given a handler.
  - **Sections of a static `List`:** a `ListSection` created in a Server Component reaches the client with a lazy type wrapping its client reference. The list never compares types: sections and items render themselves and find their place through context (§ List, "No type checks", 2026-10-13), so a static list takes sections and items from the server.
  - **Checked by** `apps/next-playground`'s `/server-components` page, which imports every value export of the three entries into a Server Component, renders each component, calls each function and class on the server, and lists any export without a check (its check tables are typed against the entries, so a new export also fails the typecheck). `e2e/server-components.spec.ts` runs it under `next start` and `next dev` (CI's playground job): no server errors, no client references among the functions, and hydration with no console error or warning.
- **Collection items must be created in client components.** `Menu`, `Select`, `Autocomplete`, `Tabs` and interactive `List` are React Aria collections, which read each item's element type (`MenuItem` / `MenuGroup` / `Tab` / `ListItem` are React Stately's `Item` / `Section`). Items created in a Server Component reach the client as client references, so React Stately fails with "Unknown element <[object Object]> in collection" (found in the docs site, where a server-rendered example passed a `Menu` to `SplitButton`). In development the components check their children (`utils/assert-collection-children.ts`) and throw an error naming the cause. The site's `menus.spec.ts` opens every menu on every component page, against the static export and again under `next dev`, where the failure shows first.
- No `window`/`document` access at module load; overlays portal only on the client.
- **No theme flash**:
  - Cookie (recommended): `getThemeFromCookies()` from `@windmorph/react/next` → render `<html data-theme data-mode data-contrast data-motion>` on the server.
  - Script: `<ThemeScript />` in `<head>` for static/exported sites (`suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>`).
- `<NextRouterProvider>` — one-line client routing integration.
- Roboto Flex via `next/font`, wired to `--md-ref-typeface-*`.
- No `transpilePackages` needed (ships compiled ESM).

## 12. Package & install

### Exports

- `@windmorph/react` — components, ThemeProvider, hooks, `cn`, tokens and the component-token helpers
- `@windmorph/react/primitives` — building blocks
- `@windmorph/react/next` — Next.js helpers
- `@windmorph/react/styles.css` — tokens + themes for Tailwind projects
- `@windmorph/react/styles.compiled.css` — precompiled for non-Tailwind projects
- The `windmorph` bin (the theme CLI, §5)
- ESM + `.d.ts`, `sideEffects: ["*.css"]`, modules preserved for tree-shaking.

### Install (Tailwind project / Next.js)

```bash
npm i @windmorph/react motion
```

```css
/* globals.css */
@import 'tailwindcss';
@import '@windmorph/react/styles.css';
@source "../node_modules/@windmorph/react";
```

```tsx
// app/layout.tsx
<ThemeProvider defaultTheme="baseline">{children}</ThemeProvider>
```

Plus font setup (Roboto Flex via `next/font` or Google Fonts) — documented, not bundled.

### Releasing (2026-10-13)

Only `@windmorph/react` is published to npm. `@windmorph/test-utils`, Storybook, the playground and the site are private workspace packages, and Windmorph Pro is published from its own repository. Versions and changelogs come from Changesets (`.changeset/`): every user-visible change adds a changeset (`pnpm changeset`).
