- **Package metadata:** `repository` (with `directory`), `homepage`, `bugs`, `keywords` and `publishConfig` (`access: public`, `provenance: true`). The tarball holds `dist` (ESM, `.d.ts`, the CSS entry points, the CLI with its shebang, and source maps that embed their sources, since `src` isn't published), `NOTICE`, `README.md` and the root `LICENSE`, which pnpm adds. There is no `engines` field: Node 24 is the repo's toolchain, and consumers' requirements come from the peer dependencies. To check a release candidate, run `pnpm --filter @windmorph/react pack`, then `npx publint` and `npx @arethetypeswrong/cli --profile esm-only` (leaving out the CSS entry points) on the tarball.
- **Workflow** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, on every push to `main`): Changesets' `select-mode` picks one of three modes.
  - _version_: pending changesets exist, so it opens or updates the "chore(release): version packages" pull request (bumped version, `CHANGELOG.md`, changesets removed).
  - _publish_: no pending changesets, and the package's version isn't on npm yet. The `pack` job runs CI's checks (format, traces, `turbo run build typecheck lint test`, with Windmorph Pro installed through `PRO_READ_TOKEN`) and packs the tarball with `pnpm pack`. The `publish` job publishes it (Changesets' `publish` action, with npm provenance from `publishConfig`), then pushes a `@windmorph/react@<version>` tag and a GitHub release. Only `publish` has `id-token: write`, and it runs in the `npm` GitHub environment, where required reviewers can gate releases.
  - _none_: nothing to do.
- **A release:** merge pull requests that carry changesets → merge the release pull request the workflow opens → that merge publishes. The release pull request is opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so CI doesn't start on it by itself: close and reopen it (or push to its branch) to run the checks before merging.
- **Authentication:** npm trusted publishing (OIDC) once it's configured for `@windmorph/react` on npmjs.com (repository `XManhTrinh/windmorph`, workflow `release.yml`, environment `npm`); npm then adds provenance automatically. Until then, the **`NPM_TOKEN`** secret: a granular access token with read and write access to the `@windmorph` scope (allowed to bypass two-factor authentication if the account requires it for publishing), written to the runner's `~/.npmrc`, since pnpm doesn't read tokens from environment variables in a project `.npmrc`. Once trusted publishing works, delete `NPM_TOKEN` and set the package to require two-factor authentication and disallow tokens.
- **Repository settings:** Actions → General → "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests" must be on; `NPM_TOKEN` is a secret of the `npm` environment (or of the repository); `PRO_READ_TOKEN` as for CI.
- **0.1.0, the first publish** (revised 2026-10-15): the accumulated changesets are collapsed into one, `.changeset/initial-release.md` (`minor`, "Initial release of Windmorph"), and the package's version is `0.0.0`, so Changesets bumps it to 0.1.0 (`pnpm changeset status --verbose` shows the plan). When the release commit reaches `main`, the workflow opens the version pull request (0.1.0, the first `CHANGELOG.md` entry); merging it publishes 0.1.0. Before that merge, the `@windmorph` scope must belong to the publishing npm account or organisation, and `NPM_TOKEN` (or a trusted publisher, which must publish within two days of being created) must be in place. Later releases go through changesets and the release pull request the same way.
- **Manual fallback:** the root `pnpm release` script (build, then `changeset publish`) publishes from a local checkout; such a publish carries no provenance.

## 13. Repo layout (pnpm workspaces + Turborepo)

```
docs/architecture.md   # this file
docs/plans/            # one plan per feature, approved before it's built
pnpm-workspace.yaml
turbo.json
packages/react         # @windmorph/react (MIT)
packages/test-utils    # @windmorph/test-utils: axe checks, Tailwind class checks and the Vitest setup, shared with Windmorph Pro
apps/docs              # Storybook 10 (theme/mode/contrast/motion/direction toolbars) + Playwright visual tests
apps/next-playground   # Next.js App Router + Pages Router test app, Playwright e2e (pnpm test:e2e)
apps/site              # Consumer docs site: Next.js App Router, static export (output: 'export'), MDX guides + generated per-component props tables + live examples, built only with @windmorph/react and Windmorph Pro
```

Windmorph Pro (`@windmorph/react-pro`) lives in its own private repository.

## 14. Quality

- Vitest + Testing Library (unit/behaviour). The helpers live in `packages/test-utils` (`@windmorph/test-utils`), so Windmorph Pro uses the same ones; `packages/react/test` keeps thin wrappers that point the class checks at this package's stylesheet.
- axe accessibility checks per component
- Playwright visual regression: every component × 6 themes × light/dark (+ contrast levels on key components) × both motion schemes where relevant
  - Screenshots are taken against the static Storybook build (`pnpm test:e2e`), with the font loaded locally (`@fontsource-variable/roboto-flex`) so results don't depend on the network.
  - Baselines are platform-specific: the snapshot path template ends in `-{platform}`, so `*-chromium-darwin.png` serve local runs on a Mac and `*-chromium-linux.png` serve CI. Both sets are committed and kept in step.
  - **Linux screenshot baselines** (2026-10-14): `pnpm test:e2e:linux` (`scripts/storybook-e2e-linux.mjs`) runs the Storybook suite in the official Playwright image for the installed `@playwright/test` version (`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v<version>-noble`), always as `linux/arm64`, against the static build (`pnpm turbo run build --filter=docs` first). It needs Docker (or OrbStack). Arguments go to `playwright test`. To update the Linux baselines after a deliberate visual change: build Storybook, run `pnpm test:e2e:linux --update-snapshots=changed` (or `=all`, or name a spec), review the new `*-linux.png` images, and update the darwin ones with `pnpm --filter docs test:e2e --update-snapshots=changed` on a Mac. A Playwright upgrade changes the image, so re-run it after one. The image runs on arm64 because CI's runners are arm64 (`ubuntu-24.04-arm`, free for public repositories) and that's native on Apple silicon; an x86 Chromium rasterises a few pixels differently.
  - **Platform differences the suite allows for:** Chromium on Linux lays the positioner dialog's text out 15px narrower (`TEXT_WIDTH` in `dialog.spec.ts`), and paints the browser's own focus ring in other colours, which the computed-style snapshot records as the macOS values it was taken with (`FOCUS_RING` in `computed-styles.spec.ts`).
  - Every class a `tv` recipe can emit is checked to appear literally in its source and to compile in Tailwind. A class assembled at runtime would never be generated.
  - **Docs site e2e projects:** `chromium` runs every suite against the static export; `next-dev` runs the menu suite under `next dev` with its own build directory (`NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-e2e`), so it can run while a developer's `next dev` holds the `.next` lock.
  - **Turbo env:** `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` is a global pass-through variable, so builds behind a TLS-intercepting proxy can still download fonts for `next/font` (strict env mode dropped it).
  - **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): checks formatting and traces (`pnpm check:traces`: no former name or prefix, Vietnamese sample text, personal or UI-library names, and since 2026-10-16 no other product's name, so docs describe a pattern instead of naming an app that uses it; lockfiles and generated files are skipped, and the social-card metadata keys are allowed), builds, typechecks, lints and tests everything and checks the bundle-size budgets; separate jobs run the site's link check and smoke suite and the playground's Playwright suite. The site has two (2026-10-15): `site-build` ("Site build, bento video & link check") builds the export, records the bento video, builds again and runs the link check, then hands `out/` and the media to `site-e2e` ("Site Playwright smoke suite (shard N/2)"), which runs the suite in two shards with `--fully-parallel`, so the one large smoke file splits by test rather than by file. The pnpm version comes from `packageManager`; also passing `version` to `pnpm/action-setup` fails the job. The `storybook-e2e` job runs the Storybook suite through `pnpm test:e2e:linux` in four parallel shards on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, against the Linux baselines, and uploads the failed tests' results. Every action is pinned to a full commit SHA with its version in a comment, in both workflows (2026-10-14).
  - **Wait for animations before measuring** an element for a screenshot clip: an entering overlay is still scaling, and a baseline taken mid-animation is cut off (found in the rich tooltip test).
  - **Screenshot tolerance:** `maxDiffPixelRatio` is 0.0002 (0.02%). Playwright's per-pixel colour `threshold` stays at its default 0.2, which ignores colour shifts as small as `surface` → `surface-container` (found with the list container change): after colour changes, re-baseline with `--update-snapshots=all` and review the images. At 0.2%, a moved hairline (FAB collapse width, TextField outline offset) still matched an outdated baseline. Re-baseline deliberately after visual changes and review the images.
  - **Storybook server:** the static build is served with `sirv --dev`, which reads files per request, so a server reused across rebuilds never returns 404s for new asset hashes.
- Layout-safety override matrix (§10)
- Next playground build + Playwright in CI
- **Docs site page weight** (2026-10-13): every component page is one `[slug]` route, so anything its server tree imports is in every component page's bundle. Live examples are therefore loaded on demand (`lazyExample` in `lib/lazy-example.tsx` → `components/lazy-example.tsx`, one chunk per example file, each in its own Suspense boundary, marked `data-ready` once interactive, which the e2e suites wait for). The root layout imports no page content: the search index is a static `/search-index.json` (a route handler, about 8 KB gzipped) fetched once the page is idle, or when the search opens first. `optimizePackageImports` covers both Windmorph packages, so a server import of one token set doesn't pull in its entry's client components. Links in long lists (gallery cards, the rail and its flyouts) use `IntentLink`, which prefetches on hover, focus or touch instead of on entering the viewport. Result (desktop, uncompressed, measured on the static export): the home page went from 4.3 MB at load (5.6 MB after scrolling, which prefetched every component page) to 2.3 MB (2.4 MB), 1.17 MB → 0.70 MB gzipped; a component page's JavaScript from 2.3 MB to 1.3 MB.
- **Home page bento video** (2026-10-15): below the large window class (1200px: phones and tablets) the home page shows a video of the desktop bento in place of the live components (`components/home/bento-video.tsx`); large windows and up keep the live bento. The live bento is never the page's scroll anchor (`overflow-anchor: none` on its root): the FAB menu's positioned wrapper grows upward as it opens, and Chrome anchoring to it scrolled the page 244px with each open and close when the bento's top was just above the window. The video is generated from the built site, so it always matches the bento: `pnpm --filter site record:bento` (`scripts/record-bento.ts`) serves `out/`, opens the home page in Chromium at 1440 × 860 and 1×, in light and in dark mode, captures it through the DevTools screencast, and keeps exactly one loop of the bento's theme cycle (`BENTO_LOOP_MS` in `components/home/bento-loop.ts`, 18 seconds: each of the six themes resting and active), measured on the page between two returns to the first theme, so the end meets the start. Each frame is cropped to the bento card measured on the page (the grid holding the "Reply" button group, 1152 × 720), resampled to 30fps and encoded into `public/media/bento/`: `bento-{light,dark}.mp4` (H.264, `yuv420p`, `+faststart`, CRF 23, for Safari on iPhone) when a system ffmpeg has libx264, `bento-{light,dark}.webm` (VP9 from a system ffmpeg, else VP8 from Playwright's bundled ffmpeg), the posters `bento-{light,dark}.jpg` (the first frame) and `bento.json` (size, loop length, formats). The media aren't committed (gitignored): the site reads `bento.json` at build time (`lib/bento-video.ts`, only when every file it lists exists) and is an input of `site#build`, so a build after recording picks the video up and a build without one gives every window the live bento. **Pipeline:** build → `record:bento` → build again → test or deploy. CI's `site-build` job does this on every pull request (installing ffmpeg with apt, uploading the media as the `bento-video` artifact), and the deploy workflow, once hosting is chosen, will run the same steps before deploying `out/`. **On the page:** both are in the server's HTML, CSS showing the one for the window, and once hydrated only that one stays, so a phone or tablet never runs the live bento's loop and a large window has no video. The video is muted, looping, `playsinline` and `preload="none"`: its sources (MP4, then WebM) are added only when it first scrolls into view, it plays in view and pauses out of it, follows the resolved colour mode (swapping videos at the same moment of the loop), and has 28px corners like the card. A 56px round icon button over the card's end corner pauses and plays it, as on M3's own site (WCAG 2.2.2, "Pause animation" / "Play animation"): its centre 12px in from the bottom and end edges, overhanging by 16px inside the page gutter, mirrored in right to left; while playing a translucent inverse surface (80%) with an inverse-on-surface icon, paused a filled primary one, both through the icon button's component tokens; with reduced motion it waits on its poster for play. The video is `aria-hidden`, with a visually hidden sentence saying what it shows. Tested by `bento-video.test.tsx`, `lib/bento-video.test.ts`, `scripts/bento-recording.test.ts` and `e2e/bento-video.spec.ts` (at 320, 390, 430, 768 and 1180px: video behaviour, the button's corner, colours and RTL position, no sideways scrolling, and axe in light and dark; 1200 and 1440px live bento, which never moves the page's scroll position; the recording's size equals the grid box).
- **Docs site metadata and link previews** (2026-10-13): the site's origin is one constant, `SITE_URL` in `apps/site/lib/site-metadata.ts` (`https://windmorph.vercel.app`). Every page sets its title, description, canonical URL and Open Graph / X tags through `pageMetadata`. `lib/site-pages.ts` lists every page (home, guides, component pages and their tabs) from the same registry, catalog and guide entries as the pages; the sitemap (`app/sitemap.ts`) and the link-preview images (`app/og/[...path]/route.tsx`, `next/og` at build time, written to `out/og/<path>.png`, in the site theme's light colours read from its generated CSS) come from it. `robots.txt` allows everything and points at the sitemap. No analytics.
- **AI-readable docs** (2026-10-13): after `next build`, `scripts/generate-llms.ts` writes a Markdown twin of every page (`<path>.md`, `/index.md` for the home page; a component page's twin holds all its tabs, each tab page also has its own), `/llms.txt` (the llms.txt format, linking the twins) and `/llms-full.txt`. Pages come from `out/sitemap.xml`; text from each page's rendered HTML (`lib/html-to-markdown.ts` drops controls, previews and anything marked `data-markdown="skip"`, such as the playground and the guides' live demos); props tables from the generated props JSON (a props list elsewhere, such as a hook's parameters, marks its root with `data-props-list` and becomes a table); example code from `examples/` (an `ExampleViewer` marks its root with `data-example-file`). `e2e/static-files.spec.ts` checks that every page has its twin, link-preview image and sitemap entry.
- Docs site (`apps/site`, §1 #23): build + static export, an internal link checker over `out/`, and a Playwright smoke suite over every page of the export (enumerated from its HTML: home, guides, every component page and every tab) asserting render, no console errors and an axe scan in light and dark mode, plus RTL (every page but the tabs, which share a template; the Button tabs stand in for them) — all run in CI (2026-10-13: the tabs got their dark scan, done on the loaded page by switching `data-mode`)
- **Site typecheck after the site build** (2026-10-13): the site's `typecheck` runs `next typegen`, which writes `.next/types`, while `next build` rewrites `.next`; run in parallel, `tsc` read half-written route types. `apps/site/turbo.json` makes `site#typecheck` depend on `site#build`, so they never overlap (a cached build makes this free).
- **Bundle-size budgets** (2026-10-13): `packages/react/scripts/bundle-size.ts` measures each public component module in the built `dist` (every module the main entry imports a component from), gzipped together with the library modules it imports statically; dependencies aren't counted, nor are modules loaded with a dynamic `import()` (2026-10-14: they load only when used, as `Menu`'s sheet does). `build` writes the report to `dist/bundle-size.json` (left out of the npm package), which the docs site shows on each component's API tab. `pnpm turbo run size` (a CI step) compares it with `packages/react/bundle-size.json` and fails when a module is more than 2% over its budget, has no budget, or a budget names a module that's gone; `pnpm --filter @windmorph/react size:update` rewrites the budgets after a deliberate change.
- Changesets for versioning/changelog; releases run in `.github/workflows/release.yml` (§12, "Releasing")

## 15. Component roadmap (M3 Expressive set)

- **Tier 1**: Button, IconButton, **Button group** (standard + connected), FAB + Extended FAB, Card, TextField, Checkbox, Radio, Switch, Dialog, Menu (Expressive: standard + vibrant colours, grouped items with gaps, selected state), **Select** and **Autocomplete** (exposed dropdown menus; built)
- **Tier 2**: Split button (built), **FAB menu** (built), Chips (built), Tabs (built), **Navigation rail** (collapsed / expanded / modal expanded, built), **Flexible navigation bar** (built), Snackbar (built), Tooltip (built), **Loading indicator** (shape morph, built), **Pull to refresh** (built, 2026-10-13), Progress indicators (linear + circular, wavy, with stop indicator; built)
- **Tier 3**: **Toolbars** (floating + docked; built), Flexible app bars (built) + search app bar (built), Slider (XS–XL, vertical, centred, range, inset icons; built), Expressive lists (segmented/grouped; built), Carousel (incl. vertical; built), Bottom/side sheets (built), Date & time pickers (built), Badges (built), Dividers (built), Search (built)

Build order: Foundations (token source, 6 themes × modes × contrast, motion schemes, ThemeProvider, `cn`, primitives) → Tier 1 → Tier 2 → Tier 3.

### Not built (deprecated in M3 Expressive)

| Deprecated              | Replaced by                                 |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| Navigation drawer       | Navigation rail (expanded / modal expanded) |
| Original navigation bar | Flexible navigation bar                     |
| Small FAB               | FAB sizes default / medium / large          |
| Segmented button        | Connected button group                      |
| Bottom app bar          | Docked toolbar                              |

Indeterminate circular progress was listed here until 2026-10-13. The M3 Expressive spec lists it, flat and wavy, so it's built; only the old separate circular token set is deprecated.

- `@windmorph/react` — **MIT**
- Bundled `@material/material-color-utilities` — Apache-2.0; attribution in `NOTICE` (decision #21)
- Token values derived from Jetpack Compose Material 3 — Apache-2.0; attribution in `NOTICE`
- Roboto Flex — SIL OFL 1.1 (consumer-loaded)
- Material Symbols — Apache-2.0 (recommended for consumers' icons, not bundled as a font; the few icons drawn inside components are its path data, attributed in `NOTICE`)
- Vendored androidx.graphics.shapes + Compose `MaterialShapes` port — Apache-2.0; attribution and the list of modifications are in `NOTICE` and `src/shapes/LICENSE-androidx.md`
- Material Design guidelines/tokens — Google, referenced per decision #3

## 17. Open checks before / during Foundations

- ✅ **`ColorSpec2025` confirmed** (2026-10-01): `@material/material-color-utilities@0.4.0` exposes `SpecVersion = '2021' | '2025'` via the scheme constructors (`new SchemeTonalSpot(hct, isDark, contrast, '2025')`). 2025 applies to Tonal spot, Neutral, Vibrant and Expressive (other variants fall back to 2021), which covers all six built-in themes. **Caveat:** 0.4.0's published ESM has extensionless relative imports (`'../dynamiccolor/dynamic_scheme'`), so plain Node ESM fails to load it. The build-time theme generator and the CLI must run through a bundler (now tsdown, which bundles the library into `dist/vendor/`, decision #21). Browser consumers are unaffected because their bundlers resolve these imports.
- ✅ **Compose tokens pulled** (2026-10-01, androidx `120345129e`, `compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/.../tokens/`). Shape scale (§4) and state opacities match. Springs (Compose `dampingRatio` / `stiffness`, mass 1; Motion `damping` = 2·ratio·√stiffness):

  | Scheme     | Spatial fast | Spatial default | Spatial slow | Effects fast / default / slow |
  | ---------- | ------------ | --------------- | ------------ | ----------------------------- |
  | expressive | 0.6 / 800    | 0.8 / 380       | 0.8 / 200    | 1.0 / 3800 · 1600 · 800       |
  | standard   | 0.9 / 1400   | 0.9 / 700       | 0.9 / 300    | 1.0 / 3800 · 1600 · 800       |

  Overshoot: expressive spatial 9.5% fast, 1.5% default/slow; standard spatial 0.15%; effects 0%. This matches §6.

- ✅ Spec gaps resolved: medium FABs use `LargeIncreased` (20px) in Compose's code (§9). `LargeIconButtonTokens` `Uniform` is the default width (§9).

### Remaining Foundations work

- ✅ **Shape library port + `useM3Morph`** (2026-10-02): see §3. Stories under Foundations/Shapes; Playwright covers the gallery, morph frames, extrapolation, settling and the reduced-motion snap.
- **Vite 8 directive warnings:** Vite 8 (rolldown) logs `MODULE_LEVEL_DIRECTIVE` for every `"use client"` file when a client-only app bundles the library. The warnings are harmless. The `build.rolldownOptions.onLog` filter is in `packages/react/README.md` and `apps/docs/.storybook/main.ts`.
- **Stylesheet size:** the six themes × three contrast levels × light/dark/system are about 140 KB unminified (`generated/themes.css`), most of the shipped CSS. If that matters to consumers, a later option is to split medium/high contrast into opt-in files.

## 18. Related

- Closest existing project: `m3-expressive-react` (CSS Modules, early stage). No mature Tailwind-based M3 Expressive library exists.
